# Changelog (open-tx-isp)

Condensed from the open-stack campaign changelog; only open-tx-isp (kernel driver) changes.
Newest first, grouped by date. Everything listed was device-tested on the SoC named unless
marked otherwise. Release tags `vYYYY.MM.DD` on the `aperto` branch are planned (the first one after the long soak of the release candidate); until then dates are the reference. Branch names are historic: the topic branches were merged into `next` and deleted.

## 2026-11-04: T30 analog gain reaches the sensor again, anti-flicker off by default as the vendor (`claude/t30-ae-gain`; host test; T30X device proof pending)

- T30X b22 (SC4236): the open AE reported analog gain code `0x71e` (15x) while the sensor gain registers `0x3e08/0x3e09` stayed `03/10` (1x) in every case; ISP digital gain ran at its maximum to compensate (vendor: `1f/1e`, dgain `0x215`).
- Cause: the compact AE state survived the ISP core init (`apical_init` -> `apical_fw_init`, which clears the OEM CMOS/AE/AWB state on every ISP open). The sensor init table puts the sensor back to 1x, but the AE kept the converged code, which equals the attribute shadow, so `sensor_set_analog_gain()` posted no message and the gain never reached the sensor. `apical_fw_init` now clears the compact AE/AWB state together with the firmware state.
- Integration time stopped at 1440 lines (vendor 1915): the compact AE used the anti-flicker frequency (system item 52, 50 Hz after `init_stab`) without the enable item 13, so it quantised to 3 x 480 lines. The OEM CMOS FSM (`cmos_fsm_process_interrupt`, `cmos_inttime_update`) uses item 52 only while item 13 is set, and the anti-flicker control sets item 13 (`apical_isp_flicker_s_control`). Both now as the vendor.
- The automatic analog gain request is capped at system item 29 (max sensor analog gain) before the sensor quantises it, the rest goes to the ISP digital gain (OEM `cmos_alloc_sensor_analog_gain`); the automatic integration time is capped at item 26 (`cmos_alloc_integration_time`). Before, item 29 was ignored (b22 "limits=1/1" still gave 15x).
- Host test `t30_ae_gain_host_test` (OEM limits + a model of the gain message path with an SC4236 mock) and source guard `check_t30_ae_gain.sh`. QEMU T30 cannot prove the I2C write (the sensor model gives no histogram, so the AE does not run); a T30X device run must show `0x3e08/0x3e09` following the AE and about 1915 lines at base.

## 2026-11-04: T30 day/night switch reloads the night parameter set as the vendor (QEMU T30; T30X device test pending)

- `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetISPRunningMode(NIGHT)` / `DAY` (DAY_OR_NIGHT, `0x80000e1`) only set a flag: the open interrupt handler never raised tuning event `0x4000003`, which the OEM frame-end case sends to reload the day or night set (T30X b19-b21: night not monochrome, vendor U = V = 128.00).
- The frame-end interrupt now raises it like the OEM. The reload itself is rewritten in C from the OEM `isp_core_tuning_event` (`0x4628`-`0x5f58`): the lift had 62 of the 93 calibration tables (one at a wrong offset), wrote through a NULL stack slot (QEMU oops "Fatal exception in interrupt" once the event was raised) and lost everything after the tables. Now: UV clip 512 at night / full range by day, all 93 tables of the new set, the 44 customer register fields, system items 37/41, the Iridix / AE limit items from the tables, the module enables in `0x40` (and `0x4e0` / `0x500` / `0x620` / `0x720` / `0x44`), then the CSC preset again when one is set.
- QEMU T30: day -> night -> day: UV clip `0x200` at night, `0x000`/`0x3ff` by day; no oops, no sleeping call in the interrupt. Host tests green.

## 2026-11-04: T30 CSC presets (beyond vendor, opt-in; QEMU T30, T30X device test pending)

- `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetCsc_Attr` / `GetCsc_Attr` (tuning control `0x80000a6`) now work on T30 as on T10/T20: presets 0-3 (BT.601 / BT.709, full / TV range) or a user matrix with offsets and Y/UV clip. The vendor T30 driver rejects the control (-1) and libimp 1.0.5 has no such call. The preset goes into calibration table RGB2YUV_CONVERSION (API id 139 = firmware table 276) through the vendor calibration API, the YUV matrix is recomputed by re-setting the current saturation, and the cs_conv clip registers of the FR/DS1/DS2 channels (`0x578`/`0x6b8`/`0x7b8` + 0..12) get the preset range. The UV clip stays as the day/night switch sets it at night; after the day/night table reload the preset is applied again.
- Default unchanged: until a preset is set the getter returns -1 and nothing differs from the vendor (the stock tuning-bin table stays in place).
- QEMU T30: presets 0-3 and a user matrix change the colour matrix registers and the clip ranges (TV range: Y 64..940, UV 64..960), the getter reports the preset; 0 oopses. Host tests green.

## 2026-11-04: T30 horizontal mirror, manual white balance and scene mode as the vendor (QEMU T30; T30X lab camera b20: mirror and scene; manual WB recovery: b21 pending)

- Horizontal mirror: the OEM ISP interrupt applies a pending HFLIP control in its frame-end case (`0x40` bit 1 = not mirrored, bayer phase in `0x18` moved by one column; one row for a vertical flip when the input format changed). The open interrupt handler had no such step, so `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetISPHflip` stored the state but never changed the picture (T30X b19: mirrored picture = base). QEMU: `0x40` `0x33242193` -> `0x33242191`, bayer 3 -> 2 and back on every toggle.
- Manual white balance: OEM `awb_normalise()` takes the red/blue feedback factors from the system items 49/50 while AWB is manual (system item 12); the open AWB update kept computing them from the statistics and overwrote the manual gains every frame (T30X b19: no picture change). `system_manual_awb()` restored from the OEM (SET stores item 12; the lift dropped the SET and stored on invalid directions).
- Scene mode: the stock handler returns without storing anything, so `GetSceneMode` reads 0 after a set on the vendor stack (T30X b17-b19); the open driver kept the value and now leaves it alone (QEMU: set 8 -> get 0).
- Manual white balance, follow-up: while AWB is manual the gains are written from the system items alone and the automatic filter state is kept, so AWB resumes from its last automatic estimate. Writing the manual factors into the filter (first version) left AWB stuck after a manual red factor of 4: every zone was rejected and the automatic estimate never moved again (T30X b20).
- Device (T30X lab camera `.231`, b20, open stack and vendor stack picture runs in the same boot): H, V and HV mirror PASS on both stacks; GetSceneMode 0 after a set on both; manual white balance now changes the picture (red factor 4: V 115 -> 25).
- Host tests green.

## 2026-11-04: T31 defog tables come from the active tuning bin, like the vendor (`claude/t31-defog-from-bin`; host + Unicorn oracle + QEMU T31 + T31X light-box camera)

- `tiziano_defog_params_refresh` wrote the built-in table values (the vendor module's ROM bank) whatever the sensor bin said. The vendor copies 29 defog tables (ev/transmission lists, main/colour/dark/block parameters, WDR variants, fpga parameters) from the active day/night bank (`tparams`, bank offsets 0x10b6c..0x1102c); with `rgbra_list` = 1 it also takes the weight LUTs and centre weights from the bank. The driver now does the same (`tiziano_defog_load_bank`); the built-in values stay as fallback when no bin is loaded, the bank has no EV axis (all-zero defog section) or `rgbra_list` is neither 0 nor 1. The loader only accepts bins with two full 0x137f0 byte banks, so the offsets are in bounds. `force_bypass_defog` and the defog enable bit are unchanged.
- Effect: bins whose defog section differs from the built-in values (sc4336p: EV axis 100..16000, transmission 80..95, other block/dark/main parameters) now dehaze like the vendor instead of stronger.
- Evidence: Unicorn oracle (`tests/t31_defog_bank_host.sh`, `tools/ko_emu/diff_t31_defog_bank.py`): the vendor refresh run on the day and night bank of 104 T31 bins equals the driver's table byte for byte (also `rgbra_list` = 1 bins); QEMU T31 debug kernel (`slub_debug=FZPU`): 5 reload cycles with the sc4336p bin, 0 oopses, 0 slab reports, dmesg `defog tables from tuning bin (ev 100..16000, rgbra 0)`, defog registers 0x5a00/0x5b80 differ from the previous driver as expected; bad bin falls back to the built-in tables. Device: proven on the T31X light-box camera (the defog tables come from the active tuning bin). The change takes effect only with a firmware image built after it; the fleet image agg67 (flashed 2026-11-04) still carries the old tables.

## 2026-11-04: T30 RawDRC, calibration table SET (gamma), antifog control and GetWB factors (QEMU T30 debug kernel + T30X lab camera for the status-argument and GetWB parts)

- RawDRC: control 0x8000168 (DRC mode, cached in the tuning block; manual mode sets the Iridix limits, other modes use the limits of the WDR mode in use, mode 5 sets the disable bit of register 0x40) and attribute 0x80000a0 (12-byte attribute, strength through the command interface; the getter reads strength, slope, black and white back from the Iridix registers 0x3c4/0x3c8/0x3d0/0x3d4) are served like the vendor module. Before, neither control reached the ISP. QEMU T30: set 0 for off/manual/unlimit, getter returns the register strength. The matching library part is in OpenIMP.
- Calibration table SET: `apical_api_calibration` is rewritten from the vendor module (the lift had dropped the branch-likely jumps after the table copy, kept the status in a global and always re-initialised the Iridix), so a SET of a calibration table now re-applies it (WDR mode switch, Iridix, colour matrices, defect pixel table). Gamma set used to return 0 while the gamma registers (0x10400) kept the old curve; QEMU T30: steep gamma reads back 2702/3326/4095 and linear 1023/2047/4095, the values of the vendor run on the T30X.
- Status argument restored at four `apical_api_calibration` call sites (gamma attribute get/set and two in the core `g_ctrl`): `apical_sensor_calibration_update` wrote its status through a stale register, which oopsed on QEMU T30 (BadVA 0) and hung the T30X camera in the gamma test. A new host check (`t30_callarg_check`, baseline of 55 not yet audited findings) fails `make check` when another call argument is dropped.
- Antifog control 0x8000163 was answered with -1; now served like the vendor module (value cached, preset for strong/medium/weak plus enable, or disable).
- The calibrated AWB publishes its red/blue factors (halved) into system table items 49/50 while AWB is not manual, so `GetWB` no longer reports the 50/50 initial values (T30X: 175/92 while the vendor reads 132/124 on the same scene: the calibrated AWB settles on another balance, an algorithm difference, not the getter; #202 stays open).
- Evidence: T30 QEMU picture area 67 cases, no oops, no slab report; T30X apitest `isp` area 48 PASS / 0 FAIL, 0 oopses; host tests green.

## 2026-11-04: T30 b21 "AE brightness regression" analysed (`claude/t30-ae-fix`; desk analysis, no code change yet)

- b20 -> b21 the AE state is identical (dmesg "calibrated AE": mean 6-7, target 29, int 1440, again 256041 = max, ISP gain 256000 = max in both): the system-items / AE-comp commit did not change the AE. Item 53 reaches the target (b21: aecomp-low target 6, high 139; b20 stayed 29).
- The b20 -> b21 drop (Y 118 -> 64) comes from the exposure-history commit: Iridix collection correction 0x3dc 0 -> 0x100 and strength 0x3c4 0x46 -> 0x2a (vendor 0xfe / 0x24). With 0x3dc = 0 the old Iridix over-brightened; b21 is the correct Iridix behaviour.
- The real defect, present since at least b19: the compact AE is pinned at maximum exposure because the histogram mean never reaches the target, so AE compensation cannot act (vendor Y 70/174, open 64/64). The vendor reads the same histogram (ae_read_full_histogram_data, 0x10000, 4.12 float bins) and computes the same mean (ae_calculate_target), so the signal itself is too low: on the open stack exp-short (ISP gain 1x) gives Y 6.7 with "again 15x" against vendor Y 13.4 at 1x/1x, i.e. the analog gain is not effective or the front end (black level) eats the signal. Also: the again cap (item 29) is ignored (again stays 256041 with limits 32 and 1) and the integration time stops at 1440 lines (vendor 1915).
- Next: dump 0x3e08/0x3e09 (sensor) and the black-level registers on both stacks at base; port vendor ae_calculate_exposure (log2 integrator with anti-windup) once the signal path is right.

## 2026-11-04: T30 compact AE keeps the exposure history, Iridix gets its linear ratio (`claude/t30-ae-exphistory`; QEMU T30, device b21 pending)

- `iridix_fsm_process_interrupt` compares the current frame exposure with the previous one (OEM ring of 8 exposure sets, `cmos_store_frame_exposure_set` / `cmos_move_exposure_history`). The compact AE bypasses `cmos_update_exposure_history()` and the open firmware IRQ gate keeps the CMOS frame-start event off, so the ring never filled or moved: previous = 0 against the current 0x1e828, linear ratio exp2(negative) = 0, register 0x3dc stayed 0 and the Iridix strength (0x3c4) was never written (RawDRC without effect; vendor 0x3dc = 0x100).
- The AE cycle (once per frame) now stores the applied exposure as the pending set and advances the ring (`tx_isp_t30_ae_store_exposure_history`), also on frames without statistics. QEMU T30 debug kernel: before cur=1e828 prev=0 ratio=0 0x3dc=0 0x3c4=0x46; after cur=prev, ratio 0x100, 0x3dc=0x100, 0x3c4 written (0x58, 0x62, 0x63, 0x64 ...). apitest isp 48/0, 0 oopses. Not ported: the 0x400a0/0x400a8 gain mirrors of the OEM function.

## 2026-11-04: T30 system table accessors as the vendor, compact AE honours AE compensation and the manual items (#202; host + QEMU T30, picture effect pending a device batch)

- 21 of the 51 lifted `system_*` accessors (`apical_command` type 1) had lost the SET arm or answered the GET wrongly (manual exposure, manual integration time/gains/sharpening/AWB/saturation, dark/bright target, limits, anti-flicker, bad pixels; `GetExpr` mode was always 0). Each vendor accessor is the same code, so all 51 are now `t30_system_item()` on the vendor item offset (`tx_isp_t30_system_item.h`, host test, `check_t30_system_items.sh`).
- The compact AE ignored the AE compensation (item 53; OEM `ae_calculate_target` adds `(item-0x80)<<11` to the Q16 log2 target), the manual exposure (items 1, 16), integration time (3, 24) and gains (4, 6, 28, 32), and overwrote the table items each frame. `tx_isp_t30_ae_manual.h` (host-tested) carries the arithmetic.
- QEMU T30 (debug kernel): `SetExpr(manual, 100 lines)` GetExpr mode 0 -> 1, it 100, held 3 s; `SetIntegrationTime` manual likewise; apitest isp 48/0, 0 oopses. Vendor-equal, unchanged: `SetExpr` in microseconds is -1 while the sensor reports 0 us/line.

## 2026-11-04: T30 WaitFrame (tuning control 0x8000162) and the frame-done event (QEMU T30; device check pending)

- The lifted `IMAGE_TUNING_CID_WAIT_FRAME` was unusable: its wait queue head was a byte array with a pointer in the spinlock word, the wake-up went to the counter, the wait loop dereferenced a static instead of `current`, and the ISR never raised the frame-done event. Now a proper wait queue, the frame end raises the OEM tuning event 0x4000002 and the control (timeout in jiffies like the vendor, 64-bit count in words 2-3, -ETIMEDOUT / -ERESTARTSYS) is served in `tx_isp_t30_tuning_ctl.h`.
- QEMU T30: three `WaitFrame(100)` return 0 with count 92, 93, 94; timeout 0 returns -1 with the count kept; apitest isp 48/0, 0 oopses. OpenIMP sends the control on T30 as the vendor 1.0.5 does.

## 2026-11-03: T40 CDNS (chroma denoise) runs like the vendor, day and night (#203; host + QEMU + Eufy T40 camera)

- `enable_cdns` is no longer opt-in: it follows the tuning bank flag 19 (TOP bit 19) like YDNS/SDNS, as the OEM `tisp_main_init` does (both sc830ai banks have the flag clear, so the vendor runs CDNS in day and night). `t40_force_active` / `t40_force_bypass` bit 19 override. The day/night bank refresh now reloads the section and repacks at the remembered gain (OEM `tisp_cdns_dn_params_refresh`).
- Eufy T40 .184 day mode: TOP 0x7FD8000D -> 0x7FD0000D (= vendor day value), CDNS unit registers written, apitest 398/0 both, 6 reload cycles 0 oops, picture luma 125.9 -> 125.0, colour ratios unchanged. QEMU t40: 5 reloads 0 oops, TOP 0x7FD0000D.

## 2026-11-03: T30 tuning-bin customer initialisation, system table items at their OEM offsets, AE on a black frame (#202; T30X lab camera + QEMU T30)

- The lifted `apical_custom_initialization()` read an unrelated static array instead of the customer table pointer stored by `init_tx_isp_customer_parameter()`, so the customer initialisation of the tuning bin was skipped (its two `apical_command()` calls also lacked the fifth argument). Now applied: maximum sensor analogue gain 117 -> 130 and maximum ISP digital gain 32 -> 125, the vendor values.
- The 60-byte system table was indexed with the OEM halfword/word index (integration time, maximum integration time, DIS x/y, manual exposure time sat on the wrong bytes, DIS y overwrote the maximum analogue gain). The items are now at their OEM offsets, the table is 4-byte aligned, and the calibrated AE writes the applied integration time into the table like the OEM exposure history (`GetExpr` / `GetIntegrationTime` report the running value: 2880 lines, before 0 / 3).
- AE leaves the minimum exposure on a black frame.
- Evidence: apitest `isp` area 48 PASS / 0 FAIL, 0 oopses on the T30X lab camera; QEMU T30 debug kernel (`slub_debug`): 6 reload cycles with the `isp` area, 48/0, no slab reports; host tests green. AE/AWB behaviour still differs from the vendor (#202 stays open).

## 2026-11-03: T40 lifted subdev-op call sites call the real op (#199; host + QEMU)

- Seven lifted `regtrace_unresolved_jalr` call sites (a counting no-op) now call the OEM callee with the OEM arguments: `tx_isp_vin_init` / `tx_isp_vin_reset` (sensor core ops init / reset of the active sensor), `tx_isp_sensor_release_sensor` (sensor ops ioctl `0x2000001` with the 100-byte user buffer), `ispcore_frame_channel_streamoff` (per-channel stream-off callback `fn(ctx, channel, 0)`; it also returns the callback result again, the lift returned the memset result), `ispcore_activate_module` (subdev event callback `0x4000000` and the slot subdevs' internal activate op) and `ispcore_video_s_stream` (slot subdevs' `s_stream`). Callees and arguments from the vendor `tx-isp-t40.ko` (headless Ghidra + objdump).
- The two `tx_isp_video_link_stream` sites stay a no-op on purpose: they call `video_ops[1]`, which no vendor subdev fills (vendor relocations: vic/vin/csi/ispcore video_ops hold only `[0]`, ispcore also `[2]`), so the call is never made. Marked `dead` in `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_baseline.txt`.
- Reach: none of the repaired sites runs in the default flow. A QEMU T40 probe build logged every site on first entry: 5 reload cycles, apitest isp (61/0) and cases (127/2 = base), ctmove, t40dev and the stream workload entered none of them, 0 oops (the Eufy sysfs counter `unresolved_jalr_count` is 0 as well). The fix is therefore proof against a lost callee the day a path reaches it, not a visible behaviour change.

## 2026-11-03: T32 S4 step 8: tisp init block BCSH from the IQ bin (`claude/t32-s8`; host, QEMU T32, not on a camera)

- One more block of the vendor's AddSensor tisp init as register writes at stream-on (`tx_isp_t32_bcsh.h`): BCSH (IQ bin section 19 with its knot payload and the colour space section 9; 49 writes: exposure-0 parameter set, the 5000 K matrix, saturation, contrast and brightness curves, hue rotation, register list, block id). Identical write for write to the vendor's AddSensor writes (cv8001 PRJ007 bin, 3840x2160; `tests/tx_isp_t32_tisp_test` with `T32_IQ_BIN` and the local `T32_ADD_TRACE`); with the earlier blocks 3347 of the ~5555 core-phase writes (60 %). Order at stream-on: CCM, BCSH, LCE as in the vendor.
- Refused: section byte `+0x4b1` set, `+0x492` > 2, a colour space section on the other `+0x62`/`+0x63` path.
- Clocks (found with the new QEMU, whose model gates the ISP clock like the chip): the open T32 driver never touched the clock framework, so CLKGR0 bit 19 (`gate_isp`) stayed closed and the sensor's frames did not reach the VIC (8 of 8 frames on the vendor module, 0 of 8 on ours). Now as the vendor module: platform rates at load (`div_isp` 200 MHz, `div_isps` 476 MHz, `div_ldc` 588 MHz, `div_ispa`/`div_vpu` 500 MHz), at the first open of `/dev/tx-isp` the parents (`mux_isp`, `mux_isps`, `mux_ldc` -> `mpll`; `mux_ispa`, `mux_vpu` -> `sclka`), the working rates (`div_isp` 219 MHz) and enable of `div_isp`, `div_isps`, `div_ldc`, `gate_isp`, `div_ispa`, `div_vpu`, `gate_ivdc`, `gate_csi`; disabled at the last release. A real kernel gates unused clocks after boot, so this was a latent camera bug as well.
- Not built (vendor tables or physical buffer state): tmo with its LUT ports (1733 writes), defog, AF, RDNS, gsm, `0xd040..0xd05c`.

## 2026-11-04: T40 AE ROI window functions as checked C (#204 part 2, class C; host oracle)

- `tisp_ae_roi_point` (vendor image `0x3175c`) and `tisp_ae_sepc_area` (`0x39c98`) replace the lifted bodies of `tx_isp_t40_recovered.c` (about 200 lines of goto fragments with dropped `isp_printf` arguments) by plain C in `driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_ae_leaf.h`. Unicorn oracle `tests/t40_ae_leaf_host.sh` (`tools/ko_emu/diff_ae_leaf.py`): 1000 random calls each, 0 mismatches in return value, every byte of the heap and globals, the register writes (`0x19054/58/00`, `0x19254/58/00`) and the log messages with their arguments; 19 source mutations detected.
- Behaviour differences to the old lift: the bad-window message now prints all four coordinates as the OEM (the lift passed two); the window check comes before the cache lookup as in the OEM. Unusable channel/frame-size/cache return `-EINVAL` instead of an oops (OEM has no check).
- The factory Eufy build differs in this class (`tisp_ae_roi_point` has no early return on a bad window, `tisp_ae_sepc_area` is another body); the driver follows the vendor image, documented in `tests/README.oracles.md`. Not done in this step: `tisp_ae_sec_min_max_set` (the two builds differ and the vendor image variant prints and stores other words), class D, gamma/WDR EV. No camera run (no ko change visible on the device path before an OTA).

## 2026-11-03: T40 lifted init/refresh twin source removed (#204 part 1; host + QEMU)

- Deleted from `driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_recovered.c` (~2.2k lines): the garbage-collected lifted `tisp_dmsc_*` init/refresh/params/wdr twins, `tisp_gamma_init`, `tisp_bcsh_main_init`, `tisp_ccm_main_init`, `tisp_bcsh_params_transmit`, `tisp_ccm_params_transmit` and their dead callers (`tisp_day_or_night_s_ctrl`, `tisp_main_day_night_mode_ctl`, `tisp_bcsh_dn_params_refresh`, `tisp_ccm_dn_params_refresh`, the unreachable second-sensor tail of `tisp_sec_init`). They were not in the built module. Kept: `tisp_dmsc_main_deinit` / `tisp_dmsc_sec_*` (reachable via `skip_tisp_deinit=0`), AE classes C/D, gamma/WDR EV (parts 2 and 3).
- Host tests and anchor check no longer reference the removed names. Built default ko: same toolchain, `.text` -516 B (static helpers now inlined into their single callers), `.data` -1264 B (the two unreferenced OEM BCSH/CCM default blobs), `.bss` -16 B; not byte-identical for that reason only.

## 2026-11-03: T40 CLM as the vendor runs it, one EV source, translated CT/EV chain and event twins removed (#85; host + QEMU + Eufy T40 camera)

- CLM (colour LUT mapping, TOP bit 16) is plain C (`driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_clm.h`, Unicorn oracle `tests/t40_clm_host.sh`: 0 mismatches against the vendor module, 34 header mutations detected). Like the vendor: `tisp_clm_init(0)` at ISP block init, bit 16 from the bank flag, the CT event re-interpolates the LUT, the LUT is written while CLM is not bypassed, the day/night refresh reloads the parameters. Before, CLM was never initialised and always bypassed. `t40_clm=0` restores that. The lifted `tisp_clm_*` are wrappers to the C; the opt-in writer CLM port is gone.
- EV: one value (integration time x total gain incl. the ISP digital gain, Q10) and one fan-out, the vendor `tisp_main_long_ev_update` (TOP-gated AWB, CCM, ADR, defog, BCSH) in the AWB thread. Before, two sources with different values fed CCM and BCSH.
- Removed: the mechanically translated CT/EV chain (`tx_isp_t40_vawb_gen.inc`, `tx_isp_t40_vct.h`, `TX_ISP_T40_VCT`, `tests/t40_vct_host.sh`, `tools/t40_vawb/`), the unused `t40_ct_top_apply` / `t40_ct_top` (its word bypassed every block on the Eufy), the lifted handlers of the AE events 6..13 / 22..30 (34 functions, never run by default) with `t40_ev_updates`, `t40_module_ctx_inits` and `enable_tisp_main_init_color_inits`. `TX_ISP_T40_BRINGUP_PARAMS=y` builds load again (no unknown symbols).
- Size: `.text` -15.7 KB, `.rodata` -10 KB, `.bss` -41 KB; stripped ko -35 KB, xz -11 KB (`docs/SIZE.md`).
- Camera (Eufy T40, ISP day mode, runtime module swap against the unchanged-next control in one session): TOP 0x40 `0x7FD8000D` (control `0x7FD9000D`; vendor day `0x7FD0000D`, the remaining difference is CDNS, bit 19, opt-in here), CLM CSC registers written; apitest 398 / 0 and t40dev 0 FAIL on both; 6 reload cycles 0 oops on both; picture luma / sat / R/G / B/G control 125.8 / 80.1 / 1.020 / 0.999, branch 127.5 / 79.2 / 1.011 / 1.010 (light changed between the groups: EV 2981 vs 3228); forced CT moves (`ctmove_t40`, now in the OpenIMP apitest) 576 frames, max step at a switch 0.04 (control 0.03), 0 missing frames.
- QEMU T40 (debug kernel, slub_debug): 5 reload cycles 0 oops, apitest isp 61 / 0, cases 127 / 2 (same as the base), `ctmove_t40` PASS, t40dev and the day/night stream workload 0 oops.

## 2026-11-03: T30 tuning controls: the getters and controls the lifted switch had lost (`claude/issue-177`, Lu-Fi/openimp#177; host, QEMU T30, not on a camera)

- The lifted `apical_isp_core_ops_g_ctrl` / `s_ctrl` lost most of their switch arms (inline jump-table cases came out as `-ENOSYS` or the default branch), so 17 of the 26 calls of the apitest `isp` area failed in QEMU. The missing arms are plain C in the new `tx_isp_t30_tuning_ctl.h` (asked first by the tuning ioctl, the lifted switch only sees what the handlers do not serve): anti-flicker, WB mode/gains, WB statistics, AWB/AE weights, AWB/AE/AF histograms, RGB coefficients, AE strategy/compensation/ROI, exposure (`Expr`), EV, max again/dgain, highlight depression, gamma, calibration tables (get and set, the lifted set read garbage), horizontal/vertical flip, colour effect, scene mode, the four picture levels (brightness, contrast, saturation, sharpness: customer mid-node scaling and the contrast curve as in the stock code, value kept in the tuning object so the getters read it back), sensor FPS, running mode (night now switches), firmware freeze, WB attribute. Each arm does what the stock tuning code does for that control id (firmware command, cached value, ISP register block, or nothing where the stock handler is a no-op); the pure arithmetic and id maps are in `tx_isp_t30_tuning_math.h`.
- Lifted-code faults found on the way: `set_sensor_fps` read the sensor fps callback as one byte (jump to address 0 on the first fps change, oops), its fps id table was `5,10,15,20,25,30` instead of the stock `5,15,25,30,50,60`, and it answered "failed" after a good set; the lifted AE strategy set had the polarity inverted (0 selected integration priority); `apical_command` had no calibration type 6 (exposure and gain log2 of `GetEVAttr`).
- Regression guard: `tests/t30_tuning_ctl_host_test.c` with 8 mutations (`tests/check_t30_tuning_ctl.sh`: fps table, mid-node scaling, contrast curve, AWB map, AE strategy, colour-effect map, exposure lines, plus the presence of every getter/setter arm, the four ioctl call sites and the lifted fixes), `make -C tests check` target `t30_tuning_ctl_check`.
- QEMU T30 (sc4236 model, debug kernel `slub_debug=FZPU`, `maxcpus=1`; evidence level `qemu`, no picture or timing claim): apitest `isp` area 25 PASS / 0 FAIL (before: 8 / 17), the cases `fwfreeze`, `hilight`, `expr`, `gamma`, `aeroi`, `aecomp`, `aestrategy`, `contrast`, `ispflip`, `rgbcoef` PASS, `scene` and `colorfx` value round trip (no picture effect in the sensor model), `inttime` reads back (the exposure-effect check cannot pass in QEMU), `tests/t30_qemu/tuning_strength.c` (strengths through the calibration tables, brightness/saturation read-back) `done bad=0`; 6 module reload cycles with the area, the strength test and a sensor register read: 0 oopses, no slab reports. Not done: `Awb_SetCwfShift` (the light source table update faults in the lifted firmware code in QEMU, stays refused).

## 2026-11-03: T40 CT event follow-up through the checked C chain (#85; host + QEMU + Eufy T40 camera)

- `t40_vawb_ct_follow_ct` (vendor AWB, CT event after every AWB process) now runs `t40_ct_update` from `tx_isp_t40_ct_chain.h` (Unicorn-checked against the vendor module): TOP read from register 0x40 gates CCM / LSC / BCSH like the vendor; CCM uses the vendor's `tisp_ccm_main_ct_update` threshold logic (was the literal combined CT+EV update); LSC re-interpolates on every CT event with the vendor's 64 K hysteresis and writes the LUT when the interpolation changed and more than `lsc_wlut_frequency` (5) frames passed since the last write (was: only when CT moved by more than 200 K, written at once; `t40_vawb_lsc_step` removed). The frame number counts AWB processes (the vendor counts frames in the ISP interrupt). CLM still has no CT follow-up (the Eufy day bank enables CLM; the SDK bins do not).
- Size: `.text` +268 bytes, `.data` +16, `.bss` -16.
- Camera (Eufy T40, ISP day mode, runtime module swap against the unchanged-next control): apitest 398/0 and t40dev with only the known scene-driven `W6_AeMaxGain` FAIL (both), 6 reload cycles 0 oops with stable slab and vmalloc, picture statistics equal to the stock module, 8 rounds of forced colour temperature changes (3000 K / 6500 K / auto) with frame-to-frame luma steps below 0.1 and no oops.

## 2026-11-03: T41 optional ISP bypass graph (`isp_bypass_graph=1`, Lu-Fi/openimp#125; device-proven on a T41 camera, default off)

- New module parameter `isp_bypass_graph` (default 0 = nothing changes: `SetISPBypass(1)` stays refused with `-EOPNOTSUPP`, the VIC frame-channel ops answer `-EINVAL`). With 1 the stock `tx_isp_vic_probe` behaviour is restored (`dump_vsd` = VIC device) and `ISP_BYPASS` (0x80085418) is served: channel 0 is pointed at the VIC input descriptor (core events 0x01000004/5) and FrameSource set_fmt/REQBUFS/QBUF/STREAMON/STREAMOFF run on the VIC frame-channel ops (MDMA into the channel buffers, pixel format 7 = RAW16 BGGR, 2 bytes per pixel). The MDMA-done interrupt (VIC status 1 bits 0/1) completes the bank buffers (`vic_mdma_irq_function`). `SetISPBypass(0)` plus the next frame channel open puts the channels back on the ISP descriptors (an addition: stock never repoints, so the vendor channel stays on the VIC ops until reload).
- The mechanically recovered fragments of `vic_frame_channel_{get_fmt,set_fmt,qbuf,streamon,streamoff}` and `vic_mdma_irq_function` were unusable (pointer arithmetic on `uintptr_t *` temporaries, e.g. set_fmt wrote every list head 0x50 bytes too far, qbuf called an unresolved target); they are rewritten as plain C from the OEM 1.2.5 decompile with the H20250310a layout (banks at vic+0x334/+0x368, 32-byte buffer nodes). Pure parts (RAW layout, DMA mode and stride, address registers) are in `tx_isp_t41_vic_bypass.h` with a host test (3 mutations caught).
- QEMU T41 (gc5603): `SetISPBypass(1)` accepted, RAW16 FrameSource enable clean (REQBUFS, QBUF x2, STREAMON), no oops; QEMU has no VIC MDMA model, so no raw frame can be shown there. Parameter off: normal NV12 channel 25/25, `SetISPBypass(1)` -1 as before.
- Camera fixes (T41 test camera, sc5336 2880x1620): (1) the open driver defers the CSI/VIC/sensor start until the first ISP output starts (`ispcore_frame_channel_streamon`), which a bypassed channel never reaches: the VIC MDMA was armed but the sensor never streamed (0 raw frames). The VIC frame-channel stream-on now runs the same gated late input start (`t41_bypass_input_start`) first, before the DMA words and buffer triggers, because the VIC start (control 2, 4, 1) resets the MDMA (with the start after the triggers: VIC frames counted, MDMA never done). (2) Back from the bypass the input start ownership is released so the NV12 channel runs the full late start (MSCA geometry). (3) `vic_mdma_irq_function`: the OEM search for the completed buffer is bounded only by the busy count, which stream-on leaves at -1; with a done address that matches no busy node the OEM loop spins forever in hard-IRQ context. Bounded here by the node count, and a NULL pop ends the search (deviation from OEM, stability).
- Camera proof (module and OpenIMP at run time from /tmp, no flash, no reboot; vendor sample order: `SetISPBypass(ENABLE)`, RAW channel, frames, `DISABLE`, NV12 channel again): 6 module reload cycles, each RAW 10/10 frames 2880x1620 RAW16 (9331200 bytes = 2880 x 2 x 1620, 10-bit data MSB-aligned: low 6 bits always 0, Bayer row/column structure, black level about 69 (10-bit)), then NV12 25/25; 0 oopses; stock module and streamer restored, snapshot 200.
- Vendor reference (vendor tx-isp-t41 1.2.6 + libimp at run time, same order): every call up to `EnableChn`/`SetFrameDepth` of the RAW channel returns 0, then the board hangs silently within about a second (no kernel line, hardware watchdog reset 60 s later), reproduced twice. The vendor `vic_mdma_irq_function` has the unbounded search described above (busy count -1 after stream-on), the most likely cause; no vendor raw frame could be captured on this board.
- libimp side: OpenIMP `claude/issue-125-v2` (`OPENIMP_T41_BYPASS_GRAPH=1`, `PIX_FMT_RAW` -> pixel format 7 on T41).

- Two more blocks of the vendor's AddSensor tisp init as register writes at stream-on (`tx_isp_t32_{ccm,lce}.h`): CCM (section 12 plus its knot payload, 24 writes: colour temperature interpolation at 5000 K with the hysteresis, exposure-0 table copy, the 1/range factors, write list, block id) and LCE (section 15, 33 writes: block grid from the frame size, gain-0 strength words, the 5x5 Gaussian smoothing words `0x25054..0x25070`, default curve). Each is identical write for write to the vendor's AddSensor writes (cv8001 PRJ007 bin, 3840x2160; `tests/tx_isp_t32_tisp_test` with `T32_IQ_BIN` and the local QEMU trace `T32_ADD_TRACE`); LCE's five DMA address words (`0xc090..0xc09c`, `0xc0a4`) are the physical addresses of two buffers the driver allocates (work 0x26400 bytes, default curve 0x5a00 bytes, freed at module exit), so they differ from the vendor run by design. CCM is refused for sections whose `+0x35f` byte is 0 or `+0x38` is 1, LCE for the 1920x1080 frame and `+0x1fb != 0` (flows that use compiled-in vendor defaults). With the earlier blocks: 3298 of the 5555 writes of the vendor's AddSensor core phase (59 %). QEMU T32 debug kernel (`slub_debug=FZPU`, `maxcpus=1`), vendor libimp 2.2.0: 5 load/run/unload cycles, 3 channels 8/8 frames each, 0 oopses, no slab reports; both blocks appear in the MMIO write trace in the vendor's order (YDNS, CCM, LCE, SDNS).

## 2026-11-03: T32 S4 step 6: tisp init blocks MDNS, DPC and GIB from the IQ bin (`claude/t32-s4-6`; host, QEMU T32, not on a camera)

- Three more blocks of the vendor's AddSensor tisp init as register writes at stream-on (`tx_isp_t32_{mdns,dpc,gib}.h`): MDNS 1281 writes (compression setup, `tisp_mdns_func_en`, two `tisp_mdns_all_reg_refresh` passes with `tisp_mdns_start`, trigger and enable), DPC 145 writes (long exposure block, short exposure mirror, other words, block id) and GIB 89 writes (black levels by gain, slopes, short exposure black level, block id), each identical write for write to the vendor's AddSensor writes (cv8001 PRJ007 bin, 3840x2160 SRGGB10; `tests/tx_isp_t32_tisp_test` with `T32_IQ_BIN` and the local QEMU trace `T32_ADD_TRACE`). With the seven blocks of step 5 and the front end, AE/AWB and MSCA blocks: 3241 of the 5555 writes of the vendor's AddSensor core phase (58 %).
- MDNS compression setting: the init parameter is the compression ratio in tenths (11..20 on, 10 off), the same value the ISP memory size uses (`isp_memopt`, default 1500 = ratio 1.5); the compression register setup follows from it and the frame size. The tables come from the bin; the MDNS and DPC interpolation/register functions are a mechanical transcription of the vendor flow, GIB is written by hand from the vendor functions (RGGB-first pointer table only; other Bayer patterns are refused). - **Scanner:** `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_check.py` has a new class `arity` (a cast call with fewer parameters than the callee's prototype, T40 only). 187 call sites in 71 functions on next, each function carries a status in `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_baseline.txt` (compared with the vendor `tx-isp-t40.ko`, headless Ghidra + objdump). Reach was measured: function-entry marks in QEMU T40 (3 reload cycles, timps start/stop) enter only `tx_isp_unlocked_ioctl` and `tx_isp_video_link_stream`; the Eufy sysfs counters (`unresolved_jalr_count`, `cnt_core_ops_init`, `cnt_sec_init`, `cnt_main_long_tgain_update`) are 0.

All other entries are `dead` / `shim` / `ok` / `unreach` with the reason in the baseline.

## 2026-11-03, T41 custom AE library is released when its client dies (an extra beyond the vendor; Lu-Fi/openimp#197)

- **Extra beyond the vendor:** `tx_isp_release` unregisters the custom AE algorithm library of the file that registered it (`t41_ae_algo_release`, same steps as the close: `tisp_ae_algo_deinit`, `tisp_ae_algo_init(ch, 0, 0)`, info block freed once, waiters woken). Stock: a killed client leaves the library registered and the next open answers `EBUSY` until the module is reloaded. With no library registered nothing changes.
- **Protocol (as the T41 AWB library):** the AE ioctls moved into one protocol block in `tx_isp_t41_vae.h`: a get without a registered library answers `EINVAL` at once (the unmodified module waits on a never initialised completion and oopses), the unregister wakes every waiter (`complete_all`), close and release free the info block once, the completion is initialised once and cleared at a new open, a failed copy of the open reply unregisters again.
- **Tests:** `tests/t41_ae_algo_host.sh` (host, 15 mutations detected), `tests/check_t41_custom_ae.sh` (release hook guard), `tests/t41_algo/aerace.c` (device client). T41 test camera, runtime swap of the module, dead-man armed, stock module restored: before (origin/next module) the open after a killed client answers EBUSY (idle client and client with a worker thread in get) and a get without a library oopses; with the change both opens answer 0 and deliver frames, 100 close races + 50 reopen cycles clean, apitest `customalgo_t41` PASS 31 / FAIL 0 (AE and AWB effect), 6 of 6 reload cycles, 0 oopses.

## 2026-11-03, T41 custom AWB algorithm library (`IMP_ISP_SetAwbAlgoFunc`, an extra beyond the vendor; Lu-Fi/openimp#195)

- **New:** the T41 module serves the custom AWB library ioctls 0xc0045423 (get), 0xc0045424 (result), 0xc0045425 (open) and 0xc0045426 (close) on `/dev/tx-isp` (`driver/t41/tx_isp_t41_awb_algo.h`). The stock T41 module has no AWB library path (the dispatcher answers ENOTTY, libimp's open fails and the library is never called); with no library registered nothing changes. The statistics are the native AWB's (current WB-register gains, global statistics, zone statistics), the library is woken once per processed AWB frame, a result with gains in 0x200..0x1800 (1024 = 1.0, the range of `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetWB`) takes the white balance manually through the same control plane as `SetWB` (frozen hardware gains) and the close gives the application's state back (mode, freeze flag, manual gains, CT, WB registers).
- **Protocol (same as the T40 review fix H1 and the T41 AE library):** one mutex guards the registration; a get without a registered library answers EINVAL at once, a get woken by the close EINVAL, an interrupted wait the wait error, the close wakes every waiter, a second open answers EBUSY, the close is idempotent, the completion is initialised once and an open only re-arms it, at most one wake-up is pending, results answer 0 for values they ignore (libimp's worker leaves its loop on any error). The release of the file that registered the library unregisters it, so a crashed streamer cannot leave a registered library behind (the stock AE path keeps it and answers EBUSY to the next open).
- **Tests:** `tests/t41_awb_algo_host.sh` (host, protocol block with poisoned allocations, 11 mutations detected), `tests/check_t41_custom_awb.sh` (source guard), `tests/t41_algo/awbrace.c` (device client: argument errors, restore of the white balance, 100 close races, killed clients, re-open). Camera evidence in Lu-Fi/openimp#195.

## 2026-11-03, T30 frame channel format ioctl in the 0x5c-byte form of the vendor libimp (Lu-Fi/openimp#178)

- **Cause of the two sizes:** the vendor T30 libimp sends `0xc05c56c3` / `0x405c56c4`: type, the 48-byte `v4l2_pix_format` of a 4.x kernel (the four words `flags`, `ycbcr_enc`, `quantization`, `xfer_func` after `priv`), then crop, scaler and rate words. The kernel 3.10 driver works on the 0x4c-byte object (32-byte pix). The two differ only by those four words; every other field sits 16 bytes further on in the 0x5c form.
- **Driver:** `tx-isp-t30` accepts both forms. The 0x5c form is converted to the 0x4c object and back (`tx_isp_t30_fmt5c.h`); the four extra words are not interpreted and come back as sent (zero on a get). Before, the driver did not know the command and the ioctl returned an arbitrary value of the raw fall-through. `GET_FORMAT` now clears the object first (fields the handler leaves unset used to be copied to user space from the kernel stack).
- **Evidence (QEMU `ingenic-t30` with the debug kernel, `slub_debug=FZPU`, one CPU; no T30 camera):** `tests/t30_qemu/fmt5c.c` sets and gets 2304x1536 NV12 and a scaled 640x360 channel in both forms (equal size, pitch and sizeimage), refuses `field=1` with EINVAL in the new form, and the extra words survive; unfixed module: SET returns garbage (`rc=-1068672244`), 9 checks fail. 6 reload cycles of the fixed module with the format test and `fs_two_channels`, all slabs validated after each step: 0 oopses, 0 red-zone or poison reports. Host: `t30_fmt5c_host_test` with a six-mutation check (`check_t30_fmt5c.sh`).

## 2026-11-03, T40 custom AE/AWB get-handle: lock, NULL and close race, interrupted wait (review H1, M3, M4, M5; Lu-Fi/openimp#65), `tisp_wdr_log2`

- **Oops fixed (H1):** the custom AWB get-handle ioctl (0xc0045423) used `awb_info_mine[idx]` after its interruptible wait without checking the wait result or the slot, and the close ioctl frees that slot. A GET without an open library, a GET interrupted by a signal, or a GET that returned after the close wrote 2.7 KB through NULL + 8, a stale pointer or a freed block. Reproduced in the debug QEMU T40 (slub_debug=FZPU) with a small race client: origin/next oopsed in all three cases (1 and 2 CPUs); the AE twin had a NULL check but the same race against the close. The vendor module has the same unchecked code; this is a stability fix beyond the vendor.
- **Protocol now:** one mutex (`t40_algo_mutex`) guards the info blocks: open, close, the result ioctls and the part of a GET between its wake-up and `copy_to_user` hold it, so a block cannot be freed under a GET. The four completions are initialised once (zeroed .bss is not a valid wait queue; the open re-initialised a queue a GET could still sit on) and an open only re-arms them, before the wake-up is enabled. A GET without a registered library returns -EINVAL at once, an interrupted wait -ERESTARTSYS (EINTR or restart, as every interruptible kernel wait), a GET woken by the close -EINVAL; the close wakes every waiter of its channel (`complete_all`), so a library worker leaves its loop without a signal. A library result (0xc004541c / 0xc0045424) after the close is refused (it took the white balance over again). A second open of a registered channel keeps its block (the OEM leaked the first one).
- **Lift defects found on the way:** the AWB open lost `lw s0,36(sp)` (the channel from the user struct; the slot index was whatever s0 held), channel 1 waited on and the second-sensor AWB step completed the completion of channel 0 (the `+16` of `awb_algo_comp + 16` was lost in both places), and the AWB get-handle dropped the gains and global statistics the OEM stores at info+8..+28.
- **M5:** with a failed AWB init (no AWB step runs) the AWB statistics interrupt still wakes a registered custom AWB library (empty statistics) instead of leaving its GET asleep.
- **M4:** the white balance the application had before a custom AWB library took it over (automatic, or its manual gains; a SetAwbAttr while the library runs updates it) is restored at unregister. The vendor leaves the library's last gains in manual mode (`tisp_awb_algo_deinit_internal` only clears the enable flag); restoring is beyond the vendor.
- **M3:** the custom AE result uses the gain the sensor really applies: the sensor's `alloc_again` (sensor attribute) on the AE ladder step, as the sensor-apply path sends it, instead of the GC4653 ladder value; the step is the one whose sensor gain is nearest to the request (a floor step was up to 15 % below the request on the camera), the ISP digital gain makes up the rest. The exposure reported to the library (get-handle) is the sensor gain too.
- **`tisp_wdr_log2`** (OEM: smallest k in 1..15 with 2^k >= x, 15 otherwise) ended in `return 0`, so `tisp_wdr_direct_reg_refresh` stored 0 for every log2 field; and that function's first log2 loop read and wrote at `uintptr_t *`-scaled offsets (+104/+144 instead of +26/+36). Both as the OEM now; WDR only (not active on the test camera's scene, so the camera run shows no regression, not the WDR effect).
- **Event thread list corruption after a streamer restart (found while testing, pre-existing on next):** every stream start called `tisp_event_init` again, which re-initialised the event wait queue and node lists while the `tisp-event-0` thread (started once per module load) slept on that queue or held a popped node: "list_del corruption" in the event thread on the second stream start (debug QEMU T40, reproduced with the unchanged next module and a plain streamer stop/start). The re-init now keeps the queue, the lock and the thread's node and only drops the events still queued; 0 reports in 5 streamer restarts with the race client running (1 and 2 CPUs).
- `dumpQueue` (the tuning-node poll wait queue) was a 4-byte int: `__init_waitqueue_head` wrote 8 bytes past it into `tisp_par_ioctl` and `major`. Now a `wait_queue_head_t`.
- **Tests:** `tests/t40_algo_handle_host.sh` (the lock/completion block of the driver compiled on the host with pthread stand-ins, 16 cases, 5 of 5 mutations detected), `tests/t40_wdr_log2_host.sh` (unicorn against the OEM instructions, 70,105 inputs, 0 differences, 3 of 3 mutations detected; SKIP without the vendor module), source guards for the channel 1 completion in `check_t40_custom_algo_wake.sh`.
- **Device (Eufy T40, runtime module swap of the final module, night scene, WDR not active so the WDR fix shows no regression only):** apitest 398 PASS / 0 FAIL, custom AE/AWB case 30 / 0 (AE effect: sensor gain 13584 for a request of 13564, AWB effect PASS, close called once), race client against the running streamer (no open, signal, close race, 60-round hammer, AE and AWB, both channels): 0 stuck, 0 oops; 6 reload cycles 0 oops. QEMU (1 and 2 CPUs, slub_debug): 0 oops, 0 slub / list reports in 800+ race rounds with streaming; the unchanged next module oopses in every race scenario.
## 2026-11-03, T41 ISP-device ioctls: LDC/GPIO vendor no-op, StartNightMode and WDR_OPEN as the stock module (Lu-Fi/openimp#75 #171)

- LDC (0xc0045479..7b, `ISP_LDC_INIT`, `ISP_LDC_Get/SetAttr`) and GPIO (0xc004548e/f, `ISP_SET_GPIO_INIT_OR_FREE`, `ISP_SET_GPIO_STA`) now return 0 without any effect like the OEM 1.2.5 module, which has no handler for them (was -ENOTTY). This is a vendor no-op, not a working LDC or GPIO function.

Host test `tx_isp_t41_devioctl_test`; QEMU T41 `t41isp` with `APITEST_RISKY=1`: PASS, 0 oopses.

N oracles SKIPPED (...)` naming every host check that printed `SKIP` (it still counts as passed, so the default flow works without vendor files). New `make -C tests check-oracles` runs the eight Unicorn / vendor-data checks with `T40_ORACLE_STRICT=1`: a SKIP is a failure. Inputs and how to set them: `tests/README.oracles.md`.

The OEM does it the other way round (main 0x50140, second 0x50170). Not visible on the Eufy (GIB runs only with `t40_gates_oem_offset=1`, and the sc830ai is no IR sensor), fixed.

Return values, blocks and every register write in order identical (1000 iterations x 9 functions, 0 mismatches after the fix), 50 one-token mutations of the source are detected (equivalent mutants were found and dropped while building the set).
- **LSC oracle** (`tools/ko_emu/diff_lsc.py`, `tests/t40_lsc_host.sh`, same gating): the hand-written LSC chain (`tx_isp_t40_lsc_lit.inc`: `valid_judge`, `interp_by_ct` with zones, blending and the 64 K hysteresis, `interp_by_gain`, `write_reg`, `real_write_lut`) against the OEM: tool / real blocks, last gain / CT / zone and register writes identical (400 iterations, 0 mismatches), 55 one-token mutations are detected. The OEM works on the pointer record of `tisp_lsc_tool_to_awdr`; the harness builds that record from the same tool block (linear or WDR tables).

## 2026-11-03, T23 optional ISP bypass graph (`isp_bypass_graph=1`, Lu-Fi/openimp#129, T23 device-tested, default off)

- New module parameter `isp_bypass_graph` (default 0 = nothing changes: the link ioctls stay stubs and `ISP_PROCESS` (control `0x8000164`) accepts only 1). With 1, the link ioctls `0x800456d0..d3` and `ISP_PROCESS` values other than 1 are served for the vendor `SetISPBypass(DISABLE)` sequence (DISABLE_LINKS, DESTROY_LINKS, control 0, CREATE_LINKS(1), ENABLE_LINKS; CREATE_LINKS(1) is refused unless the control came first, as the vendor orders it): frame channel 0 is then fed by the VIC MDMA with the sensor's raw frame as `RG12` (2 bytes per pixel, S_FMT returns the sensor size, `sizeimage` = w*h*2; other formats and sizes and channels 1 and 2 are refused in this graph). QBUF goes to the five VIC banks, STREAMON arms the VIC MDMA (registers `0x300..0x380`, the same ones the `snapraw` capture uses) and the frame-done interrupt completes the oldest loaded bank into the normal done list, so DQBUF and the wait ioctl need no change. Like the vendor frame-done interrupt, the capture count (`0x300` bits 16..19) is refreshed after every frame and QBUF, so the engine stops instead of writing into a bank whose buffer user space holds. DESTROY_LINKS (also `ENABLE`/`CREATE_LINKS(0)` from the library, the last close and the module exit) stops the graph; the raw channel then delivers nothing more and is recreated, as at the vendor.
- Bank bookkeeping is a pure header (`driver/t23/tx_isp_t23_bypass.h`) with a host test and a mutation check (`tests/tx_isp_t23_bypass_test.c`, `tests/t23_bypass_host.sh`: 15 mutations, all detected).
- Device (T23 Jooan A6M, sc1a4t, test module and libimp from /tmp, 120 s dead-man, no flash, no reboot, no sound; vendor sample order: CreateChn, SetISPBypass(DISABLE), SetChnAttr(RAW), EnableChn): 10/10 raw frames in 0.63 s, 1280x720, fourcc RG12, 1843200 bytes (vendor reference: 10/10 in 0.54 s, same size and format); sample mean 95..100 against 101 at the vendor, 16-bit samples (10-bit value in the upper bits), neighbour correlation 0.93 horizontally and 0.99 vertically (a real picture). SetISPBypass(ENABLE) returns 0; a second raw capture in the same module load and the recreated NV12 channel (25/25 frames) work after it. 6 module reload cycles (each: raw, raw, NV12): 0 oopses, stock module and streamer restored, snapshot 200. The camera was in night mode (ISP running mode 1) for the run. QEMU T23 does not model the VIC MDMA (see the issue). Parameter off: unchanged.
- libimp side: OpenIMP `OPENIMP_T23_BYPASS_GRAPH=1` (RAW -> RG12 on T23).

## 2026-11-02 (night), T31 optional ISP bypass graph (`isp_bypass_graph=1`, Lu-Fi/openimp#129, T31L device-tested, default off)

- New module parameter `isp_bypass_graph` (default 0 = nothing changes, link config 1 is only stored as before). With 1, the vendor `SetISPBypass(DISABLE)` sequence (DISABLE_LINKS, DESTROY_LINKS, S_CTRL `0x8000164`=0, CREATE_LINKS(1), ENABLE_LINKS) builds the bypass graph: the ISP core stays idle (IRQ off, as the vendor) and frame channel 0 is fed by the VIC MDMA with the sensor's raw frame (`RG12`, 2 bytes per pixel). QBUF goes to the VIC bank ring (`ispvic_frame_channel_qbuf`), STREAMON/OFF to `ispvic_frame_channel_s_stream`/clearbuf, the MDMA-done interrupt delivers the finished bank buffer to the frame channel (`tx_isp_bypass_frame_done`). Channels 1 and 2 have no source in this graph. Value 1 + CREATE_LINKS(0) restores the normal graph; like the vendor, the raw channel is dead afterwards and must be recreated.
- Device (T31L sc2336, runtime ko from /tmp, vendor sample order: CreateChn, SetISPBypass(DISABLE), SetChnAttr(RAW), EnableChn): 10/10 frames in 0.40 s, 1920x1080, fourcc RG12, 4147200 bytes, 16-bit samples with the low 4 bits clear and a black pedestal of 4160 (vendor reference: 10/10 in 0.35 s, same size and format). SetISPBypass(ENABLE) with the raw channel up: no more frames, as the vendor. Recreated NV12 channel: 25/25 frames. No oops, stock modules and streamer restored without a reboot. QEMU T31: the VIC MDMA is not modelled (links, format, REQBUFS and STREAMON round trips clean, no frame); parameter off: normal channel 25/25 and DISABLE -1 unchanged.
- libimp side: OpenIMP `claude/bypass-t31-lib` (`OPENIMP_T31_BYPASS_GRAPH=1`, RAW -> RG12 on T31).

## 2026-11-02: T31 frame channel open no longer rewrites a registered misc minor (`claude/t31-reload-minor`; QEMU repro + fix, T31X sc4336p reload x6)

- **T31: rmmod left `/sys/class/misc/framechan0`/`framechan1` behind.** `frame_channel_open()` binds the FS node `/dev/framechanN` (its own misc minor) to the shared `frame_channels[N]` by name and `frame_channel_prepare()` then wrote that node's minor into `frame_channels[N].miscdev.minor` while the `framechanN` misc device was registered under another minor. At unload `misc_deregister()` destroyed the device with the wrong minor and the `framechanN` sysfs device stayed (its dynamic minor bit leaked too). Every later insmod then failed `misc_register("framechan0")` with -EEXIST: ours tolerated it (core frame channel nodes missing), the vendor tx-isp-t31 oopses in its error path (misc_deregister of an unregistered device, NULL+4) and the camera rebooted. On cameras where `/dev/framechanN` points at the FS node (the streamer opens it at boot), every unload leaked. Fix: the minor is only written while the miscdev is not registered.
- QEMU (T31L image, stock ko): opening the FS node changes the core minors 51/50 to 55/54 and rmmod leaves framechan0/1; fixed ko: minors unchanged, nothing left after 2 load/open/unload rounds, the vendor ko then loads (rc 0, no misc error). T31X sc4336p: 6 unload/reload cycles of the fixed ko with the streamer running 15 s per cycle: all rc 0, snapshot 200 every cycle, 0 oops, no new leftover nodes (the two nodes leaked by the stock ko at boot remain until the image carries the fix).
