- Proof: `tools/ko_emu/diff_ccm_conv.py` runs the OEM instructions of the vendor `tx-isp-t40.ko` in Unicorn against the header on edge-weighted random matrices (3000 calls each, 0 mismatches; four one-character mutations are caught); `tests/t40_ccm_conv_host.sh` is part of `make check` (SKIP without `T40_OEM_KO`). `.text` -64 bytes, `.data`/`.bss` identical.

## 2026-11-02 (later), T40 AWB init / EV / per-frame chain in C, last translated AWB function gone, `t40_vawb=0` fallback removed (Lu-Fi/openimp#85 step D, host bit-exact, Unicorn-checked, camera-tested on T40)

- The remaining AWB functions are plain C: `tx_isp_t40_awb_init.inc` (`tisp_awb_params_transmit`, `params_refresh`, `set_hardware_param`, `dn_params_refresh`, `main_ev_update`, `ev_update_Ywgt`) and `tx_isp_t40_awb_main.inc` (`tisp_awb_long_par_update`, `spec_calculate`, `main_process`). The mechanically translated AWB code is gone (`tx_isp_t40_vawb_gen.inc` 5948 -> 3798 lines, only the CT/EV colour chains remain). The 128-entry context pointer table is two offset tables, the parameter block copies are a table. The dual-sensor tele branch of the OEM process is not taken with one sensor and is gone.
- The old lifted AWB path (`t40_vawb=0`, `t40_vendor_awb_process`, lifted init / process / IRQ branches) is removed; a failed AWB init now returns its error instead of falling back.
- Proof: `tests/t40_vawb_host.sh` (`GR_REF`, `FUZZQ`) runs C and the translated OEM code on the same fuzzed state per call (register writes and reads in order, events, completions, faults, all state of both channels): about 990,000 compared calls over three seeds plus the `make check` seeds, 0 differences; every C line is covered except the invalid-pointer guards; 13 one-token mutations are detected (wired into `t40_bitexact_host`). `tools/ko_emu/diff_awb_chain.py` runs the C (built for mipsel, `tests/t40_vawb/build_emu.sh`) and the real vendor module in Unicorn on the same random state: 8 functions, 0 mismatches (known differences of that vendor build, see the script header). QEMU reload x5 with the debug kernel (`slub_debug`, one CPU): 0 oops, same mm-slab noise as the stock module. `make -C tests check`: 129 passed, 0 failed.
- Size against next 838f2b84: `.text` -104.9 KB, stripped module -113.7 KB, xz -26.3 KB, `.bss` -176 bytes.

## 2026-11-02, T40 layout class: VIC interrupt counters and the output format table are real objects (Lu-Fi/openimp#189, camera-tested on T40: no regression; the lifted VIC interrupt routine accounted no interrupt there)

- `isp_vic_interrupt_service_routine` bumped its 75 error/stat counters at `&isp_memopt + 72 words + K` (typed-pointer x4 scaling of an OEM `.bss` anchor), i.e. inside unrelated live objects (`pstMainAeOri_storage`, `vawb_frames`, `ae_cache`). They are the real object `vic_irq_cnt[3][25]` now (OEM `vic_err`, 3 x 25 words, section `.t40c2`). Compared with the vendor `tx-isp-t40.ko` (objdump, headless Ghidra, and executed in Unicorn: 900 random register states, all 75 counters hit, counters/VIC device words/register writes/return value identical; the old object differs in every case; local only, nothing vendor stored).
- Same function: `v0`/`v1` were `uintptr_t *`, so every `v1 + 1` counter step added 4 (the VIC device counters too); the 45 counters of status bits 16..30 were bumped on ANY pending bit (the lift dropped the MIPS `ext`), bit 31 was an always-true pointer compare; every exit returned a leftover register instead of 1.
- `/proc/jz/isp/isp-w02` (OEM `isp_vic_frd_show`: three state rows and the three 23-value counter rows) is published; the lifted show function copied `vic_err` onto itself. The 96 KB of unused lifter pads (`vic_err`, `data_b441c`, `data_b4480`) left `.bss`; `.data`/`.rodata` keep their layout.
- `ispcore_core_ops_init` read the OEM `isp_output_fmt` descriptors (9 x 44 bytes) at `&sclk_name - 7384` words; they are the const object `t40_isp_output_fmt` now (byte-identical to the vendor table). The other typed-register findings of that function stay `open` in `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_baseline.txt`.
- Host: `make -C tests check` 129 passed / 0 failed (new `t40_vic_layout_check.py`, scanner `--expect-clean` for the ISR). QEMU T40 (debug kernel, `slub_debug=FZPU`, one CPU), 5 reload cycles: the only slab report is the known `vm_area_struct` red-zone noise, no driver cache. Camera (T40 test board, runtime module swap, dead-man armed): apitest all areas 398 PASS / 0 FAIL, t40dev 82 PASS / 0 FAIL, 6 reload cycles clean (slab flat, vmalloc 0), 0 oopses, snapshot 200, stock module restored. The VIC counters and the VIC device words read 0 before and after the runs (not reached: the lifted routine accounts no interrupt on this camera), so the counter fix itself has host/emulation evidence only.

## 2026-11-02, T40 AWB gain / threshold chain and statistics unpack in C (Lu-Fi/openimp#85 step D, host bit-exact, camera-tested on T40)

- Two groups of the translated vendor AWB chain are plain C now: the gain / threshold chain (`tx_isp_t40_awb_gain.inc`: `tisp_awb_set_gain`, `tisp_awb_set_regional_threshold`, `tisp_awb_set_lum_th_freq`, `tisp_awb_tgain_update`, the interpolation helper, the AWB trigger write and the AE mean update) and the per-frame statistics (`tx_isp_t40_awb_stats.inc`: `tisp_awb_get_statistics` as one table-driven loop, `tisp_awb_sat_weight`). The vendor tele-gain branch (about 965 translated lines) is gone: it is reachable only with the wide-tele parameter set, which the single-sensor init clears. The generated chain file shrinks from 8190 to 5948 lines, `.text` by about 51 KB.
- Proof: `tests/t40_vawb_host.sh` runs C and the translated code on the same fuzzed state per call and compares the register write sequence (address, value, order), the fault counter and every byte the functions reach; about 700,000 compared calls over several seeds, 0 differences. Camera (T40 test board, runtime module swap): apitest all areas 398 / 0, 6 of 6 reload cycles, 0 oopses. The issue stays open for the rest of the AWB chain, DMSC / MDNS literals and the AE / YSP leftovers.

## 2026-11-02 (night), T41 custom AE algorithm library path (Lu-Fi/openimp#76, QEMU and camera-tested on T41)

- **New:** the user-space AE library commands of the stock T41 dispatcher on `/dev/tx-isp`: 0xc004541d open, 0xc004541b get handle, 0xc004541c set handle, 0xc004541e close (`t41_ioctl_ae_algo`, `tx_isp_t41_vae.h`). `tisp_ae_algo_init`, `tisp_ae_algo_handle` and `tisp_ae_algo_deinit_internal` are added to the mechanically translated vendor AE chain (`tools/t41_vae/gen.sh` roots, the new `ta_custom_*` objects in `asm2c.py` / `tx_isp_t41_vae.h`; the regenerated file is identical for the existing 67 functions). While a library is registered the translated `tisp_ae_calc_process` skips its EV allocation and completes `ae_algo_comp`, which wakes the get-handle ioctl once per frame; the library's answer goes to the sensor through the translated sensor callbacks, and the realized exposure of the proc file and the IMP getters follows it (AE state +8608..). The statistics block is the tuning AE statistics (`t41_ae_stats_to` splits the old user-copy function).
- The stock module has one AE channel and no AWB library path (0xc0045420 and 0xc0045423..26 are not in its dispatcher): they keep answering -ENOTTY. Hardening against the stock code: the info block is cleared after close (a second close freed it twice, #65 on T40), a get handle without open or after close answers -EINVAL, an interrupted wait returns the error, channel numbers above 0 and the safe AE controller (`t41_vendor_ae=0`) are refused.
- Source guard `tests/check_t41_custom_ae.sh` in `make -C tests check` (128 passed / 0 failed). QEMU T41 with the `slub_debug` kernel (fresh guest, three library sessions on one module instance): open / close 1 of 1 each time, about 245 handle calls, slab validate clean. Camera (T41 test board, runtime swap of the module, dead-man reset armed): the library's integration time and gain reach the sensor (ISP state and picture follow, luma 65.4 to 26.4 to 73.5 to 66.3), the internal AE controls again after the close; apitest sys,fs,enc,osd,ivs,isp,su PASS 266 / FAIL 3 (known AF / LDC no-handler), default cases 164 / 0, 6 of 6 reload cycles, 0 oopses.

## 2026-11-02, T23 lifted-code triage against the vendor T23 module (Lu-Fi/openimp#189 pass 5, host and compile, camera-tested on T23 for the default path only)

- Compared with the vendor T23 module 1.3.0 `tx-isp-t23.ko` (local analysis, nothing vendor copied): `ispcore_core_ops_ioctl` is now a hand-written event walk (`tx_isp_t23_core_ioctl.h`, host test `tx_isp_t23_core_ioctl_test`; the lifted version had no third argument and walked the sub-device table with typed-pointer strides), `jz_isp_ccm` (lost `movz` and skip branch, index/offset registers retyped) and `tisp_ae_mean_update` (offset registers retyped). The latter two run only with the module parameters `source_core_start=0`.
- Scanner: the indented closing brace of `printf` made every prototype below it a reachability root; fixed, so `open` now means reachable. The 22 reachable T23 entries were triaged (3 fixed, 19 dead because the hand-written layer replaces them or nothing dispatches them), unlinked entries are `dead`. Camera (T23, default image, full OTA): apitest all areas PASS 420 / FAIL 11 (previous image, same session: 399 / 32; no function worse), 6/6 module reload cycles, RTSP, stream and day/night cycles clean, oops 0. `jz_isp_ccm` / `tisp_ae_mean_update` are reached only with the bring-up parameter `source_core_start=0`; that mode resets the camera within seconds on this tree and on its parent alike, so those two fixes have no camera-verified effect.

## 2026-11-02 (night), T40 custom AE/AWB results reach the sensor and the white balance (Lu-Fi/openimp#65, camera-tested on T40: exposure and colour follow the library)

- **AWB result oops:** the first library answer with `change = 1` (ioctl 0xc0045424) ran `tisp_s_awb_attr` -> `tisp_awb_set_frz`, whose lifted code addressed a bss anchor that is NULL (kernel oops in the library's worker thread, found in QEMU with `slub_debug`). It now writes through the AWB context (`pst{Main,Sec}AwbOri + 476`, the object `tisp_awb_get_frz` reads) and skips a context that is not set up.
- **AE result went nowhere:** `tisp_ae_algo_handle` (ioctl 0xc004541c) drove the `sensor_ctrl_main` slots, which are stubs in this driver. For sensor 0 without WDR the result is now committed like the driver's own exposure loop: integration time clamped to the sensor range (gain scaled to keep the product, as the vendor does after `alloc_it`), analog gain to the nearest ladder step, the remainder in the ISP digital gain, through the AE sensor-apply path. The info handed to the library (get-handle ioctl) carries the live exposure time and gains instead of zeros.
- **AWB result:** the returned R/B gains set the driver's manual white balance (the path of `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetAwbAttr`); unregistering the library gives the automatic mode back. While the gains are held `tisp_awb_main_process` still completes `awb_algo_comp`, otherwise the library's get-handle ioctl slept for good after its first answer. The AWB info carries the live gains.
- Camera (T40 test camera, runtime module swap, dead-man armed): AE request 1/6 of the exposure time read back as 319 lines, luma 88 to 36; 4x analog gain luma 133; start exposure again 85; after unregistering the normal AE is back (1800 lines, luma 120). AWB red gains Cb/Cr 112/197, blue gains 214/141, ISP gains followed each request, automatic mode back. Regression: apitest all areas PASS 398 / FAIL 0, `t40dev` 0 FAIL, 6 of 6 reload cycles, 0 oopses. Host: `make -C tests check` 127 passed, 0 failed (source guard `check_t40_custom_algo_wake.sh` extended); QEMU T40 with `slub_debug`: 0 oops, slab validate clean.

## 2026-11-02, T40 custom AE/AWB algorithm workers are woken (Lu-Fi/openimp#65, camera-tested on T40: callbacks reached, effect on the picture not verified)

- Why `handle()` of a user-space AE/AWB library (`IMP_ISP_SetAeAlgoFunc` / `SetAwbAlgoFunc`) was never called: (1) AWB: the translated vendor AWB chain read a private copy of `tawb_custom_en` that `tisp_awb_algo_init` never writes, so `complete(awb_algo_comp)` at the end of `tisp_awb_main_process` was never reached; it now reads the driver's array (`awb_algo_enable`). (2) AE: the vendor ends `tisp_ae_main_process` (event 1) with `complete(ae_algo_comp)` when a custom AE is registered; this driver does not push event 1 (T40-DROP-OEM-AE), so nothing woke the get-handle ioctl. The AE histogram IRQ now does that last step (statistics are already filled there), and the own NV12 exposure loop stands still while a custom AE is registered, as the vendor skips its exposure allocation (`ta_custom_en == 0` guard).
- (3) AE open: a freshly loaded module has no sensor attribute in the core's copy of the sensor video block until the first stream starts, but libimp registers the libraries in `IMP_ISP_EnableSensor`; `tx_isp_set_ae_algo_open` therefore answered -1 (EPERM) and the get-handle ioctl read `attr + 180` from address 0. Both now take the bound sensor's own attribute (`sd + 0x304`, the block that is copied later) while the copy is empty (`t40_ae_algo_attr`). The AE init attribute limits are still 0 when registered before the first stream.
- Host: `make -C tests check` 126 passed / 0 failed (new source guard `check_t40_custom_algo_wake.sh`; the bit-exact harness still patches the private `vawb_tawb_custom_en` declaration).
- QEMU T40 (debug kernel, `slub_debug=FZPU`, one CPU), apitest case `customalgo_t40` after a fresh module load without a prior stream (the camera sequence), run twice: AE `handle()` 119-125 calls (about 12 per second), AWB 121-125, open and close 1 of 1, FPS notify reached, 0 oops, slab validate clean. Before the fixes: AE open EPERM, AE and AWB `handle()` 0 calls.
- Free once: the QEMU run with `slub_debug` showed a double free in the AE and AWB close ioctls (0xc004541e / 0xc0045426): libimp sent a second close after a worker that did not leave on the cancel, and both handlers freed the info block unconditionally. They now take the pointer, clear the slot and free only a non-NULL pointer; the AWB close also takes the channel from the argument (the lift used 0) with the range check of the AE twin. libimp (OpenIMP) sends the close once.
- Camera (T40 test board, image with the stock module, branch module swapped in at runtime, test libimp, `OPENIMP_ISP_ALGO_FUNC=1`): `customalgo_t40` AE and AWB open 1, `handle()` about 190 calls each with live statistics, FPS notify 2, close 1 of 1, 0 oops. Regression with the branch module: apitest all areas PASS 398 / FAIL 0, area t40dev 83 PASS lines / 0 FAIL, 6/6 reload cycles clean, picture luma equal to the stock module within 2. "Reached" means the callbacks ran with delivered statistics; the test callbacks return neutral values, so a library changing the exposure or white balance is not verified. The first camera run of this series left the camera unreachable after the restore (cause unknown, not reproduced; the restore is now stepwise and a dead-man reset was armed).

## 2026-11-01, T40 lost tail calls / lost ALU in lifted code, camera proof (Lu-Fi/openimp#189 pass 4, camera-tested on T40; T41 proc open left as a shim)

- Restored on T40 (vendor T40 module and libt40-firmware compared): `system_reg_write_af` (both legs fall through to the AF register store), `tisp_s_module_control` (lost mask `0x200080` and the MDNS trigger tail), `tisp_ae_s_comp` (hand translation of the lost tail), `tisp_csc_set_attr` and `tisp_dmsc_params_refresh` (no default runtime change). Host test `tests/t40_lost_tail_host.sh`.
- Camera (T40 test board, image with `tx-isp-t40.ko` of this tree): apitest 398 PASS / 0 FAIL, t40dev equal to the previous image in a same-scene runtime ko A/B, 6 module reload cycles clean, no oops. The module-control word follows toggled bits (`0x7FD9000D` -> `0x7FD9008D` / `0x7FD9000F`), the AF register 0x1a038/0x1a238 reads `0xC8F1` on the old and the new module (the stored value is not distinguishable on that board), contrast and AE compensation numbers equal. These are "reached by that code path" results, not a visible picture change.
- Reverted after the first camera run: `tisp_s_fixed_contrast_attr` stays a shim. The restored tail `tisp_bcsh_set_mjpeg_contrast` runs the dead lifted `tisp_bcsh_ev_update` (NULL state) and oopsed on `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetFixedContraster`.
- T41 `video_input_cmd_open` stays a shim: the lifted `video_input_cmd_fops` has no `.read`/`.release` (a read is -EIO), and with the tail plus the seq trio restored the proc file printed garbage because the lifted `video_input_cmd_show` uses wrong string labels. Re-lift `video_input_cmd_show` first.

## 2026-11-01, T40 module size: opt-in colour chains and bring-up parameters out of the default build (Lu-Fi/openimp#85, camera-tested on T40)

- `TX_ISP_T40_VCT=y` (Kbuild, default off) builds the translated OEM CT/EV colour chains (`t40_ct_chains=1`, opt-in, never device-tested, 0 hits in QEMU coverage); without it `t40_ct_chains` is the constant 0 and the literal/C follow-ups run as before. `t40_ct_top` / `t40_ct_top_apply` stay. `tests/t40_vct_host.sh` keeps testing the chain on the host.
- 49 parameters that no doc, test, tool, OpenIMP or firmware file uses moved behind `TX_ISP_T40_BRINGUP_PARAMS` (variables keep their defaults; list in `docs/SIZE.md`). One of them, `t40_module_ctx_inits` (default 0), was the only way into the lifted gamma/LSC/CCM/BCSH/CLM main inits, which leave the default build with it.
- Module (stripped, `TX_ISP_LOAD_STATS=y` image build): 1,221,560 -> 1,075,212 bytes (-146 KB), xz 339,580 -> 302,692 (-37 KB), `.text` 765,612 -> 638,372, `.modinfo` 14,272 -> 11,928, loaded size 1,836,704 -> 1,706,048; `.bss` -80 and `.data` -48 bytes (removed parameter variables), `.t40c2` unchanged.
- Coverage leg (QEMU T40, translated-block log): apitest areas sys/fs/osd/ivs/isp/su/cases plus t40dev on top of the earlier stream/day-night/image-control workload reach 101 more functions (33 KB); 542 functions (329 KB of the old build, including the 118 KB chain above) stay unexecuted. The other lifted twins the plan listed (BCSH/CCM/gamma/LSC event and API bodies) are still referenced at run time (event callbacks registered in `tisp_stream_on`, gamma Set/Get, the documented `t40_csc_unsafe` and fallback switches) and stay until their chains are rewritten in C.
- Proof: host `make -C tests check` 124 passed / 0 failed; QEMU (debug kernel, slub_debug, one CPU) 5 load/stream/unload cycles, same result as the unchanged module (only the known core-mm slab noise, no driver cache hit), plus 5 reload cycles with snapshot 200 and 0 oopses. Camera (Eufy T40XP, image agg65n = agg65m with only this module changed): apitest all areas PASS 398 / FAIL 0 and t40dev 82 / 0 (as agg65m), 6 planned reload cycles clean, 0 oopses; night picture A/B in the same scene with module swaps agg65n -> agg65m -> agg65n: after the same reload both modules settle at the same AE step (again 2864, ISP dgain 1024, EV 5034) with luma 118.4 / 118.4, p1/p50/p99 16/103/255 and frame noise 1.02 identical, edge 3.43 / 3.41; the first agg65n sample (taken after the reload cycles) sat on a lower AE step (again 2416, ISP dgain 1109: luma 120.4, edge 3.83), the same module later reached the 2864 step, so that difference is the AE starting point, not the change. Day picture not compared (light off).

## 2026-11-01, lost branch delay-slot statements behind `return` in the lifted drivers (Lu-Fi/openimp#189, camera-run clean on T40 and T41)

## 2026-11-01, T41 lost tail calls / lost call arguments, scanner extended to T23, QEMU coverage triage (Lu-Fi/openimp#189, host and QEMU only, needs camera proof)

- Third and fourth lifter defect class: a MIPS tail call (`jr t9` + delay slot) left as an unmatched fragment, so the callee never runs (class `tailcall`), and a `system_reg_write` call that kept only its address argument because the value register was set in the delay slot (class `argc`). Both are in `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_check.py` (selftest, baseline, `--expect-clean TAG:FUNC:CLASS`); the scanner also resolves one-line `#ifdef REGTRACE_KERNEL_TREE_BUILD return X; #endif` stubs and `#if 0 /* comment */` now (about 20 T41 false "open" entries gone) and scans the T23 core with all classes (T31 has no lifted code: hand-written driver).
- Restored, vendor-compared in Ghidra (vendor41): T41 `tisp_msca_ir_init` (IR msca stride register 0x2001c), `tisp_msca_addr_ir_fifo_write` (0x20018), `system_reg_set_ae_trig` (0x19000 = 1), `tisp_tstp_reg_cfg` (register 0x60004 had no value, tail register 0x600a8), `tisp_lce_clr_ram_part_0` (the 1440 LCE RAM clear writes had no value 0, tail 0x501c0), `tisp_gib_write_reg` (value of the 0x50140 commit), `tisp_csc_yuv_dmo` (0xd000). `tisp_raw_rdma_fd_interrupt` counters are plain integers (typed pointers stepped them by 4). `tisp_bcsh_wdr_en` is a KERNEL_TREE stub for the WDR-sensor `tisp_init` leg: the lifted body indexed `ivdc_threshold_line` as the `bcsh_info` slot table and stored at address 335.
- Triage by runtime reach with QEMU translation-block coverage of the linked modules (T41 gc5603 and T23 sc2336: stream, snapshots, day/night, tuning calls) plus the shipped constants: 64 T41 functions are `dead` in the baseline (only reached through the tuning-event callbacks that `t41_safe_tuning_events=1` suppresses, the vendor-AE replacement, never-called lifted ioctl handlers, `tisp_msca_Shd_ctrl`, WDR-sensor paths). T23: `apical_isp_core_ops_s_ctrl` / `g_ctrl` are `shim` (the hand-written switch takes every id the caller lets through).

## 2026-11-01, lost branch delay-slot statements behind `return` in the lifted drivers (Lu-Fi/openimp#189, host-tested only, needs camera proof)

- Second lifter defect class: the lift put the MIPS `jr ra` in front of its delay slot and the instruction ended up as dead code behind the `return` (the same cause as the lost `state = 2` store of `vic_core_ops_init`). New scanner class `postret` in `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_check.py` (T40, T41, plus T21/T23/T30 lifted files, baseline-guarded, selftest case, `--expect-clean TAG:FUNC:CLASS`). It found 25 functions outside the hand-written early-return dispatchers.
- Restored in front of the return, vendor-compared in Ghidra: T40 `pop_buffer_fifo` (list poison word `0x200`), `tisp_ae_get_state`, `tisp_ae_main_mean_update`, `tisp_ae_sec_mean_update`, `tisp_ae_params_transmit` (`param[0x56]`), `tisp_awb_set_statis_localtion`, `tisp_dmsc_wdr_en`, `tisp_ae_ir_en_update`, `tisp_af_set_attr_refresh`, `tisp_awb_get_frz`, `tisp_defog_wdr_choose_para`, `tisp_mdns_wdr_en`, `tisp_sdns_wdr_en`, `tisp_wdr_get_lum_s`; T41 `api_ae_set_exprinfo_attr` (AE cache dirty byte), `tisp_msca_ch_curve_write`, `tisp_wdr_get_lum_s`, `tisp_ae_api_get_antiflick`, `tisp_awb_ev_update_Ywgt`; T30 `mem_write_u32`. Five more hits are hand-written dispatchers (baseline `shim`).
- Camera passes (wording: the functions are not instrumented, "reached" means the code path ran clean, not a measured register or picture effect): T40 (Eufy T40XP, image agg65m): apitest all areas PASS 398 / FAIL 0, t40dev 82 / 0, 6 planned module reload cycles, 0 oopses, snapshot 200; the AE exposure numbers for commanded states equal the run before. T41 (Cinnado T41NQ, image agg5y): apitest areas sys,fs,enc,osd,ivs,isp,su PASS 266 / FAIL 3 (AF statistics, AF metrics and LDC answer "no handler": AF off by default, no LDC), 13 FrameSource cycles, 7 planned reload cycles, 0 oopses. Not exercised on a camera: T41 `api_ae_set_exprinfo_attr` (no default case calls it), `tisp_msca_ch_curve_write` (non-default scaler curve) and T30 `mem_write_u32` (host only).

## 2026-11-01, T41 typed-pointer triage of the runtime-reachable lifted functions (Lu-Fi/openimp#189)

- 21 lifted T41 functions compared with the vendor ISP module 1.2.5 (headless Ghidra, new project `t41`, `decomp.sh vendor41`) and fixed by making the stepped register variables plain `uintptr_t` (byte arithmetic as the OEM): `tx_isp_suspend`, `tx_isp_resume`, `vic_frame_channel_streamon/streamoff/qbuf/set_fmt`, `vic_core_ops_init` (state slot offset x4, and the lift lost the delay-slot store `state = 2` behind the return), `tx_isp_vic_start` (DVP leg), `isp_frame_done_wakeup`, `tisp_s_wdr_en`, `isp_info_show`, `isp_mbus_to_bayer` (format code offsets x4), `ivdc_core_ops_init`, `tisp_lce_clr_ram_part_0`, `tx_isp_set_ae_algo_open`, `tx_isp_vin_suspend/resume_module`, `fs_resume_module`, `ispcore_suspend_module`, `tx_isp_switch_bin`, `tisp_switch_bin`. Camera proof (Cinnado T41NQ, image agg5y, together with the `postret` set): WDR on -> off toggle, SwitchBin day and night, 13 FrameSource disable/enable cycles, 7 planned module reload cycles, apitest PASS 266 / FAIL 3 (the 3 are the AF and LDC "no handler" answers), 0 oopses; the code paths were reached, not instrumented per function. Not exercised (host/compile only): the DVP leg of `tx_isp_vic_start` (MIPI camera), `tx_isp_suspend/resume`, `tx_isp_vin_suspend/resume_module`, `fs_resume_module`, `ispcore_suspend_module` (no power management on the board).
- Dead, not fixed: `frame_channel_unlocked_ioctl`, `tx_isp_unlocked_ioctl`, `__fill_tisp_buffer`, `isp_core_tunning_unlocked_ioctl_body`, `aisp_core_tunning_unlocked_ioctl` and `isp_core_tunning_default_ioctl` (with `tx_isp_core_ops_g_ctrl` behind it): the hand-written typed dispatchers return (`-ENOTTY` / `T41_UNRECOVERED_RET`) before the lifted body.
- Scanner: unconditional early returns cut the unreachable lifted tail, function names that only appear in comments of the hand-written headers no longer count as call roots, new class `var` (typed pointer plus a variable byte offset), `--expect-clean` regression list in `make check`.

## 2026-11-01, T40 typed-pointer triage of the runtime-reachable lifted functions (Lu-Fi/openimp#189)

- Eleven lifted T40 functions compared with the OEM (headless Ghidra) and fixed by making the stepped register variables plain `uintptr_t` (byte arithmetic as the OEM): `csi_core_ops_init` (state slot index x4, use count -4 instead of -1, csi device offset), `ispcore_slake_module`, `ispcore_activate_module` (sclk name table walk and strcmp stepped 4 bytes per character), `tx_isp_vin_slake_subdev`, `isp_core_tunning_open`, `fs_core_ops_ioctl`, `subdev_sensor_ops_ioctl`, `tx_isp_subdev_pipo`, `vic_mdma_irq_function1/2` (frame-done IRQ path) and the DVP leg of `tx_isp_vic_start`. Host/compile only; camera proof (Eufy T40XP, image agg65k): apitest all areas PASS 398 / FAIL 0, t40dev 83 / 0, 11 FrameSource disable/enable cycles, 6 planned reload cycles, about 25 minutes of streaming, 0 oopses; 12 of the 13 fixes were reached by those paths. Not reached: the 21-count wait of `__frame_channel_vb2_streamoff` (no buffer stayed pending) and the DVP leg of `tx_isp_vic_start` (no DVP camera).
- Scanner: a lifted function whose body starts with an unconditional `REGTRACE_KERNEL_TREE_BUILD` return stub (hand-written replacement, e.g. `tx_isp_vic_index_cal`, `tx_isp_subdev_init`) is no longer scanned behind the stub; new `--selftest` fixture in `make check`; new baseline status `layout` (confirmed but tied to stray OEM .bss anchors: `isp_vic_interrupt_service_routine`, `ispcore_core_ops_init`) and notes for the `shim` entries.

## 2026-11-01, lifted-driver typed-pointer scanner in `make check`; two lifted-code fixes (`claude/ptr-scan`)

- New `tests/lifted_ptr_scale_check.py` (+ `lifted_ptr_scale_baseline.txt`): flags the #186/#187 bug class in the lifted T40 and T41 drivers (a byte offset added to a `uintptr_t *` / `uint32_t *` register, which C scales by 4): stride walks (`step`), offsets from a typed base (`off`), loops whose typed counter never meets the integer bound (`loop`/`ctr`) and u16 fields read as unaligned words (`unal`). Only the code the Kbuild build compiles is scanned. 981 function/class entries are recorded as reviewed-open/unreachable; a new finding or a larger count fails `make check`.
- `__frame_channel_vb2_streamoff` (T40): the wait counter was a `uint32_t *` counting 0,4,..24 and never met the OEM bound 21, so the streamoff wait for pending buffers had no 2.1 s limit. It is an integer now (as the OEM). Needs camera proof.
- `Tzn_Msca_init_cfg` (T40): rebuilt from the OEM (state +10/+616 per channel, lock at +1856, final 0x1600c store); the lift wrote at x4 offsets and the lock 7424 bytes in, behind the 1860-byte state. Shared helper `t40_msca_init_state` in `tx_isp_t40_draw.h`, replayed against the OEM u16 pointer arithmetic in `tx_isp_t40_draw_test`. The main path already used the same data via `regtrace_t40_msca_state_seed` (unchanged output). Host/compile only, no camera run.

## 2026-11-01, T40 AWB bit-exact replay in `make check` on generated inputs; ct_detect special-light fix (`claude/issue-163-awb`)

- `tests/t40_bitexact_host.sh` now also replays `tisp_awb_long_alogrithm` (3 fuzz seeds) and `tisp_awb_ct_detect` (5 seeds, generated zone geometry and tables via `FUZZT`) against the translated OEM code on every call, with a synthetic tuning bank (zeros plus 88 generated bytes) and zero OEM `.data`: no vendor tuning bin, `.data` or tables are used or stored. It also mutates one character in each C function and requires the replay to detect it. About 35 s of the ~90 s `t40_bitexact_host` run. With `T40_TUNING_BIN` + `T40_OEM_DATA` (+ `T40_AWB_DUMPS`) the replays additionally run on the real data (local only).
- Found by it: the ct_detect hook of the harness had been dead since long_alogrithm became C (ct_detect was compared 0 times); fixed. The now-live comparison showed a real divergence: the special-light "few clusters" test read the cluster candidate counts (`out[27]`) instead of the cluster counts (`out[28]`, as the OEM does); fixed in `tx_isp_t40_awb_ctd.inc`.

## 2026-11-01, T40 bit-exactness and alignment proofs run in `make check` without fixtures (`claude/issue-163`)

- `tests/t40_bitexact_host.sh` (in `make -C tests check`) now always runs all seven C-chain comparisons against the literal OEM chains (ADR, DPC, LCE, SDNS, Defog, CDNS, BCSH; every register write and chain state) on two generated deterministic tuning bins (`fuzz:1`, `fuzz:2`), plus the ADR chain once with `-fsanitize=alignment` (no unaligned access). About 55 s. A real tuning bin (`T40_TUNING_BIN`) is added when set. The AWB long_alogrithm/ct_detect replay still needs the vendor tuning bin and OEM `.data` and skips without them. Host only.

## 2026-11-01, T40 privacy mask and ISP draw on the scaled output (`claude/issue-186-187`)

- **T40 IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetMask without effect on output channel 1, GetMask empty (#186):** the lifted `tisp_s_mscaler_mask_attr` stepped typed pointers (unit +96 bytes instead of +24, output channel +2464 instead of +616), so only unit 0 of output channel 0 was stored and the writes of the other units hit the memory behind the state; `tisp_g_mscaler_mask_attr` stored 4-byte words at x4 offsets (a write past its 288-byte buffer on the last units) and read the colour from the wrong state offset; `Tzn_Msca_Shd_mask` ORed 32-bit enable words, pulling the u16 position fields into the register 0x16014 value. All three rewritten from the OEM, plain-data helpers in `driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_draw.h`.
- **T40 IMP_ISP_SetDrawAttr never drew (#187):** the lifted `tisp_s_draw_attr` stored 8-byte words at doubled state offsets and its loop never ended its pointer comparison; `Tzn_Msca_Shd_line_chx` read the u16 unit fields as unaligned 32-bit words and always took the negative-slope branch of the line quantiser. Both rewritten from the OEM (window, range and line units). `ispcore_irq_sec_fd_work` now advances the shadow slot index first like the OEM (second sensor only).

  Host test `tests/tx_isp_t40_draw_test.c` (draw state, per-unit register sequence of window/range/line, mask set/get round trip over 3 channels x 4 units without a write behind the state, enable word from byte enables). Device proof (Eufy T40, image agg65i = next + this change, full OTA, avpu.ko md5 unchanged): `t40dev` W19_Mask PASS (cover in the 1280x720 output: region luma 160 -> 255, outside unchanged, disable restores; readback of channel 1 matches what was set, a cover on channel 0 alone does not show in channel 1) and ISP_Get/SetDrawAttr PASS (window top edge changes, removed again by the disabling set); apitest all areas 398 / FAIL 0, t40dev 0 FAIL; 6 planned reload cycles clean, snapshot 200, 0 oopses. Open: the drawn window reads Y/U/V 0/0/0 for every colour (alpha 0 draws nothing, alpha 7 black), see the follow-up issue.

## 2026-11-01, T40 pipeline-top block cleared at ISP init (`claude/issue-150`)

- **T40 ISP top block "main" not zero after the ISP open (#150):** the kernel-build `tisp_main_init` shim never ran the OEM `tisp_tstp_main_init` (fresh zeroed 154-byte block + `tisp_tstp_reg_cfg(0)`), so the 31 registers at ISP + 0x60000 kept their hardware defaults (0x28=0x200, 0x40=0x22, 0x90=2, 0xa8=2) while the vendor reads 0. The shim now frees the block of an earlier stream start and runs the OEM init right after the block inits; the sec block keeps its defaults like the vendor. Host check `tests/check_t40_tstp_init.sh` (`make -C tests check`, 120 passed, 0 failed).

  Device proof (Eufy T40, image agg65f = next 32f4ccfe + this fix, full OTA, avpu.ko md5 unchanged): register dump 0x13360000..0xc4 before (agg65e) 0x28=0x200, 0x40=0x22, 0x90=2, 0xa8=2, after all 31 registers 0 (0xb0..0xc4 are status counters on both stacks); snapshot 200, 0 oopses; apitest all areas with `OPENIMP_ISP_ALGO_FUNC=1` PASS 398 / FAIL 0; 6 reload cycles of `tx_isp_t40` clean (0 oopses, no leak beyond +16 kB slab) and the main block still 0 afterwards.

## 2026-11-01, T41 AF statistics block (`claude/issue-179`)

- **T41 GetAfStatistics / GetAFMetricesInfo (Lu-Fi/openimp#179, #76), behind `t41_af=1`:** the AF block was kept off (`tisp_af_init` wrote 0x1a000 = 0, no IRQ) because the lifted `tisp_af_set_hardware_param` had lost its register values. Re-translated from the OEM 1.2.5 module (Ghidra decompile, checked against the SDK libt41-firmware disassembly: unaligned u32 parameter loads, lhu fields, unsigned divisions): `tisp_af_set_hardware_param` (zone grid 0x1a004..0x1a034 once, 0x1a000 = 1, 0x1a038..0x1a0c8), the AF-done interrupt (status 0x40028 bit 6, ring index 0x1a0e0, OEM `tisp_af_get_statistics` 136-bit zone unpack with the CSWTCH.12 row table, frame tag 0x1a120), `tisp_af_alogrithm` (on the ordered tuning workqueue) and the two queries (0x08000031: 5400 bytes, 0x08000034: 12 bytes). Pure code in `driver/t41/tx_isp_t41_af.h`; `tests/tx_isp_t41_af_test.c` compares it with a literal transcription of the decompile (20000 random cases, 0 mismatches), `tests/check_t41_af_gate.sh` guards the gate. Default `t41_af=0`: unchanged behaviour (block off, bit 6 masked, both queries EPERM). Robustness over OEM: geometry check before arming, ring index masked, buffer cleared and written back after the read, workspace under a spinlock, disarm before free in `tisp_deinit`/`tisp_af_deinit`. Counters `af_live`, `af_irqs`, `af_runs`, `af_bad_index`.

  QEMU (ingenic-t41): armed load, apitest `t41isp` both PASS, default load both N/A (EPERM), 3 reload cycles, 0 oopses. Device proof (Cinnado T41NQ sc5336, image agg5v = next + this change, full OTA): snapshot 200, 0 oopses with the image default; reloaded with `t41_af=1`: apitest `t41isp` via libimp swap GetAfStatistics / GetAFMetricesInfo PASS (37/0); 30 min streaming with a stats+metric read every 10 s: 180/180 reads ret 0, 179 with all 225 zones non-zero (one startup-like transient), metric 1.0e6..5.0e6 following the scene, `af_irqs` = `af_runs` = 47079 (25/s), `af_bad_index` 0, isp-m0 IRQ rate 143 -> 170/s (+ the AF interrupt per frame), slab 6896 -> 6948 kB, snapshot 200, 0 oopses; 6 reload cycles with `t41_af=1` (each armed, 362 AF IRQs in 15 s, snapshot 200, 0 oopses); image default restored (`af_live` 0, 128 IRQ/s).

## 2026-11-01, T40 ISP draw state getter (`claude/t40-draw-get`)

- **T40 IMP_ISP_GetDrawAttr oops (#183):** the lifted `tisp_g_draw_attr` read the draw state of the first ISP output through a NULL pointer (the `&mscaler_s0` relocation was lost) and fell from the end of its loop back into the type dispatch, so every `IMP_ISP_GetDrawAttr` oopsed (BadVA 0x64), killed the calling thread and left the `tx_isp_t40` module reference held (the apitest area `t40dev` could not be unloaded afterwards). Rewritten from the OEM function (tx-isp-t40.ko `tisp_g_draw_attr`): state `mscaler_s0` for output 0 and `mscaler_s1` otherwise, 2 channels x 16 units, type 1 also copies the half word at +26.

  Device proof (Eufy T40, image agg65c = agg65b + this fix, full OTA): snapshot 200, 0 oopses; apitest all areas PASS 398 / FAIL 0 (was 397 / 3, the three were case expectations, #184); `t40dev` now runs through (rc 0, was an oops at ISP_Get/SetDrawAttr), the `tx_isp_t40` reference count is the same before and after the run (6 / 6, was +1 until reboot); 6 planned module reload cycles, 0 oopses.

## 2026-11-01, T40 clock teardown at module exit (`claude/issue-152`)

- **T40 ISP/CSI clocks gated at rmmod (#152, QEMU + device):** the open driver enabled `gate_isp`, `div_isp`, `gate_csi` and `gate_ahb0` at ISP/CSI activation but never released them (the default `t40_rmmod_skip_slake=1` skips `ispcore_slake_module`, and the tree-build `isp_subdev_release_clks` only dropped the handles), so every load added one enable and the ISP clock (CLKGR bit 23) stayed on after rmmod. The OEM gates them in `tx_isp_subdev_deinit` -> `isp_subdev_release_clks` (`clk_disable_unprepare` + `devm_clk_put`, core remove first). The open driver counts each successful `private_clk_prepare_enable` per clock name (the sensor modules' MCLK enable goes through the same exported function) and `tx_isp_t40_core_exit` releases what is still counted after the last child remove: the CSI remove still writes registers behind the ISP clocks, which is why gating inside the core remove (the OEM order, and what hung the camera with the slake) is avoided. The sensor modules stop their own MCLK in their remove, as on stock. QEMU (ingenic-t40): 22+ load/unload cycles with the streaming application running, clock counts back to `gate_isp=0 div_isp=0 gate_csi=0` and CLKGR bits 23/24 set after every rmmod (stock driver: counts stayed raised and grew by one per load), 0 oopses. Guard: `tests/check_t40_clk_teardown.sh`, loop script `tests/t40_qemu/clk_reload_cycle.sh`.

  Device proof (Eufy T40, image agg65d = next + this change, full OTA): snapshot 200, 0 oopses; apitest all areas plus t40dev PASS 398 / FAIL 0; 6 planned reload cycles of `tx_isp_t40` + sensors on a fresh boot: after every ISP rmmod `div_isp=0 gate_isp=0 gate_csi=0` in `/proc/jz/clock/clocks` and CLKGR 0x1F9F37C5 (ISP/CSI gates set), the sensor MCLK stopped by the sensor remove (CIM1 stop bit 27), counts back up on the reload, 0 oopses, streamer and snapshot 200 afterwards. Before the change the same camera showed `gate_isp=7 gate_csi=2 div_isp=8` after the earlier reload cycles (one leaked enable per load).

## 2026-11-01, T40 WDR-enable / LCE-tgain readers and writers (`claude/issue-182`)

- **T40 dn_params_refresh x3 + LCE tgain (#182):** `tisp_dmsc/ydns/ysp_dn_params_refresh` read the per-channel WDR enable and `tisp_lce_tgain_interp_strength` read the LCE tgain word through `&isp_memopt` (the module parameter), and the tail call of each refresh passed the isp_memopt value as the gain. The writers used other stand-ins (`&dmsc_wdr_en + 17176` words, `&lce_tgain + 17280` words, i.e. stray stores 68 KB past a 4-byte object). Now one OEM array per block with reader and writer on the same object: `t40_dmsc_wdr_en[2]`, `regtrace_ydns_wdr_flag[2]`, `regtrace_ysp_wdr_flags[2]`, `t40_lce_tgain_oem[2]`; the refresh tails pass the per-channel gain slot like the OEM. Host check `t40_hardcoded_anchor_check.py` extended (fails on the old code).

  Device proof (Eufy T40, image agg65b = agg65a + this fix): full OTA, snapshot 200, 0 oopses; apitest all areas plus t40dev PASS 397 / FAIL 3 (the same `tisp_node_par_*` failures as before, and the gamma-bypass module-control picture case); 6 of 6 module reload cycles clean on a fresh boot.

## 2026-11-01, T30 GetSensorRegister and ISP init interrupt window (QEMU only)

- **T30 `IMP_ISP_GetSensorRegister` (Lu-Fi/openimp#174, QEMU):** the lifted ioctl handler copied the 0x38-byte request over 4-byte stack locals and then walked the sub-device table through garbage pointers (NULL dereference). Both register ioctls now translate the request to the register structure and use the S_REGISTER / G_REGISTER events over the existing sub-device walk; the value is copied back. QEMU T30 (sc4236 model): chip-id registers read back the values the sensor driver reports, no oops (`tests/t30_qemu/sensor_register.c`).
- **T30 ISP init window (#176, QEMU):** in `apical_fw_init` the handler table is set before the event queue exists; a frame interrupt in that window of a few milliseconds wrote into a NULL ring buffer. The firmware IRQ dispatch is now gated by `tx_isp_t30_fw_ready` (set right before the hardware interrupts are enabled); early events are acknowledged and dropped. A test build with the window held open oopsed in the first ISP init without the gate and runs 6 of 6 init cycles clean with it (`tests/t30_qemu/isp_init_cycles.c`, `tests/check_t30_fw_irq_gate.sh`).
- Both issues stay open until a T30 camera confirms them (there is none in the lab).

## 2026-11-01, T30 multi-scaler stream-off (`claude/issue-175`, QEMU only)

- **T30 mscaler FIFO at stream-off (Lu-Fi/openimp#175, QEMU):** stream-off reset only the software FIFO head; the buffer addresses already pushed to the channel's hardware Y/UV push FIFO stayed there. The next stream-on wrote frames into them and reported them done; the new session's buffers sit at the same addresses, so the frame layer completed a buffer that was still linked in the mscaler fifo, user space re-queued it and the list broke (LIST_POISON2 oops in `tx_isp_t30_mscaler_configure_dma` from the mscaler ISR, about 1 in 7 two-channel cycles). Stream-off now does what OEM `mscaler_frame_channel_streamoff` does: unlink every queued entry and write 1 to the channel's push-FIFO clear registers (+0x19c Y, +0x1a0 UV); the free-buffer event unlinks every entry like OEM (no hardware clear there). Entries are unlinked with `list_del_init`. Regression `tests/t30_qemu/fs_two_channels.c` now checks after every teardown that both channels' push FIFOs are empty (deterministic): old module `STALE cycle 0 ch0 n=2 ch1 n=3`, and without the check the old module oopsed in cycle 8 (BadVA 0x00200200); fixed module 45 cycles `cycles done`, 0 oopses. No T30 camera: evidence level qemu.

## 2026-11-01, T40 custom AWB algorithm close (`claude/issue-173`)

- **T40 tisp_awb_algo_deinit_internal (#173, QEMU + device reload):** the lift dereferenced the bare address 16712 (kernel oops when a custom AWB algorithm is unregistered at `DisableSensor`); it now clears `tawb_custom_en[ch]`, the per-sensor array `tisp_awb_algo_init` fills, like the OEM. The AWB main process read a separate stand-in for that array and now reads the same one. New host check `tests/t40_hardcoded_anchor_check.py` (no local pointer initialised from a bare literal). QEMU T40: `customalgo_t40` ends with 0 oops (before: oops in the AWB close). Eufy T40 (agg65a): apitest 285 PASS / 0 FAIL, 6 reload cycles clean. The custom algorithm path itself stays off by default in OpenIMP (`OPENIMP_ISP_ALGO_FUNC=1`, #65).
- **Found, not changed:** `tisp_{dmsc,ydns,ysp}_dn_params_refresh` and `tisp_lce_tgain_interp_strength` read the per-channel WDR/tgain words through `&isp_memopt`; the OEM reads `dmsc_wdr_en`/`ydns_wdr_en`/`ysp_wdr_en`/`lce_tgain` (the writers use another stand-in offset), so writer and reader need one common fix (follow-up issue).
