## 2026-10-31: T40 user gamma curve reaches the LUT (`claude/issue-167-gamma`; QEMU T40, camera-proven in agg59)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetGammaAttr (0x08000025)** had no picture effect on T40 (issue #167, Eufy .184): production has no
  lifted gamma context (the soft-gamma writer owns the LUT), so `tisp_gamma_set_attr` returned -EAGAIN and
  `tisp_s_Gamma` (lifted as "return 0") hid it. Now the set path programs the 129-node LUT through the soft-gamma
  writer for mode 1 (sRGB), 2 (HDR) and 3 (user, nodes validated monotone <= 4095); mode 0 restores the tuning-bin
  curve. The choice is kept and re-applied at every writer re-init; GetGammaAttr reports the mode. `tisp_s_Gamma` now
  returns the real result. QEMU T40: set/get round trip for modes 0..3, no oops; QEMU has no picture model; on the Eufy T40XP (agg59) the soft-writer set path works: Y 156 against 64 at 250 ms.

## 2026-10-31: T40 review-fix, argument-audit and re-translation bundle (`next` 24c2c556, agg57)

Issue numbers refer to https://github.com/Lu-Fi/openimp/issues. Device proof: T40XP test camera, test module swapped in at run time, apitest all areas plus 28 cases PASS 262 / FAIL 8, 0 oopses, real sensor frames (the harness now sends the board information first; earlier runs without it saw only fallback frames). Items marked QEMU were not run on a camera.

- **Core in slot 0 (device):** the channel slot loops treat slot 0 as the core (ops dereference and the `s_stream`/`link_stream` loops fixed); the agg54 oops at AddSensor/MDNS is gone. Frame-channel mapping from `slot+112` instead of the OEM pipeline word (fix of agg55).
- **Ioctl dispatch (#145, device):** scaled request constants, link setup 0x80085409, tuning ioctl and the frame-channel QUERYBUF/QBUF/SET_BANKS handlers re-translated from the OEM; the lifted frame-channel dispatcher is removed.
- **Re-translated from the OEM:** AE zone geometry and scratch copies, second-sensor ISR frame-use countdown, custom-AE chain, LSC entry points, LCE tuning par get/set (device run clean; their individual effects are not measured separately).
- **GetSensorAttr (#147, device):** fps and size from the core copy of the sensor video block (3840x2160, fps 20).
- **SDNS/DPC refresh and `SetModule_Ratio(sinter)` (#154), `SetAwbAttr` live path (#156):** OEM offsets and stored CT/start gains; the critical hang is fixed.
- **`/dev/tisp` (#155, #157, QEMU):** one parameter block per open file, ioctls serialised, every single-line `isp_printf` has the arguments its format needs; nodes skip modules whose block was never allocated; only the reviewed setters (BLC, GIB) are enabled for parameter set (#139c, not behaviour-checked on a camera).
- **Anchor sweep (#140, QEMU/device run clean, effects unmeasured):** DMSC/MDNS/YDNS/YSP/SDNS, LCE/WDR/ADR/AF/second-sensor seeds, BCSH/CCM refresh, MJPEG range, HLDC and the VIC snapshot buffer use the OEM objects instead of `&sclk_name` offsets.
- **Optional blocking frame-channel DQBUF (#149 part 2):** module parameter `t40_dqbuf_wait_ms`, default 0 (off); not measured on a camera.
- **T41 (#134, QEMU/host):** sensor register access from user space under the quiesce mutex; failed `isp_malloc_buffer()` no longer leaves a bogus buffer.
- **Open at agg57, closed in agg59 (see the entry above):** `SetGammaAttr` (#167), stale frame after a pause (#168), `SetScalerLv` channel 1 (#169, test fix).

## 2026-10-29: T40 face AWB attribute round trip (`claude/issue-138`; QEMU T40)

- **T40: `IMP_ISP_Tuning_GetFaceAwb` returned stale stack words instead of the stored attribute** (control 0x8000014). The OEM `tisp_awb_face_set/get` are empty 8-byte stubs and its getter copies an uninitialised stack slot to user space, so SetFaceAwb stored nothing. The driver now keeps the last 0x18-byte attribute per ISP channel and Get returns it (zero before the first Set). The OEM leaves the value without effect on the AWB; so does this.

## 2026-10-29: T40 lifted ioctl handlers swept for uninitialised-pointer stores and lost arguments (`claude/issue-139`; host + QEMU T40, not device-tested)

- Issue #139. New static checks: `tools/t40_uninit_gcc.sh` (the recovered file once more with
  `-Wuninitialized -Wmaybe-uninitialized -O2`) and `tools/t40_uninit_scan.py` (stores through never-assigned local
  pointers or frame slots, copies to user space from never-written slots, calls that lost arguments, each with its
  call depth from the control/tuning ioctl handlers).
- Fixed from the OEM disassembly: tuning-node AE manual set (`tisp_set_ae_info`, ioctl 0x20007405, oopsed on every call)
  with `tisp_ae_{main,sec}_manual_set` (164-byte block by value; the sec lift ended in a store to 0x3868);
  Get/SetFrameDrop (first entry pointer lost, channel byte); SwitchBin (`tisp_core_switch_bin` stored through
  never-assigned pointers, `tisp_switch_bin` lost the attribute, the wrapper returned 1 on success; the pipeline-top
  re-init is skipped because the lifted `tisp_tstp_main_init` oopses on a second run); `/dev/tisp` commands
  0x20007402..05 (the lift tested the C return of the asm `__copy_user`, so every command failed) and its release
  (freed NULL instead of the 20 KB parameter block). QEMU T40: SwitchBin oopsed on next (NULL store), the tuning-node
  AE set never ran; with the fix all four cases pass, frames flow.
- Not changed (dead code, not reached from the streamer API, or kept apart from the unmerged OSD branch): see the
  issue. The class of lost arguments in per-frame fragments (about 380 call sites) is left for a follow-up.

## 2026-10-28 (4): T41 failed isp_malloc_buffer() no longer leaves a bogus buffer (`claude/issue-134`; host tests only, not device/QEMU-verified)

- Issue #134: with no ISP private pool (`ispmem` unset, e.g. QEMU 64M) `isp_malloc_buffer()` returns 0. t41_rawinject already
  handled that (stays disabled, logs once). Two other callers did not: the VIC snapraw buffer (`+424/+428` got
  `phys_to_virt(0)`) and `tisp_vic_mmap` (stored/remapped 0). Both now fail cleanly (-ENOMEM / buffer left clear).
  The reported kworker oops itself was not reproduced here; if it persists, send the oops text.

## 2026-10-28 (3): T32 S5 step 1: IQ bin section table, AE offset question resolved (`claude/t32-s4`; host)

- **IQ bin sections** (issue #97): `tx_isp_t32_iq.h` walks the section table as the vendor's tisp_bin_data_parse does
  (28 x { enable, length } at 0x14, enabled sections packed from 0xa0). AE = section 1 (bin + 0x104, 3288 bytes), AWB =
  section 2 (bin + 0xddc, 6740), LSC = section 7 (bin + 0x695c, 120): exactly the offsets the bit-exact matches used. The
  vendor copies each section to a fixed tparamsP offset (AE + 0x68, AWB + 0xd40, LSC + 0x68c4), so the 4-byte AE
  "offset question" was the per-section copy, not a second pointer (confirmed in a vendor QEMU RAM dump: the tparamsP
  pages hold bin content at two different shifts). Stream-on now checks the bin's table before starting the statistics.
  Host test with the cv8001 bin.

## 2026-10-28 (2): T32 S4 step 3b: bring-up exposure controller, not the vendor AE (`claude/t32-s4`; host, QEMU T32)

- **Bring-up controller, not the vendor AE/AWB** (issue #97), module parameter `bringup_ae` (default 0), target
  `bringup_target`: scaffolding to close a loop through the statistics until the vendor AE flow is reimplemented. On each
  AE histogram interrupt a work item takes the mean bin of the histogram written last and steps the integration time
  proportionally (x0.25..x4 per step, 1 .. VTS - 4 lines, gain unchanged) through the sensor EXPO event 0x02000017. No
  AWB, no tuning. Host test: histogram decoding and step limits (`tx_isp_t32_stats_test`). QEMU: 13 histogram
  interrupts, 5 steps accepted by the sensor, 3 channels 8/8 frames, no oops. Off by default, nothing changes without it.

## 2026-10-27 (10): T40 tisp-event-0: ADR knee search + per-event profile (`claude/issue-133b`; host + QEMU T40, not yet on the device)

- **Device status after (8)** (issue #133): on the Eufy T40 the thread went from ~35 to ~27-29 ms CPU per frame, less
  than the QEMU gain. QEMU only runs event 14 (AWB) there; the Eufy also runs ADR (event 2), defog (4) and LCE (0x10)
  every frame, which the earlier profile had not measured with the device tuning.
- **ADR knee search** (`tx_isp_t40_adr.h`): `subsection_map` scanned all 512 entries of the cumulative histogram for the
  nearest value, 6 times per block, 24 blocks per frame (~80 % of the ADR event). The histogram is normally
  non-decreasing: `t40_adr_fpga` checks that once per frame (and that no distance can overflow) and then a binary search
  plus the run of equal values gives the same count and index sum; the gamma knot lookups (`t40_adr_lookup`) use a
  binary search the same way. Unsorted or out-of-range tables take the old scan. Bit-exact: `c_chains_host adr` against
  the literal OEM chain (Eufy bin + 6 random bins, 0 differences) and 7.6 M random sorted/unsorted tables with ties and
  distance-10000 edges (fast vs scan: 0 differences). Host (Eufy bin): ADR event 163 -> 85 us.
- **Profile build** (`TX_ISP_LOAD_STATS=y` only, off by default): work slots 70..72 time the translated AWB process, its
  EV follow-up and its CT follow-up inside event 14; `t40_ls_reg_w`/`t40_ls_reg_r` count the register accesses of the
  event thread per event and `t40_ls_reg_cyc` the CP0 Count cycles inside `system_reg_read/write`; reading `t40_ls_bench`
  times readl / `system_reg_read` / flight-recorder mark / wmb (reads ISP reg 0x20 only). QEMU T40: event 14 =
  10.4 ms, of which the translated AWB process 10.37 ms, EV/CT follow-ups 25/13 us, ~17 register writes; ADR/defog/LCE
  do not run in QEMU. Host register writes per event: ADR 194, defog 184, LCE 132.

## 2026-10-28: T32 S4 step 3: AE/AWB statistics engines from the IQ bin (`claude/t32-s4`; host, QEMU T32)

- **AE/AWB statistics** (issue #97): the engines are initialised as the vendor does (reverse engineered tisp_ae_malloc_cfg,
  set_hardware_param, roi_point; tisp_awb_malloc_cfg, set_hardware_param, set_regional_threshold, set_lum_th_freq):
  zone grid, centre ROI, EV dependent thresholds, statistics buffers. Host test `tx_isp_t32_stats_test`: 24 AE and 26 AWB
  writes identical to the vendor's (with `T32_IQ_BIN`). QEMU: the writes as the vendor's apart from the buffer addresses,
  statistics interrupts arrive, 3 channels 8/8 frames over 2 load cycles; the engines are switched off before the buffers
  are freed. No AE/AWB control loop yet.

## 2026-10-27 (12): T41 defog ratio set no longer returns the OEM's leftover v0 as an error (`claude/issue-135`; QEMU T41, .34)

- **Defog ratio** (issue #135): `t41_dfg_ratio_apply` passed the return value of the translated `tisp_s_defog_ratio` through
  as a status. That routine leaves whatever was in v0 (seen as -2111087330); the ratio is applied. Now only a trapped access
  in the translated code is a failure. `SetModule_Ratio` with the defog entry returns 0 (QEMU T41 and the T41 camera).
- The "bypass-defog leaves the key unchanged" part is not a driver defect: the sc5336 calibration keeps the defog block in
  bypass, so setting the bit changes nothing; clearing it (`unbypass-defog`, apitest) runs the defog chain (frames counted,
  picture changes: mean abs diff 1.78 against 0.5 drift).

## 2026-10-27 (11): T32 S4 step 2: stream-on register list = LSC from the IQ bin (`claude/t32-s4`; host, QEMU T32)

- **LSC** (issue #97): the stream-on list now carries the lens shading block as the vendor's does, computed from the IQ bin
  (`tx_isp_t32_lsc.h`; reverse engineered flow of tisp_lsc_init / calc_para_refresh / interp_by_tgain / write_all_reg):
  grid and control registers 0x5040..0x5070, the 1152-cell mesh through 0x32020/0x32024, block id to 0x5000. Built from
  the cv8001 PRJ007 bin it equals the vendor's list word for word (3479/3479, host test `tx_isp_t32_lsc_test` with
  `T32_IQ_BIN`/`T32_RCFG_REF`). Stage 1: no blend between colour temperature tables (nearer table), CT = module
  parameter `lsc_ct`, gain 0. QEMU: list built and run, frames unchanged (the model does not execute T32 lists).

## 2026-10-27 (10): T32 S4 step 1: MSCA polyphase filter curves (`claude/t32-s4`; host, QEMU T32)

- **MSCA filter curves** (issue #97): each channel start loads the curves of all three channels through the shadow port, as
  the vendor tisp does (ratio out/in Q14, 25 kernel samples, 8 phases x 6 taps normalised to 2048, 13 words per direction).
  Own computed kernel table (`tools/t32_msca_sinc_tab.py`); 6 of 75 coefficients differ from the vendor's by 1/2048. The
  `+0x28` filter-select pulse is written as the vendor does. Host test `tx_isp_t32_msca_curve_test` (vendor coefficients
  from our QEMU traces). QEMU: 3 channels 8/8 frames, MSCA writes as the vendor run v1 apart from the curve LSBs.

## 2026-10-27 (8): T40 vendor AWB: colour-temperature detector in C (`claude/issue-133`; host + QEMU T40, not yet on the device)

- **tisp-event-0 CPU** (issue #133): the event thread of the Eufy T40 used ~30 ms CPU per frame (~60 % of one core at
  20 fps; system CPU ~35 %). Host profile of the translated OEM AWB process (`t40_vawb`, replay of a device dump):
  ~0.7 M OEM instructions per frame, every OEM load/store a guarded call (~15 MIPS instructions each, the cross-built
  ct_detect is 4.7x the OEM code size); `tisp_awb_ct_detect` with its helpers is ~65 % of it, `long_alogrithm` ~25 %.
  ADR/defog/LCE (plain C) cost a fraction of that. The vendor AWB code runs the same work natively, every frame.
- The translated chain now calls the driver's C translation of `tisp_awb_ct_detect` (`tx_isp_t40_awb_ctd.inc`) in its
  place (`tools/t40_vawb/gen.sh`: VAE_STOP, adapter `vawb_s_tisp_awb_ct_detect`). Bit-exact: `tests/t40_vawb_host.sh`
  with `CTD_REF=f2c180f7` runs both on every call (2 device dumps, 30 x 1000 calls with random zone statistics and
  tuning switches: 0 differences); the replay of both device dumps stays identical to the device result.
  Host: 660 -> 270 us per AWB process. QEMU T40 (Eufy image, streaming application running, model AWB statistics): tisp-event-0 27.3 -> 8.0 ms
  CPU per AWB frame (-71 %), same CT (4996), no fault, no oops. Module text -65 KB. The periodic AWBCTD diag lines now
  need `enable_isp_3a_diag`.
- tests: `t40_vawb_host.sh` and `t40_vct_host.sh` build again (BCSH symbols since the C BCSH chain); `REPS`/`CONSEC`
  timing in the replay. Camera proof open (T40 test flash: tisp-event-0 stime per frame before/after).

## 2026-10-27 (9): T41 RAW input: RDMA block at the OEM addresses (`claude/isp-rawinput-rdmafix`; host build, not on the device)

- **`tisp_ddr_to_isp_raw`** (RAW injection path, opt-in): the lift declared the RDMA page base as `uintptr_t *`, so every
  `s2 + off` scaled the offset by 4 (0x21008/0x2100c/0x21010/0x21024 went to 0x24020/0x24030/0x24040/0x24090), and two
  writes lost their value through a one-argument cast. Now the OEM addresses and values (vendor tx-isp-t41.ko 0x6b300..0x6b468).
  Candidate cause for "RDMA done" (irq 69) never firing; device proof open (RAW inject on a T41).

## 2026-10-27 (8): T41 api_ae_dn_par_refresh body repaired (`claude/t41-nightae1025`; host)

- **T41 `api_ae_dn_par_refresh`**: the lifted body stored the "parameters changed" byte (+1281) through the channel number instead of
  `ae_cache[channel]` (a write to a near-NULL address). Repaired as the stock function (AE statistics setup, deflicker table, flag in the
  channel's AE cache). Its only caller is the lifted `tisp_day_or_night_event`; the open day/night bank worker does not run it (an opt-in
  step was tried on .32 on 2026-10-25: no change of the converged night exposure, 15 guarded divisions by zero in the translated
  `tisp_ae_par_update_trig`, so it was not kept). No runtime change.

## 2026-10-27 (7): T21/T30 MoveState through the SYSTEM_TAB block (`claude/movestate-t21-t30`; host, QEMU T21 + T30)

- **T30 SYSTEM_TAB (0x800002c)**: the lifted `apical_isp_stab_s_attr` read the enable bytes from an uninitialised local and
  the values 0x10 bytes off, and the tuning get had no arm for the control (-1). New `tx_isp_t30_stab.h`: item table of the
  60-byte system table, an item is copied when its enable byte (0x3c + item) is set, as the T20; the get returns the table.
  Host test `t30_stab_host_test`. QEMU T30 with the OpenIMP MoveState: exposure ceiling 1916 -> 1149 -> 1916 (image ko: get -1).
- **T21 MoveState block**: the motion block of the vendor libimp logic (enable bytes 71/99/100/101) used to read as a sinter
  block ([70] set: manual sinter at strength 1). It is now recognised first and applies only its exposure cap (u16 [26] with
  [82], 3/5 of the ceiling in use; the Disable block puts the captured ceiling back); the Apical sinter/temper/sharpening
  bytes have no T21 equivalent and are not applied (beyond stock, the stock driver ignores the control). Host test
  `t21_it_block_host_test` extended. QEMU T21: ceiling 2246 -> 1347 -> 2246, frames flow, no oops. Camera proof open (T21).

## 2026-10-27 (4): T41 real ISP bypass refused until the VIC path works (`claude/issue-125`; QEMU T41)

- **IMP_ISP_SetISPBypass(1)** (issue #125): with OpenIMP `claude/issue-125` the request now reaches the driver in
  the vendor order (before AddSensor) and routes frame channel 0 to the VIC frame-channel ops (input 4, core+0x2a4).
  The open VIC frame-channel path is not functional (`vic_frame_channel_set_fmt` answers -EINVAL, no VIC device in
  `dump_vsd`), so FrameSource_EnableChn failed and no frame flowed (QEMU T41). Until the VIC MDMA capture path works
  the driver refuses value != 0 with -EOPNOTSUPP (libimp -4095) without touching the routing; value 0 (ISP
  processing, the normal state) stays served. QEMU T41: bypass 1 -4095 with frames flowing, bypass 0 / apitest
  `ispbypass` PASS, no oops.

## 2026-10-27 (8): T23/T31 ISR enable parameter cannot re-enable the core interrupts after a disable (`claude/issue-108`; host)

- **t23_isr_enable / t31_isr_enable** (issue #108): the run-time parameter wrote register 0xb0 after an unlocked
  "core interrupts on?" check (T23: the enabled flag, T31: `readl(0xb0) != 0`), so a write racing with the stream
  disable could switch the sources back on after the driver had cleared 0xb0. The stream path and the parameter now
  decide under one spinlock per SoC with a shared state helper (`driver/common/tx_isp_isr_enable.h`); a value
  written while the interrupts are off is applied at the next enable. Not the cause of the reboot in #108 (nobody
  writes the parameter at run time on the fleet), found while checking e72efa2a for races. New host test
  `tx_isp_isr_enable_test` (both orders of disable vs. parameter write); T23/T31 build clean.
## 2026-10-27 (6): T40 BCSH user CSC tables and user CCM whole (`claude/issue-83`; host, not yet on the device)

- **CSC gamut 4 (user tables) and the BCSH user CCM** took only the first word of each table, like the OEM
  (`tisp_bcsh_params_refresh` copies 4 bytes): the rest of the RGB->YUV matrix, its inverse, the clips and the three
  CT matrices stayed from the previous gamut or the bank (issue #83). New module parameter `bcsh_user_full` (default 1,
  beyond stock) copies the whole tables into both WDR halves; `bcsh_user_full=0` keeps the OEM behaviour.
  c_chains_host bcsh: 31 bins bit-exact against the literal OEM chain with 0, new check for 1 (whole tables, register
  writes change; fails with the old copy). Camera proof open (T40 test flash).

## 2026-10-27 (7): T40 tisp event thread sleeps until an event is queued (`claude/issue-81`; QEMU T40)

- **regtrace_tisp_event_thread** (issue #81) polled: `tisp_event_process` waited on a completion with a 20 ms timeout
  (uninterruptible, the thread sat in D state and counted into the load average) and the loop retried an empty
  list after `msleep(1)`. The channel now has a wait queue: `tisp_event_push` wakes it after queueing the node
  (also from the ISR), the thread waits interruptibly for "list not empty or kthread_should_stop()" with a 1 s
  safety timeout, and the 1 ms retry is gone. QEMU T40 (Eufy image, streaming application running, no ISP frames in the model):
  50 -> 1 wake-ups per second of tisp-event-0, state D -> S, no oops. The dispatch path with frames (event wake-up
  latency, AE/AWB per frame) still needs the .184.

## 2026-10-27 (6): T40 remaining .bss strays replaced by the OEM objects (`claude/issue-86`; host)

- **%hi(.bss+N) inline asm** (issue #86): the last 36 lines of lifted code that addressed fixed offsets of the OEM
  .bss layout (in this module they hit whatever lies there) now use the objects the OEM tx-isp-t40.ko resolves them
  to (its symbol table, cross-checked: .bss+17424 = defog_main_tool_para as in 6e9b4983):
  `sensor_alloc_analog_gain_short` (g_ispcore, the lift read .bss+0), `tisp_awb_get_mode` (pst{Main,Sec}AwbOri
  pointer fields -> tisp_wb_attr_{main,sec}), `tisp_wdr_sec_long_ev_update` (wdr_ev_changed[1], ev_wdr_l[2..3];
  ev_wdr_l is now the OEM 16 bytes, it was read at +8 out of bounds), `tisp_defog_get_data` (sensor 1: the three
  21-bit tables behind defog_seco_soft_para; the lift jumped to a fixed address), `tisp_adr_ev_update`
  (adr_ev_changed[arg3], adr_ev_top[arg3]), `adr_remove` (ADR state words and adr_version_num; the lift wrote the
  .data start), `tisp_sdns_params_refresh` (copies the bank into the block behind main_sdns/sec_sdns; the lift
  copied the other way into the bank), `tisp_clm_prepare_write_lut` (clm_wlut_times[arg1]++), `tisp_gsm_main_init`
  (pstMainGsmOri = stMainGsmInterOri, main_gsm_slock_hist). `tisp_af_get_statistics` is inert: the C AF unpacks the
  statistics, the lifted body wrote the .bss start and cleared 4 KB from `&isp_memopt`. Missing objects are added
  with the OEM sizes. tests/t40/c_chains_host (dpc, lce, sdns, adr, defog, cdns, bcsh; 31 bins) identical before and
  after: 0 differences. Own commits (to revert one by one): `sensor_alloc_analog_gain{,_short}` load the
  sensor attr like the OEM (content of g_ispcore, then the attr pointer), `tisp_gsm_sec_init` like the OEM,
  `tisp_af_main_init` registers the AF IRQ handler instead of calling it once.

## 2026-10-27 (7): T23 ISP OSD: odd picture width refused (`claude/t23-osd-oddwidth`; host test, QEMU T23)

- **SetOSDBlock** (0x08000182) with enable = 1 and an odd picture width now returns `-EINVAL` before anything is
  compared or stored (beyond stock: the stock handler takes it). Reason: an odd width written into the MSCA stops the
  T23 ISP. QEMU T23 (sc2336, `isp_osd_devtest` phase p5oddw): next without the guard stored the 127-pixel block,
  showed it and both frame channels stopped (devtest stuck, "2 of 2 buffers still queued" at stream off); with the
  guard the direct SetOSDBlock fails, the region show returns -1, block 2 stays disabled, ch1 20.4 fps before and after.
  Host test: `tests/tx_isp_t23_tuning_ext_test.c` (refused, mscaler byte-identical); `make -C tests check` 104 passed.

## 2026-10-27 (6): T41 AE tuning API: statistics, histThresh, FaceAe, AeExpList (`claude/issue-90`; host test, QEMU T41, .34 by runtime swap)

- **GetAeStatistics** (0x08000022) now answers like the stock `tisp_g_ae_statis_attr` from what the vendor AE computed for the
  last frame: `tisp_ae_hist_last` (256 bins x4, bin 0 = width x height minus the rest; the 5-bin histogram over the
  histThresh limits, scaled to 65535) and the 15x15 zone Y of the AE state (+7648, `tisp_ae_weight_mean`). Before: raw
  hardware bins, fixed fifths for the 5-bin histogram and the frame mean in every zone. Without the vendor AE
  (`t41_vendor_ae=0`) the old path remains.
- **SetStatisConfig histThresh**: an enabled AE part sets the four limits of the vendor AE's 5-bin histogram (stock
  `api_ae_set_hist_bin`); before they were only stored in the record.
- **Set/GetFaceAe** (0x08000035, 24 bytes): stock `tisp_ae_sepc_area` stores (enable + update flag, window clamped to
  [20, w-20] x [10, h-10] through `tisp_ae_roi_point`, target 10..200); GET returns the last accepted request (stock
  returned the first word from the face-history ring and never filled the enable). Beyond stock: enable > 1 and an enabled
  empty window are refused.
- **Face AE luma source**: the ROI-window statistics 0x19060..0x19074 are copied to AE state +7448..+7468 every frame (stock:
  ISP IRQ 61, `tisp_ae_ai_interrupt_static`; the open dispatcher skips IRQ 61 and the recovered body was a no-op). Without
  them the face AE metered 0 (division guard faults in `fix_point_div_32`, no effect). Parameter `t41_ae_roi_stats` (default 1).
- **Set/GetAeExpList** (0x08000027, 324 bytes): stock `tisp_ae_api_set/get_exp_list` stores (params +1732 mode, +580 list,
  state flags, update flag); GET returns the stored list (stock took columns 2/3 from unrelated AE cache words). Mode > 1 refused.
- Host test `tests/tx_isp_t41_tuning_ctl_test` (reply packing, face clamps/stores, list layout). .34 (sc5336, OpenIMP
  `claude/issue-90`): 256-bin sum 4665600 = 2880 x 1620, 5-bin split of bins 1..4 equal to the 256 bins over 13/64/144/192 and
  after 32/64/96/128 (bin 4 5342 -> 14239), zones 1..226; face window centre target 40 -> frame Y 88.4, target 180 -> 105.8
  (base 98.2, 9 s each; the vendor AE's face path converges slowly), back after off; list mode on/off with the calibration's
  own list: frames flow, Y 100.5 -> 97.0 -> 96.9; 0 guard faults, 0 oops. QEMU T41: same cases, 3 reloads of the test ko clean.

## 2026-10-27 (5): T23 AutoZoom Get and crop off (`claude/rc3-gaps2`; QEMU T23, .30 by runtime swap)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_GetAutoZoom** returned -1 (the driver answered cid 0x80000e8 Get with -EINVAL): now served from the channel record of the MSCA (scaler enable and
  output, crop enable and window; same fields the Set writes, channel above 2 refused).
- **Crop off** reset the window to the channel's own output size, which as an input window shows the top-left corner of the sensor picture (a SetAutoZoom with crop
  off after a zoom left the corner on ch1); now the whole sensor picture, and a disabled scaler keeps the channel size. Beyond stock. Host test updated.
  Camera .30: zoom shows the centre half (layout distance 0.03), crop off shows the whole picture again (0.000).

## 2026-10-27 (5): T23 ISP-drawn OSD pictures appear in the picture (`claude/issue-127`; QEMU T23, .30)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetOsdRgnAttr / ShowOsdRgn** (ISP OSD, applications using the IMP API) drew nothing on the T23 (issue #127):
  SetOSDAttr / SetOSDBlock (0x08000181/182) were stored in the mscaler object, but nothing wrote them into the
  MSCA. The core frame-done interrupt now does what stock `ispcore_irq_thread_handle -> tisp_msca_Shd_ctrl ->
  tisp_msca_set_omi_api -> tisp_msca_api_set_osd` does: (value, register) pairs on the MSCA shadow port 0xe004
  inside 0xe000 = 0x101 / 0x01010102 (order 0x754; per picture 0x760 + 16n position, size, address, stride;
  enable 0x048; format 0x750), kick 0xe100 = (pairs - 1) << 4 | 1 (`tx_isp_t23_osd.h`, README "ISP OSD").
  Emulator audit against the stock libt23-firmware 1.3.0 code (`audit/osd_commit_emu.py`, 300 random states):
  identical writes except the enable word, which here follows the stored pictures (stock only ORs bits in, so a
  hidden picture kept its bit: beyond stock). Busy shadow port or running curve loader: retried next frame;
  channel 0 start resends the shown pictures; `isp_osd=0` switches the writes off; counters `osd_commits`,
  `osd_busy_skips`. Host test `tests/tx_isp_t23_osd_test.c`. QEMU T23: apitest isposd FAIL -> PASS (black
  picture Y 180 -> 16 exactly in its 128x64 cells), ISP OSD devtest 7 phases, no oops, rmmod clean. Cam .30
  (sc1a4t, runtime ko swap): isposd Y 111.3 -> 234.0 (stock agg47 ko: 110.7 -> 110.7), hide back to 111.2,
  destroy, frames flow; devtest: picture A/D/B/C at their positions in the right colours (BGRA bytes, order 0),
  move, second region, hide, destroy all as requested, ch1 15.0 fps throughout, 8 commits, 0 busy skips, no oops.
  Draw blocks (SetDrawBlock) and mask blocks are still stored only.

## 2026-10-27 (5): T40 BCSH day/night host test builds again (`claude/issue-87`; host)

- **tests/t40/bcsh_dn_host** (issue #87) included the removed `tx_isp_t40_bcsh_lit.inc`; it now runs the C chain
  (`driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_bcsh.h`) in the order of `regtrace_t40_refresh_daynight_pipeline` (init, brightness,
  contrast, hue, saturation; night with saturation 0) and checks the BCSH registers after three day -> night -> day
  cycles against the first day. New guards against a vacuous pass: the day registers must be written and the night
  bank must change them. Part of `make -C tests check` (target `t40_bcsh_dn_host`); the bin comes from
  `T40_TUNING_BIN`, else the pinned SDK submodule `sensor-iq/t40/gc2053.bin`, else SKIP. PASS with the SDK gc2053,
  gc4653, imx327, gc5603 and the Eufy sc830ai bins (EV 286830 and 3375).

## 2026-10-27 (4): T20 manual integration time takes effect (`claude/issue-128`; QEMU T20, .107)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetIntegrationTime(MANUAL)** and **SetExpr(MANUAL)** had no exposure effect on the T20 (issue
  #128): the request was right (system tab: `global_manual_integration_time` + `global_integration_time`, read back
  by GetIntegrationTime), but the compact AE (`tx_isp_t20_simple_ae_apply`, sensor_drv.c) ignored the manual flag,
  wrote its own time to the sensor and overwrote the table every frame (GetExpr after a 30-line set: 2022). Like the
  OEM 3.12.0 `cmos_alloc_integration_time`, the AE now takes the table time while the flag is set (clamped to the
  sensor window and the AE ceiling) and only allocates the gain; the total exposure is kept inside
  [time, time + reachable gain] so the loop cannot wind up (helpers in tx_isp_t20_ae_max.h, host test extended).
  Cam .107 (night scene, runtime ko swap), gains pinned to 1: stock ko Y 51.6 -> 51.6 for 30 -> 240 lines, GetExpr
  2022; test ko Y 5.6 -> 37.3 (apitest inttime PASS), GetIT/GetExpr read 30/240; gain freed: AE brings Y back to
  ~87 at 240 lines; AUTO again: Y 87.9, AE time 1028. QEMU: GetExpr after the manual set 2022 -> held; no oops.
  T10 (own AE) unchanged.

## 2026-10-27 (3): T20 GetGamma reports the curve in use (`claude/issue-121`; QEMU T20, .107)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_GetGamma (0x800002b)** returned 129 zeros on the T20 (issue #121): the getter read the gamma LUT
  back from the registers at 0x10400, which read as zeros on the hardware (QEMU returns the values). It now
  reports the linear gamma calibration table (`CALIBRATION_GAMMA_LINEAR`, `COMMAND_GET`), which SetGamma writes
  and the LUT is programmed from; the register read stays as fallback. Cam .107 (jxf23, runtime ko swap): 0 -> 128
  of 129 points non-zero (g[64]=3101); writing the read curve back leaves the picture unchanged (mean Y 72.9 for
  8 s before and after), SetGamma x^0.3 vs linear Y 131.6 vs 42.3. apitest's "picture restored" check missed its
  7.6 s window there because AE was still settling in the dark scene (Y ~48-75), not because of the curve.

## 2026-10-27 (1): T31 WDR output mode getter on a linear sensor (`claude/issue-118`; QEMU T31, .26)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_GetWdr_OutputMode (0x80000ea)** returned -1 when word 0 of the WDR tool control block (parameter
  0x431) is not 8/1/2 (issue #118), e.g. on a linear sc2336 after `IMP_ISP_WDR_ENABLE(0)` and in the QEMU image.
  Stock `tisp_get_wdr_output_mode` (1.1.6 tx-isp-t31.ko 0x662a8) only logs such a word and returns 0; the getter
  now reports the default mode 0 (FUS_FRAME) with 0. Cam .26: `wdr` then `wdrout` gave Get -1 before, 0 (PASS)
  after; QEMU T31 standalone -1 -> 0; frames flow, no oops.

## 2026-10-27 (2): T31/T23 ISP custom mode without a custom bin (`claude/issue-117`; QEMU T31/T23, .26/.30)

- **T31 IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetISPCustomMode(DISABLE)** returned -1 when no `/etc/sensor/<sensor>-cust-t31.bin` is loaded
  (issue #117). Stock `tisp_cust_mode_s_ctrl` (1.1.6 tx-isp-t31.ko 0x6263c) does the same for every mode ("make sure
  xxx-cust-t31.bin exist"); beyond stock, DISABLE while the custom bank is not active is now a no-op returning 0
  (no bank switch, no day/night re-stage), so a streamer writing back the value it read does not fail. ENABLE without
  a custom bank still fails (-1) without touching the ISP.
- **T23 IMP_ISP_Tuning_GetISPCustomMode** returned 0xffffffff without a custom bank and writing it back failed
  (issue #122): the getter now reports the custom state (0 = DISABLE until a custom bank is active), DISABLE without
  a bank is a no-op returning 0 like on T31.
- QEMU T31 (sc2336) and T23 (sc2336), apitest `custom`: Set of the value just read -1 -> 0 (T31), Get 4294967295 -> 0
  and Set -1 -> 0 (T23); frames flow afterwards, no oops. Cam .26 (T31 sc2336) and .30 (T23 sc1a4t), runtime
  ko swap: the same before/after, apitest `custom` PASS 26 / FAIL 0.

## 2026-10-26 (11): T21 integration time and WaitFrame (`claude/issue-110`, issue Lu-Fi/openimp#110)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_GetIntegrationTime on T21** read mode 0 / it 0 / max 0: the stock dispatcher leaves the
  0x0800002c block untouched on get, and the driver hook filled only the sinter bytes. The get now also fills the
  bytes the legacy getter reads: [0x03] manual, u16 [0x18] the integration time in use (live AE value, or the fixed
  one), u16 [0x1a] the AE exposure ceiling (sensor maximum or the RANGE cap).
- **SetIntegrationTime MANUAL/AUTO on T21** were no-ops (only RANGE worked): MANUAL now fixes the exposure the way
  the stock SetExpr MANUAL path does (`tisp_ae_manual_set`: lines at `tisp_ae_ctrls` 0x0c, manual-IT flag 0x34;
  the AE then only moves the gain), clamped to the sensor limits; AUTO clears the flag. Works with the stock
  T21 1.0.33 libimp as well as OpenIMP (same block). Beyond vendor (stock oem-t21 ignores the block).
- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_WaitFrame on T21** returned 0 with the frame count stuck at 0: tuning control 0x08000162 was
  not handled (stock acknowledges it without writing). Added a 64-bit frame-done count ticked by the VIC
  frame-done interrupt; the call waits for the next frame (timeout in ms) and returns the count in words 2-3,
  also on a timeout (-ETIMEDOUT), as the T20 SDK. Host test `t21_it_block_host_test`.

## 2026-10-26 (10): T31 sensor register access (`claude/issue-113`, issue Lu-Fi/openimp#113)

- **IMP_ISP_Get/SetSensorRegister on T31** did not reach the sensor: the attached sensor subdev runs with the
  driver's delegating `sensor_subdev_ops`, whose core ops had no `g_register`/`s_register`, so the
  TX_ISP_EVENT_SENSOR_G/S_REGISTER handler got -ENOIOCTLCMD, the ioctl (0xc0385650 / 0x8038564f) still returned 0,
  reads gave back the caller's uninitialised buffer (sc2336 chip id 0x0404 instead of 0xcb3a) and writes were
  dropped. Both now delegate to the sensor driver's own handlers under `sensor_ops_call_mutex`.
## 2026-10-26 (11): T41 default bin path ioctl (`claude/issue-116`; QEMU T41, .34)

- **IMP_ISP_SetDefaultBinPath (0xc004542a)** is served (issue #116; it fell through to the disabled recovered
  dispatch and answered -ENOTTY, so OpenIMP's AddSensor failed after SetDefaultBinPath). Like stock
  `tx_isp_set_default_bin_path` the 132-byte record `{vinum, path[128]}` goes to core+736+vinum*132, the path
  `ispcore_core_ops_init` hands to `tisp_init`, which loads it instead of `/etc/sensor/<sensor>-t41.bin`. As on
  stock, vinum >= 2 is logged and answered 0 (the T41 1.2.5 vendor libimp sends only its path buffer); unlike stock an
  unterminated path is refused (-ENAMETOOLONG). Serialized with SwitchBin, which reads the same path. QEMU T41
  (gc5603): `/tmp/at/alt.bin` is the file loaded, a missing file fails `tisp_init` (-2) without an oops, frames flow
  afterwards; the older libimp with the 68-byte record also works. Cam .34 (sc5336, runtime ko swap with the
  OpenIMP `claude/issue-116` libimp): AddSensor after SetDefaultBinPath -1 -> 0, apitest binpath PASS.

## 2026-10-26 (12): T41 ISP bypass ioctl (`claude/issue-115`; QEMU T41, .34)

- **IMP_ISP_SetISPBypass (0x80085418)** is served (issue #115; it answered -ENOTTY, so OpenIMP returned -4095).
  Ported from stock `tx_isp_unlocked_ioctl` (T41 1.2.5): the 16-byte input records of the three frame channels
  (channel+0xd8) are copied, channel 0 is set to input 4 (VIC internal input = ISP bypassed) for a non-zero value or
  input 0 otherwise, then the core events 0x01000004 and 0x01000005 go through `ispcore_core_ops_ioctl` (core
  points channel+0xd4 at the input descriptor, VIC programs its internal input entry). The vendor header requires
  the call before AddSensor; as on stock, value 0 leaves channels that were never bypassed alone. QEMU T41
  (gc5603): same value 0 on a running ISP returns 0 and frames keep flowing; bypass 1 before AddSensor, Add/Enable/
  Disable/DelSensor and bypass 0 return 0 without an oops, frames flow afterwards. Cam .34 (sc5336, runtime ko
  swap): same value 0 PASS; bypass 1 before AddSensor returns 0 but the picture is identical to bypass 0 (Y 97.3 vs
  97.6), so the VIC internal input routing has no effect in the open driver yet (open gap, see issue 106).

## 2026-10-26 (13): T41 user gamma curve reaches the LUT (`claude/issue-114`; QEMU T41, .34)

- **IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetGammaAttr (0x08000025)** with a fixed curve (sRGB, Rec709, HDR or user) had no picture effect
  (issue #114): `tisp_gamma_strength_transform` answers 1 for a fixed curve ("curve already in info+0x21c"), and the
  set path wrote the LUT only on 0, so the stored curve never reached the gamma LUT registers. The LUT is now written
  for any non-negative result. The exposure-driven worker keeps skipping fixed curves. QEMU T41 (gc5603): type 4 now
  logs `ret=0` (was `ret=1`), 4 runs, frames flow afterwards, no oops (QEMU does not model gamma); on cam .34 (sc5336, runtime ko swap) the
  user curve x^0.3 gives mean Y 129.7, the linear curve 67.3 (base 95.6); before the fix both stayed at ~97.5.

## 2026-10-26 (9): T40 release-gate oopses (`claude/gate-1026`; host tests, .184)

- **Awb_Get/SetRgbCoefft (0x0800009b)** no longer oopses (agg47: Oops, epc c03d1920): the lifted `tisp_g_coefft_wb` /
  `tisp_s_coefft_wb` dropped the buffer argument and the getter looped while the WDR byte was set. Rebuilt with the OEM
  semantics (1.3.1 `tisp_bcsh_get/set_offset_rgb`): the RGB offset vector of the BCSH outer block (WDR half when
  selected) as three u16; Set writes both halves and reruns the BCSH EV and CT paths. vinum 0 only; before the BCSH
  init the getter reads the tuning bank.
- **GetAfStatistics (0x08000031)** and **GetAFMetricesInfo (0x08000034)** no longer oops (epc 801bf570 memcpy from an
  absolute address, epc c03b49d4 on the never-built AF context). The AF block and its DMA ring run (Eufy .184:
  index cycles, frame counter in every row trailer); the getters unpack the newest completed ring buffer on demand
  (OEM `tisp_af_get_statistics` layout: 15x15 zones, 256-byte rows, 136 bits per zone) and compute the metrics like
  `tisp_af_alogrithm` (AF zone weights and parameters from the tuning bank). Clean C in `driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_af.h`,
  host test `tests/t40/af_stats_host` (independent bit packer, newest-buffer pick, metrics). Read-only probe of the
  live ring on .184: Fir/Iir/YSum zone maps smooth and consistent, edges raise FIR/IIR together, HighLuma 0 except
  at a light.
- **SetSensorFPS** with the current rate returned -1 (sc830ai streams 20/1 but its set_fps range check refuses 20/1):
  like OEM `sensor_set_fps` the same rate is now a no-op; a pending rate the sensor refuses no longer stays as the
  read-back (GetSensorFPS reports the sensor's own rate again).
- AF buffer pick: newest buffer whose 15 row trailers all carry the same counter (the hardware writes rows in order;
  picking by the first row hit the buffer in progress and returned zeros 40 % of the time on the first device run).
- Device (.184 sc830ai, test ko next to the streaming application, 2026-10-26 16:2x): GetRgbCoefft x100 1024/1024/1024, Set 1040/1024/1032
  read back equal and restored; GetAfStatistics x100 all non-zero (Fir0 mean ~3000, YSum mean ~50000), GetAFMetricesInfo
  x100 metric 448425..1674000, frame counter advancing; SetSensorFPS(20/1 current) x100 ret 0; apitest sys,fs,enc,isp
  PASS 187 FAIL 0; 0 oops.

## 2026-10-26 (8): T41 ISP-drawn OSD (`claude/t41-isp-osd`; host test, .34 and .32)

- **OSD attribute (0x080000a1) and OSD block (0x080000a2)** are implemented in clean C
  (`driver/t41/tx_isp_t41_osd.h`, host test `tests/tx_isp_t41_osd_test.c`): the request is stored and the next frame's
  MSCA shadow commit writes the MSCA OSD registers (0xf0064 order, 0xf0600 + 16n geometry/address/stride, 0xf0018
  enables, 0xf0060 format) like stock `tisp_msca_set_osd`. Before, both controls returned -EPERM (the recovered
  `tisp_s_osd_block_attr` was a fragment with placeholder bases). Beyond stock: pinum checked, out-of-range pictures
  refused with -ERANGE, the enable word clears hidden pictures, GetOSDBlock reports the requested block.
- Device (.34 sc5336, .32 gc5603; region API call sequence through OpenIMP): pictures at the right place and in exact
  colours in ch0 NV12 and JPEG, move/update/second region/hide/destroy, fps unchanged, 0 oops; ch1 is not drawn
  (hardware OSD on output 0 only, as the vendor's one ISPOSD channel). A B,G,R,A byte bitmap needs component order
  ARGB (0); BGRA (13) takes byte 0 as the alpha. README "ISP OSD".

## 2026-10-26 (7): T40 AE tuning API on the frame-3A AE (`claude/t40-ae-api`; host test, .184 under the streaming application)

- **GetAeStatistics** returns the hardware AE statistics with the OEM arithmetic (`tisp_ae_main_long_process_api`):
  256-bin histogram = hardware bins x4 with bin 0 the rest of width x height (sum 8294400 at 3840x2160), 5-bin
  histogram over the SetStatisConfig histThresh (default 13/64/144/192) scaled to 65535, 15x15 zone Y =
  (sum0+sum1+sum2)/(row height x column width). Before: one count in the bin of the mean luma (sum 1) and the
  mean in every zone. Frame-grid fallback (and YUV hist domain) from the frame-3A sample. `tisp_ae_set_hist_bin`
  had main/sec swapped (histThresh of sensor 0 went to the sec copy): fixed.
- **AeTargetComp** with the OEM `tisp_ae_s_comp` factor (comp <= 128: comp/128; above: (128 + comp*300/127 - 302)/128)
  and enable semantics (DISABLE -> 128, ROI/GLOBAL -> comp, AUTO keeps; readback 1 for 128 else 3). Before, DISABLE (1)
  enabled the compensation and ROI (2) was ignored. ScenceAttr also reports luma, stable, target and ae_mean.
- **GetAeExprInfo** in the T40 1.3.1 layout (220 bytes, ExposureValue @212, EVLog2 @216). The T41 packer used before
  wrote 232 bytes, 12 past the caller's structure, with ExposureValue 0. Modes and caps read back as set.
- **SetAeExprInfo**: AeMode freeze, manual integration time / analog gain / ISP digital gain, minimum analog gain act
  on the frame AE (before only the max caps did); us units converted like the OEM.
- **AeWeight** meters the frame AE (OEM order: word 0 + bytes 8..232); **HLC/BLC** strength 0..10 weigh the brighter/
  darker zones; **FaceAe** meters the face zones to its target; **AeSpeed** (step_s lowering, step_d raising, 128 =
  default, 1/8x..8x) scales the AE steps. The lifted FaceAe/AeSpeed getters copied uninitialised kernel stack.
- **Antiflicker** (SetAntiFlickerAttr) now reaches the AE: integration time in whole half mains periods once it
  reaches one (2250 lines x 20 fps: 450 @ 50 Hz, 375 @ 60 Hz), NORMAL never below one period. Only the
  application's own SetAntiFlickerAttr (50/60 Hz) counts: the Eufy tuning bin's default (AUTO 60 Hz) does not, and
  the streamer's early request (cached before the AE context exists) is recorded at the ioctl, so it is no longer
  lost - on next GetAntiFlickerAttr read back the bin's AUTO/60 although the application sets NORMAL/50. .184 with the streaming application
  (image.anti_flicker = 1): readback NORMAL/50, IT 1800 = 4 x 450. Picture A/B under the 50 Hz LED cellar light
  (8 snapshots each): no banding difference (row-mean residual 6.20 next, 6.31 50 Hz, 6.29 60 Hz, 6.28 off),
  noise 5.6/5.5/5.5/5.5. `t40_ae_af_enable=0` switches it off.
- All new state in `.t40c2` (a first build with ~5 KB new `.bss` delivered no frames). Diagnostic parameter
  `t40_ae_api`. Host test `tests/tx_isp_t40_ae_api_test` (5-bin histogram against an instruction transcription of
  the OEM loop, 2000 random cases; comp factor for all 256 values; layouts).
- .184 (streaming application running, `t40_aetune` beside it): stats sum 8294400 / 255 bins / 5-bin sum 65535 / zones 3..159;
  histThresh 32/64/96/128 moves bin 4 to the value computed from the 256 bins; ExprInfo canary intact, EV = IT x AG x
  ISPDG; comp 60 -> target 57, AE lowers gain and IT; comp 200 raises; BLC 10 metered 98 vs mean 129 (AE brightens),
  HLC 10 metered 147 vs 119 (AE darkens); manual IT 937 held; freeze holds IT/AG through a comp change; min AG 8x ->
  gain index 13; top/bottom weights meter 129/111; speed 16 vs 1024: luma change 1 s after comp 60 2 vs 51; antiflicker
  50 Hz IT 1800 (4 x 450), 60 Hz IT 1500 (4 x 375); 0 oops. Not device-proved: the devtest's own pipeline delivered no
  frames today (also with next), and the frame-3A AE samples only ~0.4/s under the streaming application (stock too), so convergence
  takes far longer than the vendor's 2-3 s (separate item).

## 2026-10-26 (6): T32 S3: VIC/CSI from the sensor attributes, sensor frame rate, channels 1 and 2, stream-on register list (`claude/t32-s3`; QEMU-tested only, not on a camera)

- **T32: VIC and MIPI CSI/D-PHY values come from the sensor attributes** instead of cv8001 constants (`driver/t32/tx_isp_t32_vic.h`): line length in words from bits per pixel x `image_twidth`, `csi_fmt`, the `mipi_sc` control word, crop starts, frame mode (linear, WDR 2/3 frames), Sony/other MIPI mode; CSI lanes, lane mask and the D-PHY settle value `115 * clk / 4000 - 3` from the lane clock. DVP sensors and the second CSI host are refused (no reference yet). Host test `tests/tx_isp_t32_vic_test` against the vendor trace; in QEMU the VIC/CSI/D-PHY writes of `AddSensor` + `EnableSensor` are identical to the vendor stack's (67 of 67, in order; the rest are the vendor's own interrupt clears). `STREAMON` follows the vendor order (interrupt enables, stream-on list, core run bits, VIC interrupt setup, both VICs reset and run, CSI).
- **T32: sensor frame rate as the vendor tisp sets it.** isp-m0 tuning id `0x08000070` is the sensor rate (`num << 16 | den`), not a version word: GET answers the sensor's current rate, SET (`IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetSensorFPS`) calls the sensor driver's `TX_ISP_EVENT_SENSOR_FPS`, which writes the frame length over I2C. QEMU, `SetSensorFPS(10/1)`: the same I2C writes as the vendor (VTS 0x3028..0x302a = 0x001b60), frames at 9.9 fps; default 15/1: 14.7 fps on every channel. (The vendor's 13.7/8.9 fps in QEMU are its own per-frame load under emulation.)
- **T32: frame channels 1 and 2.** Channel start with scaled size, crop, scaler steps (input/output in Q14, +0x14 horizontal, +0x10 vertical; equal to the vendor's for /2, /3, /4.5, /6) and the channel parameter words, in the vendor's order; geometry checked against the sensor size (no upscaling). QEMU: ch0 1920x1080 + ch1 640x360 + ch2 1280x720 at once, 8/8 frames each, colour bars correct at every size; ch1 640x480 + ch0 1080p frames byte-identical to the vendor run's. Not yet: the polyphase filter curves the vendor loads through the shadow port and the per-channel filter select (both from tisp/IQ bin, S4) — the model does not use them, a camera does.
- **T32: stream-on register list.** The RCFG format is decoded completely: a command with bit 31 clear writes all its values to one register (a table port), so the vendor's stream-on list (3479 words: 0x5010, 13 words at 0x5040, 3456 words into the 0x32024 table port, 0x2f4) walks to the end (`tests/tx_isp_t32_mem_rcfg_test`, also with the dumped list). The stream-on list is table word 49 (0x22014 bit 17). The driver builds and runs the list at `STREAMON` (table, trigger, wait); its content is tisp state from the IQ bin and stays empty until S4.
- 5 x (modprobe both, three channels with frames, rmmod both) in one boot: clean, no oops. tisp/3A still off (S4).
- QEMU: the cv8001 model now runs at the rate its HTS/VTS registers give (`image_sensor_frame_ns()`, local QEMU branch `t32-sensor-vts`), so the sensor frame rate is visible in the emulator.

## 2026-10-26 (5): T40 unused lifted OEM AE removed (`claude/t40-drop-oem-ae`; host chains bit-exact, QEMU reload x5, .184 picture/AE/tuning readbacks equal to next)

- **T40: the lifted OEM AE is gone.** `tisp_ae_main_process` / `tisp_ae_sec_process` (event 1 / 24) and their closure
  (long/short algorithm, weight means, target, tune, tune2, EV alloc, Pweight, IspDg, short exposure, flicker detect,
  EV list, the OEM sensor gain/fps setters; 32 function bodies, about 9300 source lines) only ran with `t40_oem_ae=1`,
  which oopsed in `tisp_ae_long_tune2`. The parameter, both event registrations and the ISR pushes are removed.
  Exposure stays with the frame-3A AE (`ae_soft_*`); AE init (hardware zones), the statistics/histogram interrupts,
  the tuning getters/setters and AWB are unchanged. Removed is only what the `--gc-sections` listing drops and no
  remaining source line references.
- `tisp_ae_g_luma` and `tisp_ae_{main,sec}_get_antiflicker_step` read fixed `.bss` offsets through `%hi(.bss)` inline
  asm (layout dependent; g_luma also had main/sec swapped and summed context words instead of the histogram): now on
  `pstMainAeOri` / `pstSecAeOri` like the OEM. `tisp_ae_g_comp` now writes its result (the lift returned before the
  store). For sensor 0 the user-visible luma/comp in `GetAeScenceAttr` still come from the frame-3A AE.
- Module (stripped): 1375932 -> 1312024 bytes (xz 386512 -> 367348), `.text` 911296 -> 872180, `.bss` -82 KB
  (AE-only and write-only objects dropped by the compiler).
- Host: `tests/t40/c_chains_host` all 7 chains on 31 bins 0 differences, `dmsc_night_host` output identical to next,
  `mdns_ratio_host` PASS. QEMU reload x5: snapshots 200, 0 oops, vmalloc 25 (same as next). .184 next / removed / next
  (lamp 3649 K): settled edge 4.68/4.66/4.66, noise 5.60/5.60/5.59, S 40.1/39.6/39.6, chroma .79/.79/.78,
  dE00 9.7/9.7/9.6, AE point 37630/3336 for all; after forced night (`running_mode`) and back x2 the AE holds the
  same point; devtest AE readbacks (scene attr comp 60/200, ExprInfo limits, getters, AE statistics) identical;
  0 oops, stock back.

## 2026-10-26 (5): T41 night AE vs vendor, analysis only (`claude/t41-night-ae`; no code change, .32 run open32n1026k0)

- Same translated vendor AE on both stacks; undisturbed night state open 2092 lines / AGain 1536 / ISP dgain ~1.0 (1.50x) vs vendor 2092 / 1312 / 1176 (1.47x): +0.03 EV, luma 102 vs 99. The vendor reference's 1x AGain samples and its low-gain MDNS/SDNS/YSP registers come from the vendor image's streaming application running with `fs_keepalive` off: every FrameSource enable restarts the vendor AE at 1x, and its dumps were taken with the pipeline idle. With `fs_keepalive=0` the open stack keeps AGain 1536 across every cycle (no restart from 1x). The night edge gap is therefore not an AE target/allocation difference; a valid A/B needs the vendor run with keepalive on.

## 2026-10-26 (4): T40 BCSH in plain C instead of the literal mips2c chain (`claude/t40-bcsh-c`; host bit-exact, QEMU reload x5, .184 registers and picture equal to next)

- **T40: BCSH (brightness / contrast / saturation / hue and the channel-0 RGB->YUV conversion, TOP bit 15, on by default) rebuilt as readable C** (`driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_bcsh.h`): CSC standard tables per mode (BT601/709 full/limited, user), bank section +0x11e38 with linear and WDR half, EV path (9-knot interpolation, saturation/brightness/contrast strength curves, luma offset through the CSC matrix, clip slopes -> 0x11000..0x11070), CT path (zones and interpolation of the three CT matrices, M x CT x Minv with the hue rotation -> 0x11024..0x11038), the vendor-AWB EV/CT follow with its thresholds. `tx_isp_t40_bcsh_lit.inc` is removed; the state keeps the two OEM byte images (inter 460 B, outer 1396 B) in `.t40c2` and addresses fields by offset instead of the 72-entry pointer table. The translated OEM code in `tx_isp_t40_vawb_gen.inc` (`t40_ct_chains=1`) gets the same pointer layout from `t40_bcsh_oem_ctx`.
- Host harness `tests/t40/c_chains_host/bcsh.c` (`run.sh bcsh`): per bin the driver flows (init in all CSC modes and with WDR + user CCM, AE EV follow, CT, the four user controls, CSC switch, day/night re-init, vendor-AWB follow at and around its thresholds) plus 3000 calls on random inter/outer images (knots ascending or not, EV/CT on the knots, brightness at its 1900 clamp, contrast curve off): 39 bins, 210834 steps, 3.5 M register writes plus both images and the pointer table identical, 0 differences. 39 of 42 one-character mutations detected (the 3 misses are equivalent or unreachable).
- OEM quirks kept bit-exact (candidates for "better than vendor"): with the user CSC mode (4) the OEM copies only the first word of each user table (offset, clips, M, Minv), and the user CCM (`0x1ac` = 1) only the first word of each CT matrix.
- QEMU reload x5: snapshots 200, 0 oops, BCSH registers identical to next. .184 (light 255/3649K) next / BCSH C / next: 0x11000..0x11070 identical at default, after saturation/contrast/brightness/hue 200/170/100/160 and 60/90/150/90 and back, at night and after day/night x2 (only the EV-following gain words 0x1106c/70 differ by 1 where the AE start state differed, 37630 vs 46251); settled frames edge 4.71 / 4.68 / 4.73, noise 5.65 / 5.63 / 5.67, HSV S 40.0 / 40.3 / 40.2, chart chroma 0.79 / 0.78 / 0.80, dE00 9.5 / 9.7 / 9.4. Stripped module 1380480 -> 1375932 bytes (xz 388612 -> 386512).

## 2026-10-26 (3): T40 CDNS in plain C instead of the literal mips2c chain (`claude/t40-cdns-c`; host bit-exact, QEMU reload x5, .184 registers and picture equal to next)

- **T40: CDNS (chroma denoise, TOP bit 19, opt-in `enable_cdns`) rebuilt as readable C** (`driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_cdns.h`): bank section +0x11b8b (683 bytes), 30 gain tables from the linear or WDR half plus two fixed tables, interpolated at the denoise EV and packed into 0x1c008..0x1c050, 0x1c004 latches; init, day/night refresh, EV follow (`cdns_thresh`), tgain refresh, WDR switch, tuning get/set, remove. `tx_isp_t40_cdns_lit.inc` is removed; the state (both channels, static buffers instead of three vmallocs each) lives in `.t40c2`. The broken recovered `tisp_cdns_*` lifts are replaced by forwarders: they read and wrote the OEM gain word as `&sclk_name - 14092`, `cdns_remove` wrote `cdns_wdr_en + 18172`, and `tisp_cdns_param_array_get/set` memcpy'd through NULL when called without a buffer (now -EINVAL). The globals `main_cdns*`, `sec_cdns*`, `cdns_wdr_en` are gone.

The vendor stack keeps CDNS bypassed on this board (TOP 0x7FD9004D), so `enable_cdns` stays 0 by default. Stripped module 1382740 -> 1380480 bytes (xz 389380 -> 388612).

## 2026-10-26 (2): T40 ADR in plain C instead of the literal mips2c chain; lifted .bss strays fixed (`claude/t40-adr-c`, `claude/t40-bss-strays`; host bit-exact, QEMU reload x5, .184 picture equal to next)

- **T40: ADR (TOP bit for the adaptive dynamic range) rebuilt as readable C** (`driver/t40/tx_isp_t40_adr.h`, 37 OEM functions): init, day/night refresh, EV and total-gain update, DRC ratio, cleanup. `tx_isp_t40_adr_lit.inc` is removed. New parameter `adr_forced_blocked` (diagnostic). Host harness `tests/t40/c_chains_host/adr.c` (`run.sh adr`): 200k random helper calls plus 39 bins x 8 variants x 4 resolutions, 7.43 M register writes plus the full state, 0 differences against the literal OEM chain. Stripped module 1424144 -> 1381920 bytes.
- **T40: two lifted functions no longer write or read fixed `.bss` offsets.** `tisp_push_frame_count` incremented `.bss+6792` every frame, and that offset holds whatever object the linker puts there. Without the ADR literal objects this was the BCSH saturation origin, so saturation stayed low (HSV S 32 instead of 40) for 20-60 s after a load. The function now has its own counter. `tisp_defog_param_array_get` copies the C defog parameter block instead of reading `.bss+17424`.
- .184 (light 255/3649K): next, next + padding, next + literal ADR kept, ADR in C, 2 loads each: S 39.7-40.6 from the first settled frame. A/B next / ADR C / next: S 40.2-40.5 / 39.7-40.4 / 40.2-40.4, chart chroma 0.77-0.79 on all, also after day/night x2 and `drc_strength` 220. ADR registers 0x9000-0x9500 equal, 0 oops. QEMU reload x5 of both modules: snapshots 200, 0 oops.

## 2026-10-26: T40 exit hardening, statistics DMA stopped before its buffers are freed (`claude/t40-exit-harden`; QEMU reload x6, .184 reload x5 + picture equal to next)

- **T40: rmmod stops the ISP before freeing the statistics buffers (beyond the OEM).** Exit step 7 now resets every VIC the stream started (VIC control 0x0 = 2) and clears the AE/AWB statistics enables (0x19000, 0x18000), then frees the buffers. The OEM order (and next) freed them with the statistics enabled and the VIC running; that is only safe while the sensor is in standby. With a sensor (or sensor model) that keeps sending frames, the ISP wrote ~150 KB per frame into the freed pages: page-table corruption ("Bad page map", bad rss-counter, kfree BUG) at the next reload. Module parameters `t40_exit_vic_reset` and `t40_exit_stats_off` (both default 1, 0644) switch the two steps off. The next load sets the enables again (tisp_main_init / AE / AWB init).
- QEMU `ingenic-t40` with the old always-streaming sensor model: next crashes at the 2nd reload (paging fault, oops=2); the hardened module ran 6 x (stream 20 s, streamer stop, rmmod, insmod) with 0 oops / Bad page / SIGSEGV, snapshots 200. With the fixed sensor model (standby stops frames) both are clean.
- .184 (light 255/3649K untouched): 5 + 5 + 2 + 2 reloads of the hardened module, 0 bad pages / oops, snapshots 200, memory stable, day/night x2 switches both ways. Picture next / hardened / next: edge 4.61 / 4.63 / 4.61, noise 5.53 / 5.59 / 5.53, HSV S 40.3 / 40.6 / 40.1, chart chroma 0.78 / 0.79 / 0.78, dE00 9.5 / 9.4 / 9.3. AE after reloads: 37630/3336 for next, and for the hardened module after 1, 2 (with and without the enable clear) and 5 reloads (38715/3375 after 5). One run after 5 reloads settled at 47704/3711, which is the known second AE start state of stock/next restarts. Stock module back.

## 2026-10-26: T40 defog better than the OEM: caches follow day/night and strength, real motion variance (`claude/t40-defog-better`; host + QEMU + .184)

- **Three OEM defog flaws fixed, each behind a parameter (0644, default 1, 0 = OEM-exact):** `t40_defog_dn_cache_reset` drops the per-value air-light and transmission caches on a day/night refresh (the OEM kept serving the old bank's values); `t40_defog_trans_reset_all` drops all 256 transmission cache entries on an EV or strength change (the OEM only 180, so luma 180..255 kept the old level); `t40_defog_motion_var` computes the motion weighting from the variance over the 5x5 window (the OEM used the squared deviation of the last cell only). Diagnostics `t40_defog_sum_air` / `t40_defog_sum_trans` (0444): sums of the last 0x500a4 table.
- Host: with the parameters at 0 the chain is still bit-exact with the literal OEM chain (39 bins, 22.7 M writes, 0 differences); a new C-only test finds the flaws with the parameters at 0 (270 stale cache entries after day/night, 27 after an EV change, 180 motion cells off the window variance) and none with them at 1.
- QEMU reload x5: snapshots 200, 0 oops. .184 (light 255/3649K) night -> day with `defog_strength` 220 -> 40 -> 128: at night the transmission table sum is 0 with the OEM behaviour (the day values stay cached) and ~10500 with the fix (the night bank applies); after the switch back to day both paths write the same tables and the pictures are equal (edge 4.82 / 4.82, noise 5.79 / 5.80, S 40.8 / 40.8; next at the same point 4.80 / 5.76). 0 oops, stock module back.

## 2026-10-25 (9): T41 night NR/sharpen host check (`claude/t41-night-sharp`; host-tested, no driver change)

- **New `tests/t41/night_nr_host`**: runs the driver's MDNS/SDNS/YSP/DMSC interpolation and packing on a tuning-bin bank at a given total gain and compares the result with ISP core dumps (`scan` mode prints the gains that reproduce a dump). It maps the bank offsets `tisp_params_copy` uses (YSP at bank+0x1cc74, not +0x16dcc).
- Result for the .32 night edge gap (open 5.94 vs vendor 7.02): the open dump equals the computed registers at its gain 38519 word for word (317 words). Rounding (bit 15 of the product, the T40 bug fixed in 4d9951e4) and the bank/gain axis match the OEM. The vendor dumps match the night bank at lower gains (MDNS/SDNS/YSP 0-3840, DMSC ~35k). In its night run the vendor sat at 1x analog gain most of the time (4 of 6 samples), the open stack at 1.28-1.5x (58 of 60). The remaining gap is the night AE operating point (a higher gain moves NR towards gain knot 1), not the NR blocks.

## 2026-10-25 (9): T23/T31 unused ISP IRQ sources masked (`claude/irq-t23-t31`, device-tested on T23 .30 and T31 .26)

- **T23: `t23_isr_enable` (0xb0 interrupt enable word), default masks the unused sources 12, 13, 16.** The core ISR consumes status bits 0 (frame done), 18 (ADR), 20 (defog), 26/27 (AE), 30 (AWB) and 31 (AF); 12, 13 and 16 each raised an interrupt of their own once per frame that the ISR only acknowledged. 78 -> 45 IRQ/s at 15 fps (5.4 -> 3 per frame); `0xffffffff` restores the stock value, writable at run time.
- **T31: `t31_isr_enable`, default masks source 16** (an interrupt of its own with no consumer): 105.6 -> 90.6 IRQ/s at 30 fps. Source 12 (needed once to latch `streaming_enabled`) and the AF/ip_done bits stay.
- Measured with a ktime wrapper on the ISR: the ISR itself is only 0.3-0.4 % of one core (T23 4.0 ms/s, T31 3.4 ms/s) and the remaining interrupts are the expensive ones, so the CPU saving is within noise; the gain is fewer interrupts/wakeups. Stream (RTSP), forced night/day and picture (Y, SSIM) unchanged, 0 new oops. T21 keeps the stock mask 0x283f03ff (3 IRQ/frame), no change.
- T23 load statistics proof: `TX_ISP_LOAD_STATS=y` test ko loaded on .30, `t23_isr_bits/isr_calls/work_*` count (60 s: 4836 ISR calls, 904 frames, ADR/AE/defog work 0.29-0.75 ms each).

## 2026-10-25 (8): T32 S2 frame path (`claude/t32-s2`; QEMU-tested only, not on a camera)

- **T32: frames from the open driver.** VIC input setup at `S_INPUT`, interrupt enables + VIC run + MIPI CSI/D-PHY start at `STREAMON` (vendor trace values for the cv8001 RAW10 mode), MSCA channel setup and buffer pushes, the ISP frame-done interrupt draining the channel's done FIFO, and the framechan buffer ioctls (`REQBUFS`, `QBUF`/`DQBUF` with the 96-byte buffer, `STREAMON`/`STREAMOFF`, `LISTEN_BUF`). QEMU `-M ingenic-t32` with vendor libimp 2.2.0 and `sensor_cv8001_t32.ko`: FrameSource 10/10 frames at 1920x1080 (same colour-bar picture as the vendor run) and 5/5 at 1280x720; the vendor JPEG and H.264 encoders bound to channel 0 deliver 10/10 (H.264 decodes, 1920x1080); `DisableSensor`/`DelSensor` teardown returns 0; 3 x (rmmod both, modprobe both, run with frames) clean, no oops.
