## 2026-10-11: T41 tuning controls (branch `claude/t41-isp-handlers`, in agg-38)

Host tests and a module build against the agg-36 T41 kernel only; not run on a camera. Details and wire layouts: `docs/T41_TUNING_CONTROLS.md`.

- **T41 `/dev/isp-m0` controls that answered "not implemented" are now routed** (stock `libt41-firmware` 1.2.6 semantics, every request size-checked, copied with `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user`, under the same locks as the neighbouring controls): `Set/GetAwbConvergeStep` and `Set/GetAwbCtTrendOffset` (native AWB parameters, used by the open AWB), `Set/GetTmoCurve` (one of the ten TMO EV curves; the per-frame TMO map uses it from the next frame; values above 8191 and ids >= 10 are refused), `GetAeEvList` / `GetAeAtList` (the EV knots and target luma the open AE interpolates), `Set/GetAeConvergeStep` (stored in the AE parameters like stock; only the recovered stock AE reads them, the default bounded AE keeps its own step), `Set/GetStatisConfig` (the stored record; an enabled AWB part moves the AWB zone grid, refused with -EBUSY while streaming if the geometry changes; AE histogram thresholds and the AF part are stored only), `WaitFrameDone` (waits for the next frame done and returns the frame counter; timeout in jiffies as stock, capped at 60 s; does not hold the tuning mutex while it waits). Fixed against stock: the EV/AT list enable word and the `GetTmoCurve` curve id no longer come from uninitialised kernel stack. Not implemented: `GetAeBv`, `GetAEFlickerFlag` (vendor-AE internal state that the open AE does not keep). Module text +2.6 KB.
## 2026-10-12: branch `claude/sensor-proc-compat` (in agg-38)

- **Vendor-compatible `/proc/jz/sensor` files (all SoCs, host build and host test only, not run on a camera):** besides the existing `sensorN/`, `count` and `events` entries the registry now publishes the vendor layout: the files `boot chip_id height i2c_adapter i2c_addr max_fps mclk min_fps name pwdn_gpio rst_gpio version video_interface width` directly in `/proc/jz/sensor` (first sensor) and again in `/proc/jz/sensor/<sensor name>/`. One value per file with a trailing newline (`chip_id`/`i2c_addr` as `0x%x`, `name`/`version` strings, the rest decimal), read-only. The values (native size, `min_fps`/`max_fps`, `version`, wiring) are those of the sensor module's own `sensor_info`, found by a strict structural match inside the module image (no new state, nothing written); without a match the registry values are used and unknown ones stay empty. The extra open-driver information stays in `sensorN/` (`fps`, `status`), `count` and `events`. Host test `tests/sinfo_vendor_host_test`.

## 2026-10-11: aggregate agg-37 (not released)

`claude/agg-37` = agg-36 (a1c28797) + `claude/agg-37-size`, `claude/agg37-drv`, `claude/agg37-t23-quiet`, `claude/agg37-t40-quiet`, `claude/agg37-t41`, `claude/t41-procwatch-report`, `claude/agg37-smallfix`, `claude/agg37-t41-stab` (6330d6fb). Host tests and module builds only; no soak, every item below needs a device test unless it says otherwise.

- **T41 `GetAwbStatistics` (control 0x8000011, 2700 bytes) with a freshness contract (branch `claude/awbstats`, device-tested on the T41 .32):** the three 15x15 u32 planes (zone means `sum / count` of the raw, pre-white-balance R, G, B AWB statistics, the way the stock `tisp_awb_get_zone` copies them from its AWB state at +60096/+60996/+61896) are handed out only while the native AWB statistics stream is live and the last frame that filled them is at most one second old (`tx_isp_t41_awb_zone_report.h`, stamp set in the AWB work function, cleared at init/stop/deinit); otherwise the control fails with `-EAGAIN` and libimp returns -1, so a consumer never takes zeros or a stale copy for a measurement (the stock copies its state regardless). The route for the control existed since the T41 native AWB work; only the freshness is new. IRQ-safe (the stamp is written under the AWB mutex in the work function, read with `READ_ONCE`), no new kernel log lines. Host tests: `tests/tx_isp_t41_awb_zone_report_test` (predicate), `tests/tx_isp_t41_awb_runtime_test` (-EAGAIN before the stream, 0 with a frame, -EAGAIN after one second, after a stop and before the first frame of a restart; the planes equal the saved state). Device (T41 .32, open stack agg-36 + this module, the streamer with the ispprobe shim, static scene after convergence): global R/G and B/G (plane sums) 1.0699..1.0728 / 0.9692..0.9721 (0.27 % / 0.30 % over 40 s), 37 us per call once a second, 13 us back-to-back (1000 calls, max 358 us), 3722 statistics frames, 0 dropped, 0 bad kernel lines, restore verified.
- **T23/T31 `GetAwbZone` (control 0x800000b) with the stock scaling and a freshness contract (branch `claude/awbzone`, device-tested on cam-A and cam-B):** the 675-byte block (15x15 byte means of R, G, B) is filled the way the stock `Tiziano_awb_fpga` does it (tx-isp-t31.ko 1.1.6 HLIL 0x18218: `(u8)(sum / pixels)`, truncated, 0 for an empty zone; the T31 driver rounded and saturated, one LSB off; T23 was already identical), shared in `driver/include/tx_isp/tx_isp_awb_zone.h`. New: the control answers `-EAGAIN` unless the block comes from a statistics frame of the running stream within the last second (T31: stamped in `Tiziano_awb_fpga`, cleared in `tiziano_awb_init`; T23: stamped with the HLIL AWB snapshot, every `source_awb_hlil_interval`-th statistics frame, default 4), so a consumer never gets zeros or a stale block as a success (the stock copies the static buffer regardless). Host test `tests/tx_isp_awb_zone_test` (layout, synthetic statistics, truncation, empty zone, freshness).
- **T31 sharpness (`claude/t31-sharpness-fix`, host test and module build only):** checked `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetSharpness` 0..255 against the OEM driver (`tisp_dmsc_sharpness_set` 0x263bc): the open driver already scales the five demosaic strength curves exactly as stock (<= 128: tuned * v / 128; > 128: towards 0x258, uu towards 0x320), so 255 sets every gain point to ~595 (15x a tuned high-gain value of 40) and amplifies sensor noise in low light on the stock stack too; no scaling bug. The formula moved to `tx_isp_t31_sharpness.h` with a host test (`tests/t31_sharpness_host_test.c`). Fixed against stock: a bank refresh (bin load, day/night) re-applies a non-neutral sharpness also before the first gain is known (it was dropped until the next set). T23 uses the same OEM formula on the interpolated values; T21 and T10/T20 have their own stock mappings; unchanged.
- **Smaller modules (`docs/SIZE.md`):** T31 exports only the symbols other modules import (whitelist `tx_isp_t31_exports.h`, generated by `tools/scan_isp_imports.py`; `tests/check_isp_exports.sh` fails the build when a known importer needs a missing symbol; `TX_ISP_T31_EXPORT_ALL=y` restores the full table): `tx-isp-t31.ko` about -46 KB. T10/T20 per-function sections, `--gc-sections` and section anchors (the recovered firmware unit keeps its verified data layout): about -20 KB each. T23 bring-up parameters that nothing reads are built only with `TX_ISP_T23_BRINGUP_PARAMS=y` (-38 KB file, -29 KB RAM); T41 keeps them by default (`TX_ISP_T41_BRINGUP_PARAMS=n` leaves them out). Log prefixes `t23:` / `t41:`.
- **Driver controls (`claude/agg37-drv`, host tests and module builds only):**
  - **T23 `SwitchBin` reworked** (same branch also carries the T23/T31 vendor-control gaps of `claude/gaps-t23-t31-drv`, see 2026-10-10): the new day/night banks are built in a private, validated buffer pair outside the tuning mutex; the commit selects the running bank from the new buffer first and publishes the pair by pointer only after that succeeded. No copy over the live banks (no torn reads), a failing select leaves everything untouched, the old pair is kept one generation. A mutex shared by day/night, custom mode and the bank free protects the publish against deinit.
  - **T23 / T31 module parameter `msca_flip_state`** (read-only): bit mask of who flips each axis (1 sensor_h, 2 sensor_v, 4 isp_h, 8 isp_v). On T23 both the sensor (sc2336p register 0x3221) and the MSCA (0xd050) flip with image hflip/vflip; `shvflip=1` only flips the lens-shading mesh. Applications that mirror a crop window can read the split from it. T21 has no separate MSCA flip and no parameter.
  - **T10/T20 anti-fog preset that cannot hang:** the V4L2 control `CUSTOM_ANTI_FOG` (0x98e903) no longer runs the stock `antifog_set_preset` / `antifog_mode` pair. Cause of the earlier hangs, from the code and the bisects recorded in `tx-isp-core-tuning.c`: the stock pair runs in the ioctl thread, flips global manual Iridix and the strength while the frame path reads them, rewrites the Iridix variance word 0x3c8 with the block running and, on "off", restores the upper strength clamp through a calibration-table lookup that never returns for a missing table (and never restores 0x3c8). Now the level is validated (0..3, else -EINVAL) and stored; the AE path (`iridix_update`, the one owner of the Iridix state) applies it between frames as manual Iridix at 0xff / 0xc0 / 0x80 with the clamp saved and restored from the saved byte; "off" only undoes what the preset did; with the Iridix block off the request is counted and nothing is written. The variance nibbles are opt-in (`t20_antifog_variance`, default 0) and written at the frame-end interrupt. Host harness scenario `antifog` (`tests/t20_fw`, -O0 vs -Os equal). The hang itself cannot be reproduced on the host.
  - **T10/T20 lens-shading scale** (`IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetMeshShadingScale`): new control `V4L2_CID_PRIVATE_BASE + 0xa3` with the vendor 3.12.0 semantics (vendor function at 0x62930 sets bits 2..4 of register 0x380, the mesh scale field); the value (0..7, else -EINVAL) is written at the frame-end interrupt and only that field is touched. Like the vendor register write it does not survive a firmware register-sequence reload (resolution / WDR switch). Harness scenario `mesh-scale`.
- **T40 kernel log quiet in normal operation (host build only, not run on a camera):** about 180 bring-up and progress lines (param load, `tisp_main_init`, lsc-lit/bcsh/dmsc/clm/sdns/mdns init, MSCA, VIC/CSI start, 3A diagnostics, ...) were logged at warning level; they are now notes at info level that are dropped unless the new module parameter `t40_runtime_trace=1` is set (writable at runtime under `/sys/module/tx_isp_t40/parameters/`). A note that reports a non-zero `ret=` or `fail=` value is still logged, as a warning. Real errors (alloc/registration failures, VIC unlock timeout, bad tuning tables, dropped MSCA writes, ...) stay at warning/error level. Builds against the 4.4.94 vendor kernel tree; not tested on a T40 camera.
- **T20/T10 scaler capability no longer shrinks after `SET_SCALER` (host build only, not run on a camera):** the maximum scaler size was derived from the channel's output size, which `SET_SCALER` overwrites with the scaled size; a later, larger resolution was then rejected with EINVAL until the next crop. The scaler input is now the DS crop window, or the full sensor frame without a crop.
- **T23 kernel log quiet in normal operation (host build only, not run on a camera):** the day/night "bank switch block refresh" line is logged at warning level only when a block failed or returned an error (`failed=`/`ret=` non-zero); a clean refresh is an info note and, like the other T23 progress notes, silent unless `t23_runtime_trace=1`. The rate-limited T23 messages (source total-gain, LSC runtime, ...) now pass the same quiet gate before the rate limit, so the kernel's "N callbacks suppressed" lines no longer appear in quiet mode. The messages of the lifted AE/ADR/defog/AF/AWB code carried the old `tx_isp_t23_recovered:` prefix and therefore missed the quiet gate; they now use `t23:`. Module 972 bytes smaller.
- **T41 MSCA diagnostic `t41_msca_enable_at_frame`** (default 0, no behaviour change): with `t41_msca_cfg_update=2` a latched output is armed and enabled by the next ch0 frame-done interrupt (frame boundary) instead of anywhere inside an input frame; fallback after `t41_msca_latch_timeout_ms`. For the ch1 >= 960x540 mid-stream hang.
- **T41 `/proc/jz/isp` watcher (report only):** after an oops in `proc_reg_unlocked_ioctl()` while reading `/proc/jz/isp`, `t41_proc_watch=1` (default) snapshots every proc entry the driver creates and every 50 ms compares the fields that never change and the fops contents, plus the rbtree links of the isp directory; mismatches are logged (10 full reports, then counters `proc_watch_hits` / `proc_watch_rb_hits`). Nothing is written back to the kernel's proc structures. Known issue in `driver/t41/README.md`.
- **T41 stability fixes (branch `claude/agg37-t41-stab`; host build against the agg-36 T41 kernel only, not yet run on a camera):**
  - **Earlier review fixes now on top of agg-36** (were never merged): `proc_ivdc_writel` uses a real coherent DMA buffer instead of a garbage pointer; the IVDC interrupt delivers its stream-state event; `sensor_set_mode` sends its mode event; the vendor-AE gain setters fail cleanly; suspend/resume call the subdev callbacks.
  - **`sensor_alloc_digital_gain_short`** read its callee from outside the `g_ispcore` object and called it; it now walks core → sensor attributes like the vendor code, checks every pointer and stores a 16-bit code (vendor-AE path only).
  - **`/dev/isp-m0`:** closing a second handle freed the tuning attributes under the running streamer (BCSH/ratio settings stopped working, later accesses hit freed memory). Handles are now counted; the last close disables tuning. Host test `tx_isp_t41_tuning_open_test`.
  - **Out-of-object accesses of the recovered core** (`vic_cmd_buf`, `tgain_wdr_s`, `direct_mode`, `ispmem`): moved to correctly sized objects or removed. A scan of the compiled module (`tests/t41_overrun/`) shows that in the agg-36 build none of them is executed on a linear-mode sensor; they do not explain the `/proc/jz/isp` oops. The only reachable out-of-object stores are in the WDR path: the unrecovered WDR fusion body writes about 36 KB past the module data and its copy loop cannot terminate, so it is now refused unless `t41_wdr_fusion_unsafe=1`.
  - **Quiet kernel log:** 55 progress lines (subdev notify on every event, format ioctls, frame-channel open, buffer/stream diagnostics, init/deinit steps) were warnings; they are now info notes, logged only with `t41_runtime_trace=1`. A note reporting a non-zero `ret=`/`fail=` is still a warning.
## 2026-10-12

- **T31/T23 `fcrop_upscale_pct` capped at 100 (2.0x):**
  - **Measured on the T31 Garage (T31X, sc4336p),** main 1920x1080 + sub 640x360 together:
    - Steps 10/20/33/50/75/100 % with windows down to 960x540, each held 60 s: clean, with no frame loss and no decode errors.
    - Crop off/on and idle frame-source off/on with the crop active also ran clean.
  - **At 150 % (768x432, 2.5x)** the window was accepted and the SoC hard-hung at once. The hardware watchdog reset it after about 60 s, without an oops, so no recovery is possible.
  - **Driver change:** it now clamps the parameter to 100 on T31 and T23, whatever value is written. The default stays 0, which never allows an upscale.
  - The T31L uses the same driver and MSCA path; the same limit is expected but has not been measured.
  - Host test `tests/tx_isp_t31_fcrop_test`. The modules build against the agg-37 kernels; the cap itself is not yet device-tested as a module.

## 2026-10-09

- **Release candidate `agg-34` (da9baf1e, on top of `next`)** = agg-32 (3cf9bdac) + the front-crop fixes below + the T20 `isp-m0` gain cap. OpenIMP `agg-34` (838f8147) is its userspace counterpart. The combination has not run on the cameras as a whole; each fix below was device-tested on the SoC named (agg-32 soak: 43.4 h, 0 unplanned reboots and oopses on the cameras without user interaction).
- **T31 front crop (d940ba20, f8ae7457, b9be16fb, 27dc8be4):**
  - **Crash:** a front-crop window smaller than a channel's scaler output stalled every MSCA output (the MSCA cannot upscale). A live 320x180 window froze the stream and rebooted the camera twice without an oops; the persisted window hung the next boot and tripped the isp-guard.
  - **Guard:** the kernel refuses such a window with -EINVAL and one log line (the streamer reports it as rejected). The set-time check reads the programmed channel outputs (`0x9900 + ch*0x100`) and covers only running channels, so a stale register value does not veto a crop re-applied at start. A scaler output larger than an active window is refused, and a main stream larger than the window drops the crop with a warning.
  - **Effect:** the scaler steps (`0x9904`, `0x9804`) and the latch are written, the crop survives an idle off/on, disabling restores the full frame. Before this, nothing happened even with a valid window. Whether a valid window really zooms the picture is **experimental, not proven**.
  - **Module parameter `fcrop_upscale_pct`** (default 0, writable at run time under `/sys/module/tx_isp_t31/parameters/`): percentage by which the geometry check tolerates an upscale, to measure how far the MSCA can enlarge a window before it stalls.
  - Tested: refusal of too-small windows, an 18-case matrix and a 30 min run with a 960x540 crop; a main stream of 2560x1440 dropped the crop as intended and going back to 1080p re-applied it.
- **T23 front crop (25b1d8c7, 164225e5):** after a crop with a 1280x720 main stream, crop off kept the picture cropped (stock only clears `fcrop_en`; the window stays locked in the channel records until module reload) and returning to a 1080p main stream hung the MSCA and the watchdog rebooted the camera.
  - Crop off now unlocks every channel record, restores the full sensor window and reloads the running channels.
  - A set-format whose output does not fit a locked window drops the crop (warning) and is checked again instead of stalling.
  - The last close of `/dev/tx-isp` releases the lock and restores the full sensor window, so a streamer that starts without crop gets the full frame.
  - Module parameter `fcrop_upscale_pct` (default 0) as on T31. Measured: the T23 MSCA upscales up to 2.0; at 2.2 it stalled briefly, the crop was dropped, no reboot.
  - Tested on a T23 camera: an 18-case matrix (crop off with a 1080p main stream, flip, day/night, fast changes with many streamer restarts, corner windows, sub-stream, reboot with a persisted crop) all green. All beyond stock.
- **T20 and T10 front crop (10bac52f, b9381941):** a window is stored while no downscaled channel is open (a streamer closes an idle sub-stream; before, such a window was refused and a stored crop was refused at streamer start) and applied at stream on; it is released together with the tuning session, so a streamer that starts with crop off no longer finds a zoomed picture. Device-tested on a T20 and a T10 camera (overlay module); T10 shares the T20 code.
- **T20 `isp-m0` reports the reachable analog gain cap (da9baf1e):** the compact AE clamps to `attr->max_again` and to the stabilizer ceiling (128), but the dump printed only the sensor maximum (158 on the tested sensors), so a streamer's day/night rule waiting for the cap never saw it reached and both T20 cameras stayed in Day mode in the dark under agg-32. The dump now shows the smaller value; shared with T10. Host-tested (`tests/t20_ae_max_host_test.c`); the streamer needs its matching change and a device run with both is pending.
- **Known issues (agg-34):** T20 day/night needs this driver fix plus the streamer change; T31 front-crop picture effect not proven; T41 channel 1 (green or flat frames at day/night switches, exposure does not follow channel 0) and T41 in general are experimental and not part of the first release; T10 occasionally switches to Day in the dark with a black frame.

## 2026-10-06

- **Release candidate `agg-28` (79b754b4, on top of `next`):** release plan: `next` goes to `aperto` (fast-forward) after the long soak, tagged `vYYYY.MM.DD`; the first release covers T10 (not re-tested), T20, T21, T23 and T31; T41 is not part of it. apitest on the candidate: T20 212 PASS / 0 FAIL, T21 228 PASS / 0 FAIL, T23 304 PASS / 4 FAIL (the AF getters, fixed by `source_af=1`), T31 274 PASS / 1 FAIL; 0 oopses on every camera.
- **Known issues (release candidate):**
  - **T23 crop hang:** the pipeline stops after a FrameSource crop change (hard hang); under investigation, release blocker.
  - **T31 H.264 stalls after the JPEG channel is torn down** (cause not found yet); under investigation, release blocker.
  - **T41 is not part of the first release** (branch `claude/t41-ch1-fix`, not merged): the MSCA output size/crop, scaler ratios, global input size and the flip/algorithm word are staged registers (a write applies at the next input frame after `0xf0010 = 1`, a read returns the active value). Without the latch every output ran with its reset geometry (channel 1 at 640x360 wrote 1280x720 into 640x360 buffers: garbage chroma, a band at the top of the next buffer); `t41_msca_cfg_update=1` fixes the geometry (640x360..1440x810, 20 restarts OK) but brings the output-restart hang back after 4 to 13 cycles, so it stays off. 38 tuning IDs are still missing (gamma, CCM, CSC, module control, auto zoom, manual exposure, DRC, DPC, defog ratio, mask, scaler level).
- T23 clocks: `ispcore_activate_module` set `cgu_isp`/`cgu_vpu` from the flags and init-state words of a clock-table entry three slots further on instead of the `isp_clk`/`isp_clka` module parameters (as the stock module reads them), so the parameters never reached the hardware; fixed (`claude/fable-chan-restart`, in this candidate).
- T23 module parameter defaults of the candidate: see the table in the README ("T23 module parameters").
- T23 AF statistics on by default (`source_af=1`, as in stock: `tisp_init` always runs `tiziano_af_init` and registers the AF interrupt). Tested on a T23 camera (sc1a4t 720p15, streamer running, module loaded in RAM):
  - No measurable cost. CPU busy was 47.8 % with `source_af=0` and 48.1 % with `source_af=1` (3 x 60 s each, both within noise). The core IRQ rate was 88.7/s in both cases: the AF interrupt is a status bit of the core interrupt.
  - apitest GetAfHist (8x8), GetAFMetrices, GetAfWeight and GetAfZone: PASS, with live values.
  - 20 `rvd` restarts and an ISP day/night switch: every snapshot OK, 0 oopses.
- T23 MSCA scratch area (`msca_scratch`, default 1; docs in `driver/t23/docs/MSCA_SCRATCH.md`).
  - Before, a channel stopped with its output kept enabled and the input running made the MSCA write every further frame into the stream's last buffer, which user space frees after the close.
  - Now STREAMOFF queues a scratch address behind the stream's buffers. The scratch area is the tail of the ISP buffer in rmem (GET_BUF asks for one Y plane at sensor size, 900 KiB for 720p). The kernel heap has no contiguous block of that size: the largest free block is 256 KiB, even after compaction.
  - QBUFs of a parked channel are held back until STREAMON, as stock does.
  - The input waits until each parked channel's scratch address has been consumed before it stops. A first build without this wait hung the camera at the next session start (3 of about 35 cold starts).
  - Counters `msca_scratch_parks/frames/deferred/skips/settle_timeouts/settle_ticks_max`; host test `tests/tx_isp_t23_scratch_test.c`.
  - Tested on the same T23 camera:
    - 20 cold starts and 20 `rvd` restarts: all snapshots OK.
    - An ISP day/night switch: OK.
    - 80 parks, 81 scratch completions, 0 settle or drain timeouts, longest settle 30 ms.
    - The apitest FrameSource disable/enable cycle: PASS.
    - No `unmatched MSCA completion` at STREAMOFF.
    - 0 oopses.
- Release candidate (branch `claude/release-t23-driver`). The T23 defaults are now the stock-like set that ran 7 h overnight plus many restarts: `chan_stop_keep_input=1`, `msca_keep_enabled=2`, `msca_fifo_rearm=0`, `msca_flip_skip_noop=1`, `msca_restart_skip=1`, `msca_session_release=1`, `crumbs=0`. The hang needed `chan_stop_keep_input=0` together with `msca_fifo_rearm=1`.
  - The cold-start snapshot 503 is now prevented as stock does it, without the FIFO rearm: STREAMOFF waits up to 21 x 10 ms until the channel's buffers have left the hardware, and QBUF invalidates the buffer's cache lines (`chan_stop_drain`, `qbuf_cache_inv`, docs in `driver/t23/docs/STREAMOFF_DRAIN_WAIT.md`).
  - Tested on the same T23 camera (sc1a4t, streamer running, module loaded in RAM, openimp `claude/release-t23-vbm`):
    - 20 cold starts (20 s idle): every snapshot OK (ch0 1.8-2.1 s, ch1 2.8-3.2 s after start).
    - 20 `rvd` restarts: every snapshot OK.
    - 44 drains (at most 70 ms), 0 drain timeouts.
    - An ISP day/night switch: OK.
    - 0 oopses.
- T21: `SetColorfxMode(SEPIA)` works (it returned -1 before): the CCM saturation list goes to 0 like B/W and the CSC U/V row sums get a tint (U below, V above neutral for a grey input, luma unchanged). A CSC attribute set during sepia keeps the tint; leaving sepia puts the stored matrix back. Device-tested on cam-D (jxf23): dU 17 / dV 19 against the plain picture.
- Pending (branch `claude/release-t31-framedrop`): T31 `IMP_ISP_SetFrameDrop`/`GetFrameDrop` (ioctl 0xc00456e6/0xc00456e7, three 12-byte records) with the stock semantics: lsize (window of lsize+1 frames, 0..31, larger is refused) at ISP register +0x130 and fmark (bit set = output) at +0x134 of ((ch+0x98)<<8); disabled writes lsize 0 / fmark 1; get reports the registers and enable = 1 like the stock module. The ISP drops the frames itself (device-checked); the old driver helper had the two registers swapped and the DQBUF software drop dropped a second time, so it is removed. Host test `tests/tx_isp_t31_framedrop_test.c`.
- T21 `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetBrightness` acts (beyond vendor, branch `claude/release-t21-brightness`; the vendor T21 kernel only stores the value and the OEM AE never reads it): the AE luma target is scaled by value/128 (clamp 1..255, floor 16/128), a converged AE re-derives its cached target at once. 128 is the vendor path unchanged. Device-tested on cam-D (jxf23): brightness 30/128/225 gives mean Y 28.8/117.7/193.0 (imgfx), the streamer's brightness setting live 44.5/130.8/223.9 (snapshot), 0 oops. Host test `tests/t21_ae_comp_host_test.c`.

## 2026-10-05

- Pending (branch `claude/t23-chan-restart-hang`, built, **device test pending**): T23 hard hang after streamer start/restart on cam-B (on-demand channel 0 wakes). Default now = the device-tested d28a0177 set (`msca_flip_skip_noop=1`: no update request for unchanged flip bits; `msca_keep_enabled=1`: the last STREAMOFF keeps the MSCA output enabled; `msca_restart_skip=1`: an unchanged restart is not reloaded, its FIFO is rearmed with the input stopped) plus `msca_session_release=1`: tx-isp STREAMON with the input stopped switches off outputs left enabled and clears their FIFOs, so a restarted streamer never starts the input under the old process's freed buffers (2227 hang). a325b523/4ea284c9 (release on close, frame-done ISR application, deferred starts) hung earlier on the device and were dropped. `crumbs` off by default (rmem crumbs do not survive a reboot, U-Boot zeroes rmem; a `mem=` hole does). Evidence, stock comparison and test in `driver/t23/README.md` "Output/channel restart hang"; same class as the T41 output restart hang.
- Pending (branch `claude/streamer-gaps-t1x-t23-t31`, host- and build-tested only, no device test yet): T23 SetAutoZoom (control 0x80000e8, 36-byte IMPISPAutoZoom) as the stock `apical_isp_autozoom_s_attr` / `tisp_s_autozoom_control`: crop window and scaler output go into the channel's MSCA record (crop/scaler lock bytes, +0x10..+0x24), a running channel is reloaded like the front crop (same lock and channel load; start/stop paths untouched). Beyond stock: windows outside the sensor picture, below 64x64 or odd, and scaler outputs above the window are refused with -EINVAL instead of stalling the MSCA. The stock T23 module has no handler for 0x80000a5 (GetBlcAttr) and 0x80000e9 (SetScalerLv) either (the firmware's `tisp_get_blc_attr` is an empty stub and `tisp_s_scaler_level_control` is never called), so those two stay -EINVAL like the stock driver; the T23 AWB cluster/trend runtime and SetMaskBlock of `claude/t23-awb-runtime` are merged in this branch.
- Pending (branch `claude/t23-af`, host- and emulator-tested only, no device test yet): T23 AF statistics chain from the stock module, off by default at the time (`source_af=1`; on by default since 2026-10-06): AF block + core interrupt bit 31, focus values, GetAfHist/SetAfHist, Get/SetAfWeight, GetAFMetrices, GetAfZone (controls 0x8000042/43/44/46). Shares the data handling of the T31 chain; verified identical to the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/af_emu.py`).
- Pending (branch `claude/t23-cids`, host- and emulator-tested only, no device test yet): T23 SetAwbClust/GetAwbClust (0x0800000e, 40-byte IMPISPAWBCluster) and SetAwbCtTrend/GetAwbCtTrend (0x0800000f, 24-byte IMPISPAWBCtTrend) on the stock objects (_awb_cluster/_awb_trend, api_para/api_status) with the stock word order; identical to the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/awb_api_emu.py`). SetMask/GetMask (0x080000e5) are not handled by the stock module either (it returns -1 for both), so they stay unrouted.
- Pending (branch `claude/t23-awb-runtime`, on top of `claude/t23-cids`, host- and emulator-tested only, no device test yet): the open T23 AWB reads the cluster and trend objects like the stock module. ClusterEn == 1 runs the stock cluster stage between the light-source weights and the weighted mean (14x14 rg/bg histogram, 12 peaks, 5 seeds per peak, mean shift, merge, zone weights by cluster size; the stock `.bss` state is kept as stock does, including its stale-count behaviour); the histogram is filled on every run. The colour temperature trend is added to the R/B gains like `JZ_Isp_Awb_Awbg2reg` (>= 5000 K / mid / <= 3000 K offsets, 32-bit wrap, limit 0x3fff, at the colour temperature before SetAwbCt). ToleranceEn/tolerance_th hold the automatic gains until the summed Q8 change exceeds the threshold (stock then ramps, the open AWB steps; without ToleranceEn the update rule is unchanged). Cluster and trend objects are reloaded from the IQ bank at stream start and day/night like `tiziano_awb_params_refresh` (a Set before that is dropped, as in stock). The lifted `tisp_awb_set_cluster_awb_params` had the cluster words in the user order (stock order differs) and `tisp_awb_set_ct_trend` an unbounded copy loop; both fixed (not reachable from the open control path). Cluster stage and trend identical to the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/awb_cluster_emu.py`: state and weights of random multi-frame scenes, trend over random objects, gains of the built module).
- Pending (same branch): T23 SetMaskBlock/GetMaskBlock (0x08000183, 20-byte IMPISPMaskBlockAttr) on the stock mscaler mask table (`tisp_s/g_mscaler_mask_block_attr`: 16-byte entry per block at mscaler + 1836, dirty bit mask at + 2308), identical to the stock module in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/mask_block_emu.py`, whole mscaler object after Set, Get block, copy sizes). Beyond stock: chx/pinum outside 0..2/0..3 are refused (stock writes past the table), Get reads chx/pinum from the user block (stock uses an uninitialised stack local) and returns 0 for the bytes it does not define. Stock quirk kept: Get never reports mask_en. The stored masks have no image effect yet: the mscaler update that consumes them (`tisp_msca_Shd_ctrl`) is not run by this driver.
- Pending (branch `claude/t20-fw-optimize`, host-tested only, device test open): the recovered T20/T10 firmware unit builds at `-Os` like the rest of the module (T20 module text 399 to 245 KB, `.ko` 589 to 409 KB; T10 the same). Spots that only worked at `-O0` fixed against the OEM disassembly (dropped call arguments, a partition-LUT walk past its object, command-interface state on the stack, a stack buffer one word short, an inline-asm tail jump, a missing return). New host harness `tests/t20_fw` runs the firmware at `-O0`/`-Os`/`-O2` through init, 239 frames, API sweeps and day/night tuning switches and requires identical register traces and state. `TX_ISP_FW_O0=1` restores the `-O0` build for A/B tests.

## RC1 (v2026.10.11-rc1, 2026-10-11)

open-tx-isp 191be6c5 (tag v2026.10.11-rc1 = soaked agg-35 6e464ac7 + docs), paired with OpenIMP fe9307c (agg-35 687a68e4 + docs). The tag adds documentation only.

**Soak evidence:** agg-35 ran on 10 cameras from 2026-10-10 18:04 to 2026-10-11 08:00 (about 14 h): 0 kernel oopses, 0 unexplained reboots, 0 application restarts, VA-API decode check 54/54. The soaked images ran a streaming application on thingino `aperto` base a4bcc1c86.

**Supported SoCs:** release: T10, T20, T21, T23, T31. Experimental (not part of the release promise): T30 (no device in the lab, host/build evidence only), T40 (first device run only, see the matrix) and T41 (channel 1 and night colour open).

**Highlights since the last `aperto` state (ca9cbf0e):** about 190 commits.
- **Front crop:** T31 refuses a window smaller than a channel's scaler output (-EINVAL) instead of stalling the MSCA, drops a crop a larger main stream cannot fit and releases the window with the ISP session; T23 crop hang fixed; T20/T10 keep the window until a downscaled channel opens; module parameter `fcrop_upscale_pct`.
- **T20/T10 day/night:** `isp-m0` reports the analog gain cap the AE really plateaus on (also for stepped gain tables).
- **T41 driver controls** applications call: frame drop, sensor register, WDR enable/disable, AF weight (device-tested on the lab T41).
- Robustness: reload/stop cycles without an oops, checked inputs, sensor module pinned while streaming.

**Known issues (RC1):**
- **T41 channel 1 (sub-stream) colour at night** differs from channel 0 (picture soak finding); T41 stays experimental.
- **T31, black picture of about 80 s** seen once during an application-driven day/night probe; not understood yet.
- **T23, dusk flapping:** on one T23 camera it switched between day and night several times at dusk. Day/night switching is decided by the application; the driver and OpenIMP report sensor values.
- **Day/night in a poorly lit location** also follows the application's thresholds; the driver and OpenIMP report sensor values.
- T40: CBR delivers only about 20 % of the target rate, H.265 not supported yet. T41: no audio hardware on the test board (API level only).

**Next (after RC1, agg-36):** work that is not in agg-35 and therefore not part of this release candidate: T40 rate control and H.265, T40 ISP-drawn OSD, further vendor-API gap work (T30, T1x, T23/T31), T41 mask/zoom, log level default, IVS result ordering and review fixes.

## 2026-10-10

- Pending (branch `claude/t41-zoom-mask`, host- and build-tested only, no device test yet): T41 AutoZoom (0x08000077), MaskBlock (0x08000074) and ScalerLv (0x080000a6) from the stock libt41-firmware 1.2.6 routines instead of -EOPNOTSUPP. A streaming output is only reprogrammed with the latch-while-off sequence (`t41_msca_cfg_update=2`, `t41_msca_stop_disable=1`) and output <= 768x432 (`t41_msca_live_max_w/h`); otherwise the request is stored and applied at the output's next start. Beyond stock: AutoZoom windows outside the sensor and mask chx/pinum out of range are -EINVAL, the ScalerLv level survives output reloads. The MSCA start words now include the input crop position (`0xf00a0 + ch * 8`). Details and device test plan: `driver/t41/README.md` "MSCA zoom, mask and scaler level".

- T41 `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SwitchBin` (tuning control `0x080000a5`, set only; branch `claude/switchbin-t41`, on agg-35). Host test and module build only, **no device test yet** (T41 camera was in a soak).
  - Request as the stock `tx_isp_switch_bin` (libimp 1.1.0 to 1.2.6): 132 bytes {u32 enable; char bname[128]}, ISP channel must be streaming (stock logs and answers 0 otherwise, here -EBUSY). enable 1 loads bname, enable 0 returns to the path the sensor started with.
  - Beyond stock, tear-free: the stock loader reads the new file over the live bank buffer and checks afterwards. Here the file is read into a private buffer and checked completely first (version, SoC tag, extents, CRC, plus both banks at least `0x1f004` bytes, which stock never checked); any failure leaves the running banks, registers and the day/night state untouched. The validated image is then published by pointer (day/night bank pointers, bin manager, day/night flag word), the previous image is kept one generation longer so a reader of the old pointer never sees freed memory, and the stock re-apply (core+468 = 1, day/night event on the next frame) runs on the ISP event thread. One switch at a time (mutex shared with `tisp_deinit`); refused with a second sensor loaded, because the driver has one bin buffer.
  - Not reproduced: the stock `tisp_tstp_init/deinit` pair (a neutral stub in this driver). Host test `tests/tx_isp_t41_switchbin_test.c` (request layout and validation, CRC, bank and extent checks; with `T41_SAMPLE_BIN` also a real gc5603 bin).

- T23 / T31 vendor-control gaps (branch `claude/gaps-t23-t31-drv`, on agg-35). **Host build only, device test pending**: neither module has run on a camera.
  - T31 `SetFixedContraster` (control `0x8000102`): the handler copied the 3 bytes and dropped them. It now calls `tisp_set_bcsh_fixed_contrast`, as the stock s_ctrl does (HLIL `tx-isp-t31.ko` 0x6e54..0x6e64), which stores mode / y low / fixed value and runs `tiziano_bcsh_update` (the BCSH MJPEG range was already consumed there).
  - T23 `SetDrawBlock` / `GetDrawBlock` (`0x8000180`, 32 bytes), `SetOSDAttr` / `GetOSDAttr` (`0x8000181`, 12 bytes), `SetOSDBlock` / `GetOSDBlock` (`0x8000182`, 20 bytes): the stock handlers on the mscaler object, in `tx_isp_t23_tuning_ext.h`; emulator audit `driver/t23/audit/osd_draw_emu.py` against the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` (state, dirty words, copy sizes, return codes, stale-size quirk, all user-block bytes identical, 300 random cases per check) and host test `tests/tx_isp_t23_tuning_ext_test.c`. Like the mask blocks the stored block has no image effect yet: the MSCA update that programs it (`tisp_msca_Shd_ctrl` -> `tisp_msca_api_set_osd` / `_set_line`) is not run by this driver, and the lifted `_set_osd` / `_set_line` bodies are not faithful (not rewritten here). Stock quirks kept: a block with x + w or y + h beyond the channel limits is logged and ignored while the call returns 0 (the stock s_ctrl drops the helper result). Beyond stock: the OSD block index above 7 and the draw block index above 5 are refused (stock does not check them); the getters read the user's block first for the index (stock passes an uninitialised stack local; the OSD block getter of stock always reports block 0).
  - T23 `SwitchBin` (`0x8000185`, 68 bytes { enable, name[64] }), tear-free like the T41 one (reworked on `claude/agg37-drv`): the file is read and checked (standard IQ size, "2.20"/"header0", size, CRC) into a private pair of bank buffers before the tuning mutex is taken; the commit (under the tuning mutex and a bank mutex shared with day/night, custom mode and the bank free at deinit) selects the running mode's bank from the NEW buffer first and only after that succeeded publishes the new pair by pointer. No memcpy over the live day/night banks; a rejected file or a failing bank select leaves banks, pointers and registers untouched (the new buffers are freed); the old pair is retired one generation. A running custom mode keeps its bank. Not reproduced: the stock post-step that writes a day/night state word of the lifted sensor object. Module build only; **device test pending**.
  - T23 `SetWB_ALGO` (`0x800000c`): accepted like stock (mode 0..2 stored in the AWB light-source flag `_light_src + 76`, other modes logged, always returns 0). The four AWB threshold registers are not rewritten: the AWB this driver runs uses the wide thresholds that stock mode 1 writes and has no IQ-threshold window to switch to.
  - T23 `AwbSync` (`0x8000011`): unchanged on purpose. The stock s_ctrl and g_ctrl reject it too (-1, checked in the emulator), and the vendor libimp 1.3.0 has only the MultiCamera variant.

- T10/T20 `isp-m0` "MAX SENSOR analog gain" now reports the step the AE really plateaus on (branch `claude/t10-gaincap`, on agg-34).
  - Cause: jxh42 (T10) boots with the SetMaxAgain ceiling 144 (customer calibration), which `da9baf1e` reports correctly, but the sensor gain table is stepped and `alloc_again()` rounds the request down: 144 (log2 x32) falls between the table entries 142.7 and 144.7, so the AE sat at 142 in the dark while the cap read 144. A streamer that waits for gain >= cap never saw "maxed".
  - Fix: the compact dump runs the same request (sensor max, then ceiling) through the sensor's `alloc_again()` and reports the applied step (`t20_ae_request_log2_16`, `t20_ae_plateau_log2_5` in `tx_isp_t20_ae_max.h`). Exact steps (ceiling 128) and the sensor maximum are unchanged. Host test `tests/t20_ae_max_host_test.c` extended.
- T41 driver controls the streamers call that the driver refused (branch `claude/t41-driver-gaps`, on `claude/t41-tuning`). Device-tested on the Vanhua T55A / gc5603 (module loaded in RAM, streamer restored afterwards).
  - Frame drop, ioctl `0xc004542c` (set) / `0xc004542d` (get): 40-byte request {channel, 3 x {enable, lsize, fmark}} as the stock libimp sends it; entry i programs the register pair 0xf0130 + i*0x100 / 0xf0134 + i*0x100. The recovered `tisp_set_frame_drop` called `system_reg_write()` without the value argument (the registers received stale data); rewritten from the stock disassembly. Beyond stock: all three lsize values are checked before anything is written (stock left earlier entries applied), channel must be 0.
  - Sensor register, ioctl `0xc040540d` (write) / `0x8040540e` (read): 64-byte request {name[32], vinum, bus type, ..., reg at 48, value at 56}; forwarded as sensor event 0x2000011 / 0x2000012 to the VIN subdev. Read of gc5603 0x03f0/0x03f1 returns the chip id 0x5603.
  - WDR enable/disable, ioctl `0x80045413` / `0x80045414`: 4-byte channel; the channel is range checked (stock indexes core memory with it) and a flag recorded. No WDR event is sent: a linear sensor (gc5603) has no WDR mode, so there is nothing to switch.
  - AF weight, tuning control `0x8000032` (get/set, 225 bytes): copied to/from the AF parameter block at +531 like stock; the AF statistics stay off on this fixed-focus build, so the table is a stored value. Refused with -ENODEV when the AF block does not exist (stock dereferences it unchecked).
  - Not changed: `0x8000054` (WdrOutputMode) still fails; the stock dispatcher has no handler for it either (unknown ID -> -EPERM). Defog ratio (module ratio unit 4) still refuses a non-neutral value: the stock path rewrites three defog parameter arrays and is not recovered yet.

## 2026-10-09

- T31 front crop (branch `claude/review34-fixes`, review of agg-34).
  - The crop window survived the ISP session (the next streamer got the old zoom back at its first set-format, and the streamer sends no "crop off" when the crop is disabled in its config). The last close of the ISP now drops the window (`tisp_fcrop_release`, like T20 b9381941 and T23 164225e5; state only, no register access with the core stopped).
  - Behaviour change since agg-34, now documented: T31 digital zoom works only when every running channel has a scaler output <= the window. With an unscaled main stream (sensor size, `scaler.enable = 0`) `SetFrontCrop` is refused with -EINVAL (the streamer logs "SetFrontCrop failed"); before, the window was accepted and the sub streams were upscaled, which hung the MSCA. A window set earlier is dropped at the next set-format of such a channel. The comment in `tisp_channel_attr_set` was wrong: without a scaler the output is the window at 1:1, the problem is the buffer size.
  - The decision logic is in `driver/t31/tx_isp_t31_fcrop.h` with host test `tests/tx_isp_t31_fcrop_test.c`.

- T20/T10 `/proc/jz/isp/isp-m0` reported an analog gain cap the AE never reaches (branch `claude/t20-maxgain-report`, on `claude/t31-fcrop-guard`; release blocker for the T20 day/night switch).
  - Cause: the compact AE clamps the analog gain to the sensor maximum and to the SetMaxAgain ceiling `stab.global_max_sensor_analog_gain` (sensor_drv.c), but the compact dump printed only the sensor maximum (`attr->max_again >> 11`). On jxf22/jxf23 that is 158 (index cap 7838), while the AE stops at the ceiling 128 (16x = 4096), which `isp_info` already shows. The streamer therefore took night_gain 4096 as reachable, and its plateau rule for an unreachable night threshold never armed; in the dark the index sat at 4096 x 0.876 (IT 1967 of 2246, 60 Hz anti-flicker steps) and the camera stayed in Day.
  - Fix: "MAX SENSOR analog gain" in the compact path is now min(sensor max, ceiling) (`driver/t20/tx_isp_t20_ae_max.h`, shared with T10, which builds the T20 sources). Non-compact path unchanged.
  - Host test `tests/t20_ae_max_host_test.c`; T20 and T10 modules build without warnings, check_ko_symbols OK. Device check pending (Wyze Cam2 .107, insmod in RAM).

## 2026-10-06

- T23 front crop with an offset window stalled the MSCA (branch `claude/t23-roi-crop-test`, on top of `claude/release-t23-crop`).
  - Cause: the per-channel window origin (MSCA channel register +0x00) was written in sensor coordinates, but the hardware reads it relative to the global input window (0xd090, the union of the channel windows). A window at (0,0) worked, an offset window made the sub channel (or both) deliver no frame, with a geometry the check accepts (960x540 at 160,90 and 768x432 at 256,144 on a 1280x720 sensor, ch0 640x360, ch1 320x180).
  - Fix: the origin is written relative to the global window (module parameter `msca_rel_origin`, default Y; N = old behaviour), and `set_fcrop` first stores the window in all channels and then reloads them, so all origins are relative to the final union.
  - Jooan A6M (module loaded in RAM, streamer stopped), ch0 640x360, ch1 320x180: 12 offset and edge windows in one boot (0,90 / 160,0 / 160,180 / 320,0 / 320,90 / 64,64 / 160,90 / 128,72 1024x576 / 256,144 768x432 / 320,180 640x360 / 640,360 640x360 / 0,0 640x360), all with frames on both channels; before: each of the first 10 stalled the pipeline (reboot needed). Invalid windows (below the main channel, outside the sensor, zero size) return -EINVAL. imgfx `fs1-crop-*` run. Streamer restart 12x and cold start 2x with snapshots, no Oops.

- T23 MSCA geometry check (release blocker, branch `claude/release-t23-crop`; docs in `driver/t23/docs/MSCA_GEOMETRY.md`, host test `tests/tx_isp_t23_msca_geom_test.c`).
  - Before, the imgfx crop batch hung every frame channel for good (a T31 sc2336 camera, sc1a4t; also with `msca_scratch=0` and with the agg-26 module). `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetFrontCrop` with a 50 % window under the full-size main channel asked the MSCA to upscale, which it cannot do. After that, no channel completed a frame, and every STREAMOFF timed out with "2 of 2 buffers still queued". The window stays locked in the channel records, as in stock, so every later session hung too, until a reboot. A frame-channel crop outside the scaler output, or larger than the 640x360 buffer, is the same class of fault (imgfx `fs1-crop-*`; on a T31 camera it also overruns the buffer).
  - Now set-format and front crop refuse such a geometry with -EINVAL before anything is written (beyond stock). The rules: scaler output <= input window, crop inside the scaler output, crop <= frame buffer. The driver logs one line and counts it in `msca_geom_rejects`.
  - Tested on the Jooan A6M (module loaded in RAM, streamer running):
    - Crop batch 10x: `fcrop-mid50`, `fcrop-topleft50` and `fs1-crop-mid50` were refused, `fs1-crop-topleft50` (crop = scaler output) ran. No hang, the base-end picture was OK each time.
    - Full imgfx run: 33/33 batches, no hang.
    - 20 `rvd` restarts: all snapshots OK, no "still queued".
    - 0 oopses.
  - Tested on a T31 camera (sc2336 1080p, streamer running, module loaded in RAM): crop batch 3x, no hang (`fs1-crop-*` 960x540 into a 640x360 buffer refused), 0 oopses.
- T23 AF statistics on by default (`source_af=1`, as in stock: `tisp_init` always runs `tiziano_af_init` and registers the AF interrupt). Tested on the Jooan A6M (sc1a4t 720p15, streamer running, module loaded in RAM):
  - No measurable cost. CPU busy was 47.8 % with `source_af=0` and 48.1 % with `source_af=1` (3 x 60 s each, both within noise). The core IRQ rate was 88.7/s in both cases: the AF interrupt is a status bit of the core interrupt.
  - apitest GetAfHist (8x8), GetAFMetrices, GetAfWeight and GetAfZone: PASS, with live values.
  - 20 `rvd` restarts and an ISP day/night switch: every snapshot OK, 0 oopses.
- T23 MSCA scratch area (`msca_scratch`, default 1; docs in `driver/t23/docs/MSCA_SCRATCH.md`).
  - Before, a channel stopped with its output kept enabled and the input running made the MSCA write every further frame into the stream's last buffer, which user space frees after the close.
  - Now STREAMOFF queues a scratch address behind the stream's buffers. The scratch area is the tail of the ISP buffer in rmem (GET_BUF asks for one Y plane at sensor size, 900 KiB for 720p). The kernel heap has no contiguous block of that size: the largest free block is 256 KiB, even after compaction.
  - QBUFs of a parked channel are held back until STREAMON, as stock does.
  - The input waits until each parked channel's scratch address has been consumed before it stops. A first build without this wait hung the camera at the next session start (3 of about 35 cold starts).
  - Counters `msca_scratch_parks/frames/deferred/skips/settle_timeouts/settle_ticks_max`; host test `tests/tx_isp_t23_scratch_test.c`.
  - Tested on the Jooan A6M:
    - 20 cold starts and 20 `rvd` restarts: all snapshots OK.
    - An ISP day/night switch: OK.
    - 80 parks, 81 scratch completions, 0 settle or drain timeouts, longest settle 30 ms.
    - The apitest FrameSource disable/enable cycle: PASS.
    - No `unmatched MSCA completion` at STREAMOFF.
    - 0 oopses.

- Pending (branch `claude/t41-ch1-fix`): T41 MSCA root cause of the ch1 garbage. Output size, crop, scaler ratio, the global input size (0xf0084) and the flip word (0xf002c) are staged registers: a write takes effect only at the next input frame after `0xf0010 = 1`, a read returns the active value (measured on the Vanhua T55A, gc5603). Since no-op HVFLIP update requests are skipped, nothing latched a programmed output: every output ran with its reset geometry (ch0 1920x1080, ch1 1280x720, ratio 1:1, input 1920x1080). ch0 at 1920x1080 was a 1920x1080 crop of the 2880x1620 sensor; ch1 at 640x360 wrote 720 lines of 1280 pixels at stride 640 into 640x360 buffers (zoomed crop, magenta/green chroma, a ~165-line band of other data at the top of every following buffer). Not a hardware ratio limit: 4.5:1 (640x360) scales correctly once latched.
  - `t41_msca_cfg_update=1` requests the update after an output is (re)programmed. Device-tested: ch1 640x360, 960x540, 1280x720, 1440x810 correct and full field of view (raw frames idx 0/1 without band, streamer snapshots of ch0/ch1), flip set/restore correct, 20 streamer restarts, 0 oops. **Default 0**: with correct geometry the output-restart hang comes back (`t41crash/targeted.py` hung after 4-13 cycles with 1, also with ch1 at 960x540 and with `t41_msca_stop_disable=1`; 30/30 cycles with 0). The hang has to be solved before the default can change.
  - Flip restore: 0xf002c is merged and compared against the last staged word instead of the read-back (`driver/t41/tx_isp_t41_msca_shadow.h`), so a set followed by a restore within one frame no longer leaves the picture flipped while GetHVFLIP reports 0. Host test `tests/tx_isp_t41_msca_shadow_test.c`.
  - Open, seen twice during these tests: after an rmmod/insmod of the ISP and sensor modules AddSensor failed with `i2c-0: Failed to register i2c client gc5603 at 0x31 (-16)` (the old client survives the reload) until the next reload. A reclaim of the stale client was tried but its path was never hit on the device, so it is not included.
  - Open, seen three times: `rmmod tx_isp_t41` oopses in `free_module` (unaligned access in `sysfs_remove_group`/`__free_pages`, page or attribute data corrupted) after runs with the default `t41_msca_cfg_update=0` (ftest, 20 streamer restarts); not seen in about 8 reloads after runs with 1. Fits memory being overwritten while outputs run with the unlatched reset geometry, not proven. Note: `dmesg | grep -c "Oops\["` does not count these (`Unhandled kernel unaligned access[#1]`).
  - The ch1 1280x720/1440x810 watchdog reboot reported for imgfx is a tool effect, not the driver: imgfx stores every picture as full-size NV12 in the camera's RAM `/tmp`; at 1280x720 the awb group (12 pictures, 1.35 MB each) fills the RAM and the camera stalls until the watchdog resets it (reproduced, MemAvailable 15 to 4 MB in 34 s; 640x360 passes).

- Release candidate (branch `claude/release-t23-driver`). The T23 defaults are now the stock-like set that ran 7 h overnight plus many restarts: `chan_stop_keep_input=1`, `msca_keep_enabled=2`, `msca_fifo_rearm=0`, `msca_flip_skip_noop=1`, `msca_restart_skip=1`, `msca_session_release=1`, `crumbs=0`. The hang needed `chan_stop_keep_input=0` together with `msca_fifo_rearm=1`.
  - The cold-start snapshot 503 is now prevented as stock does it, without the FIFO rearm: STREAMOFF waits up to 21 x 10 ms until the channel's buffers have left the hardware, and QBUF invalidates the buffer's cache lines (`chan_stop_drain`, `qbuf_cache_inv`, docs in `driver/t23/docs/STREAMOFF_DRAIN_WAIT.md`).
  - Tested on the Jooan A6M (sc1a4t, streamer running, module loaded in RAM, openimp `claude/release-t23-vbm`):
    - 20 cold starts (20 s idle): every snapshot OK (ch0 1.8-2.1 s, ch1 2.8-3.2 s after start).
    - 20 `rvd` restarts: every snapshot OK.
    - 44 drains (at most 70 ms), 0 drain timeouts.
    - An ISP day/night switch: OK.
    - 0 oopses.
- T21: `SetColorfxMode(SEPIA)` works (it returned -1 before): the CCM saturation list goes to 0 like B/W and the CSC U/V row sums get a tint (U below, V above neutral for a grey input, luma unchanged). A CSC attribute set during sepia keeps the tint; leaving sepia puts the stored matrix back. Device-tested on PC420 (jxf23): dU 17 / dV 19 against the plain picture.
- Pending (branch `claude/release-t31-framedrop`): T31 `IMP_ISP_SetFrameDrop`/`GetFrameDrop` (ioctl 0xc00456e6/0xc00456e7, three 12-byte records) with the stock semantics: lsize (window of lsize+1 frames, 0..31, larger is refused) at ISP register +0x130 and fmark (bit set = output) at +0x134 of ((ch+0x98)<<8); disabled writes lsize 0 / fmark 1; get reports the registers and enable = 1 like the stock module. The ISP drops the frames itself (device-checked); the old driver helper had the two registers swapped and the DQBUF software drop dropped a second time, so it is removed. Host test `tests/tx_isp_t31_framedrop_test.c`.
- T21 `IMP_ISP_Tuning_SetBrightness` acts (beyond vendor, branch `claude/release-t21-brightness`; the vendor T21 kernel only stores the value and the OEM AE never reads it): the AE luma target is scaled by value/128 (clamp 1..255, floor 16/128), a converged AE re-derives its cached target at once. 128 is the vendor path unchanged. Device-tested on the PC420 (jxf23): brightness 30/128/225 gives mean Y 28.8/117.7/193.0 (imgfx), the streamer's brightness setting live 44.5/130.8/223.9 (snapshot), 0 oops. Host test `tests/t21_ae_comp_host_test.c`.

## 2026-10-05

- Pending (branch `claude/t23-chan-restart-hang`, built, **device test pending**): T23 hard hang after streamer start/restart on cam-B (on-demand channel 0 wakes). Default now = the device-tested d28a0177 set (`msca_flip_skip_noop=1`: no update request for unchanged flip bits; `msca_keep_enabled=1`: the last STREAMOFF keeps the MSCA output enabled; `msca_restart_skip=1`: an unchanged restart is not reloaded, its FIFO is rearmed with the input stopped) plus `msca_session_release=1`: tx-isp STREAMON with the input stopped switches off outputs left enabled and clears their FIFOs, so a restarted streamer never starts the input under the old process's freed buffers (2227 hang). a325b523/4ea284c9 (release on close, frame-done ISR application, deferred starts) hung earlier on the device and were dropped. `crumbs` off by default (rmem crumbs do not survive a reboot, U-Boot zeroes rmem; a `mem=` hole does). Evidence, stock comparison and test in `driver/t23/README.md` "Output/channel restart hang"; same class as the T41 output restart hang.
- Pending (branch `claude/streamer-gaps-t1x-t23-t31`, host- and build-tested only, no device test yet): T23 SetAutoZoom (control 0x80000e8, 36-byte IMPISPAutoZoom) as the stock `apical_isp_autozoom_s_attr` / `tisp_s_autozoom_control`: crop window and scaler output go into the channel's MSCA record (crop/scaler lock bytes, +0x10..+0x24), a running channel is reloaded like the front crop (same lock and channel load; start/stop paths untouched). Beyond stock: windows outside the sensor picture, below 64x64 or odd, and scaler outputs above the window are refused with -EINVAL instead of stalling the MSCA. The stock T23 module has no handler for 0x80000a5 (GetBlcAttr) and 0x80000e9 (SetScalerLv) either (the firmware's `tisp_get_blc_attr` is an empty stub and `tisp_s_scaler_level_control` is never called), so those two stay -EINVAL like the stock driver; the T23 AWB cluster/trend runtime and SetMaskBlock of `claude/t23-awb-runtime` are merged in this branch.
- Pending (branch `claude/t23-af`, host- and emulator-tested only, no device test yet): T23 AF statistics chain from the stock module, off by default at the time (`source_af=1`; on by default since 2026-10-06): AF block + core interrupt bit 31, focus values, GetAfHist/SetAfHist, Get/SetAfWeight, GetAFMetrices, GetAfZone (controls 0x8000042/43/44/46). Shares the data handling of the T31 chain; verified identical to the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/af_emu.py`).
- Pending (branch `claude/t23-cids`, host- and emulator-tested only, no device test yet): T23 SetAwbClust/GetAwbClust (0x0800000e, 40-byte IMPISPAWBCluster) and SetAwbCtTrend/GetAwbCtTrend (0x0800000f, 24-byte IMPISPAWBCtTrend) on the stock objects (_awb_cluster/_awb_trend, api_para/api_status) with the stock word order; identical to the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/awb_api_emu.py`). SetMask/GetMask (0x080000e5) are not handled by the stock module either (it returns -1 for both), so they stay unrouted.
- Pending (branch `claude/t23-awb-runtime`, on top of `claude/t23-cids`, host- and emulator-tested only, no device test yet): the open T23 AWB reads the cluster and trend objects like the stock module. ClusterEn == 1 runs the stock cluster stage between the light-source weights and the weighted mean (14x14 rg/bg histogram, 12 peaks, 5 seeds per peak, mean shift, merge, zone weights by cluster size; the stock `.bss` state is kept as stock does, including its stale-count behaviour); the histogram is filled on every run. The colour temperature trend is added to the R/B gains like `JZ_Isp_Awb_Awbg2reg` (>= 5000 K / mid / <= 3000 K offsets, 32-bit wrap, limit 0x3fff, at the colour temperature before SetAwbCt). ToleranceEn/tolerance_th hold the automatic gains until the summed Q8 change exceeds the threshold (stock then ramps, the open AWB steps; without ToleranceEn the update rule is unchanged). Cluster and trend objects are reloaded from the IQ bank at stream start and day/night like `tiziano_awb_params_refresh` (a Set before that is dropped, as in stock). The lifted `tisp_awb_set_cluster_awb_params` had the cluster words in the user order (stock order differs) and `tisp_awb_set_ct_trend` an unbounded copy loop; both fixed (not reachable from the open control path). Cluster stage and trend identical to the stock `tx-isp-t23.ko` in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/awb_cluster_emu.py`: state and weights of random multi-frame scenes, trend over random objects, gains of the built module).
- Pending (same branch): T23 SetMaskBlock/GetMaskBlock (0x08000183, 20-byte IMPISPMaskBlockAttr) on the stock mscaler mask table (`tisp_s/g_mscaler_mask_block_attr`: 16-byte entry per block at mscaler + 1836, dirty bit mask at + 2308), identical to the stock module in the MIPS emulator (`driver/t23/audit/mask_block_emu.py`, whole mscaler object after Set, Get block, copy sizes). Beyond stock: chx/pinum outside 0..2/0..3 are refused (stock writes past the table), Get reads chx/pinum from the user block (stock uses an uninitialised stack local) and returns 0 for the bytes it does not define. Stock quirk kept: Get never reports mask_en. The stored masks have no image effect yet: the mscaler update that consumes them (`tisp_msca_Shd_ctrl`) is not run by this driver.
- Pending (branch `claude/t20-fw-optimize`, host-tested only, device test open): the recovered T20/T10 firmware unit builds at `-Os` like the rest of the module (T20 module text 399 to 245 KB, `.ko` 589 to 409 KB; T10 the same). Spots that only worked at `-O0` fixed against the OEM disassembly (dropped call arguments, a partition-LUT walk past its object, command-interface state on the stack, a stack buffer one word short, an inline-asm tail jump, a missing return). New host harness `tests/t20_fw` runs the firmware at `-O0`/`-Os`/`-O2` through init, 239 frames, API sweeps and day/night tuning switches and requires identical register traces and state. `TX_ISP_FW_O0=1` restores the `-O0` build for A/B tests.

## 2026-10-04

- thingino: `open-tx-isp` is part of upstream `aperto` ([#1756](https://github.com/themactep/thingino-firmware/pull/1756)), pinned to this fork's `next`; the kernel VPU/rmem patches (#1748, #1752) and the optional boot guard (#1749) are merged there. All test cameras run `aperto` images (30/30 snapshots, 0 oops, 0 VPU errors).
- Pending (on branches, not yet in `next`, soak first): T23 root cause of the snapshot 503 on a second channel (stale MSCA address FIFOs after a stream stop, the vendor clears them on STREAMOFF; fix clears and re-arms them at STREAMON, 260 cold-start cycles without a failure) and the T23 log switch (`t23_runtime_trace`, informational output off by default, boot log 522 to 78 lines, errors stay visible).
- T41: AE compensation, gain/exposure caps and 2D/3D noise reduction reach the ISP; unimplemented tuning IDs are refused.
- T20/T10: the unused 8 MiB V4L2-MMAP frame pool is off by default (`isp_mmap_pool_kb=0`, parameter kept): about +8 MB free RAM (T20 MemFree 46.7 to 55 MB, T10 1.9 to ~9.7 MB); only the recovered firmware unit stays `-O0` (-140 KB).
- Shortfall logging: when reserved memory is too small the driver logs once with have/need/missing and a concrete value (`isp_mmap_pool_kb`, `ispmem`).
- Hardening: T31 proc writes bounded, VIC stop busy-wait bounded (~10 ms), AE handle wait with 2 s timeout; T20/T10 IRQ registered before its data was published (oops on early interrupt) fixed.
- T20 AE after a streamer restart: sensor exposure cache invalidated on every sensor sync and AE state reset like on a fresh load (5/5 restarts converge).
- Sensor module pin fixed on T21/T31/T41 (the sensor driver was registered with the ISP module as owner): `rmmod` of a sensor during streaming is refused, no more use-after-free.
- T23: white-balance jump to ~10000 K with the vendor AE fixed (black level rewritten after stream start).
- T41: unload, flip, BCSH and isp-m0 fixes merged into `next`; module 80 KB smaller; three clean reloads.
- `next` branch created; it tracks the tested aggregate (fast-forward only).

## 2026-10-03

- Module size: T23 1,047 to 622 KB (vendor 857), T31 859 to 711 KB (vendor 829), T21 760 to 452 KB (vendor 616), T20 775 to 736 KB, T10 770 to 731 KB.
- T41: module reload root cause fixed (a decompiled tuning-node helper overwrote `.bss`, 10/10 reload cycles clean); picture controls, `isp-m0` in vendor layout, flip registers, sensor re-registration.
- T23: dynamic ADR and defog lifted from the vendor module (44/44 emulator cases identical); lifted vendor AE is the default; review fixes for unload use-after-free, defog allocations, front-crop overflow, ADR/AE locking; front crop on the vendor path.
- T21: `ae_it_max_us` acts (beyond vendor, kept by decision); AWB hysteresis at dusk (39 switches to 0 in 40 frames); colour-temperature updates only above 50 K change; lifted AWB 1.41x to 0.95x of vendor instructions.
- T10/T20: `ae_it_max_us` limits the AE; Sinter/Temper strength acts; CCM/LSC readable in `isp-m0`; T10 reload safe (5 cycles, build for the wrong kernel tree refused); T10 duplicate `isp_printf` export fixed.
- T10: sub-stream and diagonal drift fixed in the lifted firmware shared with T20.
- Hardening: QBUF buffers must lie in the rmem window (`qbuf_guard=0` disables); sensor module pinned while the ISP is open; AVPU review fixes (minor-number leak, use-after-free on unbind, flush range).
- T31: privacy mask implemented like the vendor (400/400 random sequences register-identical); scene mode and colour effects on T23/T31; tuning stubs replaced (black level, colour matrix presets, front crop, scaler level).
- T30 readiness without hardware: builds for a real T30 kernel, missing `isp_printf` export fixed.
- Kernel patches 0099/0100/0101 for the soc_vpu driver and rmem flush ioctl (sleeping wait, immediate error interrupt, validated flush range, statistics registers readable for the vendor T20 rate controller).

## 2026-10-02

- Robustness: T23 10 stop/start and reload cycles without an oops, two out-of-bounds writes fixed, `.bss` 434 to 180 KB; T20 `rmmod` while streaming refused, 53 user copies checked; T31 vmalloc leak (252 KB per cycle) fixed; T21 reload oops root cause (statistics DMA into freed buffers) fixed.
- Independent review: deadlock between sensor unload and `/proc/jz/sensor/*`, orphan sensor slots, T21 open/release counting.
- T21: white balance, CT detection and CT-driven CCM/LSC lifted from the vendor module (10/10 scenes register-identical); event callbacks with IRQs off like the vendor.
- T23: gain index for gain-driven blocks was linear instead of log2; sharpen parameters loaded from the active IQ bank; exposure kept across stream restarts; failed stream start and reload fixed (statistics DMA freed while active); vendor AE chain behind `source_ae_oem`.
- T20: vertical flip chroma overrun (pink band) and sharpness fixed; vendor AWB chain behind `t20_simple_awb=0` after fixing decompilation errors.
- T10 integrated: the NVPU wrote 21 KB/10 KB past each reference plane, fixed; day/night panic from a zeroed FSM manager pointer fixed.
